A Complete introduction to Panini Sutras for the use of beginners.

Laghu Siddhanta Kaumudi is a text in which Panini Sutras are so rearranged as to bring together the relevant sutras bearing on a particular topic. The present book is an English translation of Laghu Siddhanta Kaumudi together with comments, references and index. The book is a valuable contribution to the study of Sanskrit Grammar.

“The Translation of Laghu Siddhanta Kaumudi by late Dr. Ballantyne has enabled even beginners to find their way through the labyrinth of Sanskrit grammar” – Max Muller.

# Laghu-siddhānta-kaumudī. Compendium of Maṇḍala-pat

## atha saṁjñā-prakaraṇam

## Rules

- **(1) a i uṇ**
- **(2) ṛ ḷk**
- **(3) e oṅ**
- **(4) ai auc**
- **(5) ha ya va raṭ**
- **(6) laṇ**
- **(7) ña ma ṅa ṇa nam**
- **(8) jha bhañ**
- **(9) gha ḍha dhaṣ**
- **(10) ja ba ga ḍa daś**
- **(11) kha pha cha ṭha tha ca ṭa tav**
- **(12) ka pay**
- **(13) śa ṣa sar**
- **(14) hal**

**(i) iti maheśvarāṇi sūtrāṇyaṇādisājñā’rthani**(iti maheśvarāṇi sūtrāṇi aṇ ādi sāṁjñā arthani) \|

**(ii) eṣāmantyā itaḥ**

**eṣām**—of these aphorisms (fourteen groups of letter); **antyāḥ**— the last (final or ending) letters; **itaḥ**— are to be termed as ***it*** (disappearance or abolition) \| (iti apagacchatīti it) \|

The final letters of these aphorisms are to be termed as ***it*** \|

**(iii) hakārādiṣv a-kāraḥ uccāranārthaḥ** (hakāra ādiṣu a-kāraḥ uccāranārthaḥ) \|

**ha-kāra**— beginning from the ***h*** of ***hayavaraṭ*** and ending in the ***h*** of ***hal*** (all the consonants); **akāraḥ (hrasva-akāraḥ)**—association of the short – ***a***; **uccāraṇārthaḥ**—is meant for easy articulation (pronunciation or utterance) \|

Beginning from the ***h*** of the aphorism ***hayavaraṭ*** and ending in the ***h*** of the aphorism – ***hal***, the short ***a*** is associated with the consonants for easy articulation \|

Repeated description of the ***h*** in the aphorisms ***hayavaraṭ*** and ***hal*** is made owing to its necessity in ***aś***, ***aṭ***, ***iṇ***, ***haś***, ***bal*** and ***śal*** ***pratyāhāra*** \|

**(iv) laṇ-madhye tv-it-saṁjñakaḥ** (laṇ madhye tu it saṁjñakaḥ) \|

**laṇ-madhye**— in between the ***l a ṇ*** (the intervening – ***a***); **tu-it-saṁjñakaḥ**— is to be termed as ***it*** \|

In the rule ***laṇ***, the short ***a*** that comes after the ***la*** and precedes to the ***ṇ*** , is declared to be ***it*** \|

Here, the ***a*** does not mean for easy articulation of the consonant ***l*** as like as in ***hakārādiṣvakāraḥ uccāranārthaḥ***. A distinct rule like ***laṇmadhye tvitsaṁjñakaḥ*** has been framed to declare the short ***a*** as ***it*** so as to establish the ***ra-pratyāhāra***, by conjoining the ***a*** as ***it*** with the ***ra*** of ***hayavaraṭ*** (***ra-a***), where both the ***r*** and the ***l*** are ascertained \| (see the precept – 29 ***uraṇ raparaḥ***) \|

## 1 – hal antyam \| 1.3.3

**PP: upadeśe 'ntyaṁ hal it (syāt)** \| upadeśe antyaṁ hal it (syāt)

*upadeśe 'ntyaṁ hal it syāt \| upadeśa ādyoccāraṇam \|*  
*sūtreṣvadṛṣṭaṁ padaṁ sūtrāntarādanuvartanīyaṁ sarvatra \|\|*

**halantyam**—the final consonant; **upadeśe**—in the specification; **antya**—the final; **hal**(**vyañjana-varṇa**)—the consonant; **it-syāt**—is to be omitted.

In the event of an ***upadeśa*** or specification, the final consonant is to be termed as ***it*** \|

another translation (RNS):

A final *hal* of an item in *upadeśa* is termed an *it* \|

Definition of ***upadeśa*** (taken from HNV) \|

*dhātu-sūtra-gaṇoṇādi-*  
*vākya-liṅgānuśāsanaṁ*  
*āgama-pratyayādeśā*  
*upadeśāḥ prakīrtitāḥ*

“The dhātus, sūtras, gaṇas (“groups of words that follow the same rule”), uṇādi-sūtras, vākyas (kātyāyana’s vārttikas), and the liṅgānuśāsana (“a work dealing with the rules of gender attributed to pāṇini”), as well as the āgamas, pratyayas, and ādeśas are called upadeśas” \|

The word ***upadeśa*** (***ādyoccāraṇa***) signifies to the following occasions as adopted by the tried of sages – *Pāṇini*, *Kātyāyana* & *Patañjali* and such practice has been continuing on the basis of regular succession. Such as ***pratyayāḥ śiva-sūtrāṇi ādeśā āgamās tathā \| gaṇa-pāṭho upadeśāḥ prakīrtitā*** \|\|

The ***adeśa*** is an enunciation which signifies the technical treaties in Sanskrit Grammar i.e.

(a) – ***pratyaya*** (an affix/suffix),

(b) – ***śiva-sūtra***,

(c) – ***ādeśa*** (a substitute),

(d) – ***āgama*** (an augment),

(e) – ***dhātu-paṭha*** (a verbal root) and

(f) – ***gaṇa-paṭha*** (a series of words or roots belonging to the same rule and called after the first word of that series \|

When the original order of the sutras is abandoned, as in the present work, it becomes necessary to place before the student, in the shape of a commentary, the words which Pāṇini left him to gather from the context. Thus, to complete the sūtra before us (viz. *hal antyam*), the words *upadeśe* and *it* are required; and these are supplied from a previous sūtra \|\|

## 2 – adarśanaṁ lopaḥ \| 1.1.60

**PP: sthānasya adarśanam lopaḥ**

prasaktasyādarśanaṁ lopa-saṁjñaṁ syāt \|

**prasaktasya (vidyamānasya)**— of the apparent (existing or present in articulation); **ādarśanaṁ**— disappearance; **lopa-saṁjñaṁ syāt**— to be termed as *elision* \|

A letter which is apparently present in articulation and subsequently disappears, that is to be termed as ***lopa*** or elision with substitution of a blank \|

another translation (RNS):

Non-appearence (*adarśana*) is termed *lopa* \|

The earnest meaning *adarśanaṁ lopaḥ* is that, the word which was previously present in articulation and subsequently disappears by rules is called as *lopa* or non-existent for articulation \|

Let this be the term for the disappearance of anything previously apparent \|

**Eluc.**: The word *sthāne* in the locative case has been carried over from the sutra *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ* (17, 1.1.50) . This has been changed into *sthānasya* by the process of *vibhakti-vipariṇāma* which consists in supposing for the purpose of interpretation a different case-ending in the place of that which is actually stated (cf. *arthavaśād vibhakti-vipariṇāmaḥ*, Abhyankar, Paribhāṣā-saṅgraha, p, 28, p. 268, etc.) \|

It should be borne in mind here that lopa is the designation of the meaning of the word *adarśana* (non-perception) and not of the word *adarśana* \|

## 3 – tasya lopaḥ \| 1.3.9

**PP: tasya itaḥ lopaḥ** \|

tasyeto lopaḥ syāt \| ṇādayo 'ṇādyarthāḥ \|

**tasya itaḥ**—of that *it*; **lopaḥ syāt**— to be termed as *lopa* or elision \| **ṇa-ādayaḥ**—the existing final *it* letters – *ṇ*, *k*, etc. of *caturdaśa-śiva-sūtra*; **aṇ-ādi-arthāḥ**— meant for constituting the aphorisms like *aṇ* (*aiuṇ*) \|

The letter which is termed as *it*, let there be an elision or *lopa* by deleting the *it* \|

another translation (RNS):

There is disappearance of that which is termed *it* \|

In the room of that which is called *it*, let there be a blank. Thus all the final consonants in the *Śiva-sūtras* are left out of view, when these are employed to designate any class of letters; the use of the *ṇ* and the rest of the fourteen being to assist in forming the brief names of these clases \|

**Eluc.**: The pronoun *tasya* in the present sūtra refers to the word *it* which has been carried over from the sūtra *upadeśe 'janunāsika it* Pā. 1.3.2. \| Although the carried over *it* is in the nominative case, it is transformed into the genitive case so that it may syntactically agree with the pronoun *tasya* \| By the present sūtra, the elision of all *its* in the *pratyāhāra-sūtras* is applicable \|\|

## 4 – ādir antyena sahetā \| 1.1.71

(ādiḥ antyena saha itā)

**PP: ādiḥ antyena itā saha svasya** \|

Paraphrased: *antyena itā saha ādiḥ* \|

*antyenetā sahita ādirmadhyagānāṁ svasya ca saṁjñā syāt \| yathā – aṇiti a i u varṇānāṁ saṁjñā \| evaṁ-ak-ac-hal-al ityādayaḥ \|\|*

**antyena itā sahita**— with the final *it* of *māheśvara sūtra*; **ādiḥ madhyagānāṁ**— of the initial (*ādi*) and the intervening (*madhyaga*) letters; **svasya ca saṁjñā syāt**—to be the name (definition) of the initial itself and the intervening letters \|

An initial letter conjoining with a final *it* of the *Māheśvara sūtra*, let be the name or definition of the initial itself and the intervening letters \|

another translation (RNS):

An initial item joined with a final *it* denoted not only itself but also all intervening items \|

This is a rule framing precept for a *pratyāhāra* (*pratyāhāra-vidhāyaka-sūrtam*) \| The word *pratyāhāra* (*pratyāhriyante saṅkṣipyante varṇāḥ*) means a comprehension of several letters into one syllable. In other words, to frame pratyāhāra –

**(i)** there must be an *it* at the end,

**(ii)** beginning or initial letter may be any one of the prior letters excluding the letter that termed as *it*

**(iii)** intervening letters are to be present in between the initial and final *it* and

**(iv)** intervening *it* letters are not to be taken into account. For example – the intervening *it* letters – *ṇ*, *k*, *ṅ* in *ac pratyāhāra* are to be deleted and the vowels are taken into account (*pratyāhāreṣvitāṁ na grahaṇam*) \|

*aṇ-iti a i u varṇānāṁ saṁjñā* :- *aṇ* (*aiuṇ*) is a pratyāhāra which denotes the vowels – *a*, *i* and *u*, by deleting the final *ṇ* as it is termed as *it*. Here the *a* is the initial (*ādi*) letter, the *i* and the *u* are the intervening (*madhyaga*) letters in between the initial – *a* and the final – *ṇ* of *aṇ pratyāhara* (*aiuṇ*) \|

**Eluc.**: This is a *saṁjña-sūtra*. It tells us the method of how to form a *pratyāhāra*. The word *svam* is lent into this sūtra from *svaṁ rūpaṁ śabdasyāśabda-saṁjñā* Pā. 1.1.68. The word *svam* changes into the genitive and refers to the principal. The reason is that the word *svam*, being a pronoun, will refer to *it*, and not to the secondary. In the present sūtra the final *it* is in the instrumental case and therefore secondary according to *sahayukte 'pradhāne* Pā. 2.3.19. \|\|

## 5 – ūkālo 'jjhrasva-dīrgha-plutaḥ \| 1.2.27

(ū-kālaḥ ac hrasva dīrgha plutaḥ)

**PP: ū-kālaḥ ac hrasva-dīrgha-plutaḥ** \|

*kāla*=*mātra* (duration)

Basing on the prosodial length of the *ū*, the vowels, whose time of articulation is equal to that of one unit, two units & three units, are to be designated as short (*hrasva*), long (*dīrgha*) & prolated (*pluta*) respectively \|

another translation:

Let a vowel whose time (or prosodial length) is that of short *u*, long *ū*, and prolated ū3, be called accordingly short, long, and prolated \|

*uś ca ūś ca ū3ś ca vaḥ \| vāṁ kālaḥ iva kālo yasya so 'c kramād hrasva-dīrgha-pluta-saṁjñaḥ syāt \| sa pratyekam udāttādi bhedena tridhā \|*

**sa pratyekam**— these short, long & prolated vowels; **udātta**—(acutely accented); **ādi**—*anudātta* (gravely accented) and *svarita* (circumflexed accentuation) \|

Again, these short, long & prolated vowels are severally threefold according to the accentuation viz. acute (*udātta*), grave (*anudātta*) & circumflexed (svarita) \|

**Eluc.**: The nominative plural of *u*, *ū* and *u3* is *vaḥ* and the genitive plural is *vām* as shown in the *vṛtti.*

There are two significant points which deserve mention in connection with this sūtra.

- 1\) Why has *u-kāra* been singled out by Pāṇini to describe the different types of vowels as *hrasva*, *dīrgha* and *pluta*? \| Pāṇini has used *u-kālaḥ* in the sūtra since the pronunciation of this vowel resembles the crowing of a cock which could be easily understood in those days. Vāsudeva Dīkṣita seems to endorse this view when he says that the short, long and pluta ukara resembles the ascending note of a cock. Pāṇini must have had this in his mind. Otherwise he could have used *ā-kālaḥ* instead of *ū-kālaḥ.*
- 2\) Why has masculine been used in the word *hrasva-dīrgha-plutaḥ*, occurring in the sūtra, which, being a *samāhāra-dvandva* compound, should have been used in the neuter gender? Jñānendra Sarasvatī says that the use in the masculine is peculiar to the sūtra only. Had it been used in the spoken language (*bhāṣā*), it would have normally been in the neuter gender.

## 6 – uccair udāttaḥ \| 1.2.29

(uccaiḥ udāttaḥ)

A vowel uttered in the upper half of the articulating organ viz. palate or lips &c. is called as acutely accented (*udātta*) \|

another translation (RNS):

That (a vowel) which is pronounced higher (with high pitch) at its place of articulation is termed *udātta*, high-pitched \|

**Eluc.**:Vāsudeva Dīkṣita says that the word *uccaiḥ* does not mean loud pronunciation of the vowels. Otherwise the definition of *udātta* would not cover those vowels which are pronounced in a very low voice just as in a prayer uttered in a low voice (BM, I, p. 13) \|

## 7 – nīcair anudāttaḥ \| 1.2.30

(nīcaiḥ anudāttaḥ)

A vowel uttered in the lower half of the articulating organ viz. palate or lips etc. is called as gravely accented (*anudātta*) \|

another translation (RNS):

That (a vowel) which is pronounced lower (with low pitch) at its place of articulation is termed *anuudātta*, low-pitched \|

## 8 – samāhāraḥ svaritaḥ \| 1.2.31

**PP: udāttasya anudāttasya samāhāraḥ svaritaḥ** \|

With the combined characteristics of both the acute and grave accentuations (in the middle part of the articulating organ i.e. palate or lips etc.) a vowel is termed as circumflexed accentuation (*svarita*) \|

another translation (RNS):

A vowel which is pronounced with a combination of features of *udātta* and *anudātta* is termed *svarita* \|

*sa nava-vidho 'pi pratyekam anunāsikatvānanunāsikatvābhyāṁ dvidhā \|\|*

(sa nava-vidhaḥ api pratyekaṁ anunāsikatva ananunāsikatvābhyāṁ dvidhā)

**sa nava-vidho 'pi**—the vowel being of nine fold viz. *hrasva, dīrgha, pluta* X *udātta, anudātta, svarita* = *hrasva-udātta, dīrgha-udātta, pluta-udātta, hrasva-anudātta, dīrgha-anudātta, pluta-anudātta, hrasva-svarita, dīrgha-svarita, pluta-svarita*; **pratyekaṁ anunāsikatva ananunāsikatvābhyāṁ dvidhā**—these ninefold vowels are further sub-divided into two nasal (*anunāsika*) and non-nasal (*ananunāsika*) \|

Accordingly, the vowels are classified into eighteen varieties as: 3 *hrasva-dīrgha-pluta* X *udātta-anudātta-svarita* X 2 *anunāsika-ananunāsika* \|

Note that this rule assigns the name *svarita* to a vowel and not to the combination (*samāhāra*) of vowels \|

The Mahābhaṣya (II: 28) illustrates this point as follows: for example, the quality (known as) whitenees is termed *śukla*, the quality (known as) blacknees is termed *kṛṣṇa*; the one which now combines both these qualities receives the third name *kalmāṣa* or *sāraṅga* "variegated; part-white, part-black". Similarly, here also: (a vowel) which is pronounced with the quality of *udātta* is termed *udātta*; one which is pronounced with the quality of *anudātta* is termed *anudātta*; and one which now combines both these qualities receives the third name *svarita* "high-low" \|

**Eluc.**: The words *udāttaḥ* and *anudāttaḥ* are carried over from the preceding two sutras and have been changed into the genitive singular by the process of *vibhakti-vipariṇāma.* A *svarita* vowel has high-pitched segment at its beginning for the length equivalent to that of half a short vowel. That is, the first half-mora of a vowel is high-pitched, its remainder low-pitched" (Cardona 1997: pp. 17-18) \| A vowel with the property of high pitch carries no accent mark. A vowel with a low pitch (*anudātta*) carries a horizontal bar beneath it whereas that with a combination of both the pitches (*svarita*) carries a vertical bar above it \|\|

## 9 – mukha-nāsikā-vacano 'nunāsikaḥ \| 1.1.8

**PP: mukha-nāsikā-vacanaḥ anunāsikaḥ** \|

Let that which is pronounced by the nose along with the mouth be called nasal \|

another translation (RNS):

A sound pronounced at once in the nose and the mouth is termed *anunāsika* "nasal" \|

*mukhasahitanāsikayoccāryamāṇo varṇo 'nunāsika-saṁjñaḥ syāt \| tadittham – a i u ṛ eṣāṁ varṇānāṁ pratyekam aṣṭādaśa bhedāḥ \| ḷ-varṇasya dvādaśa, tasya dīrghābhāvāt \| ecāmapi dvādaśa, teṣāṁ hrasvābhāvāt \|\|*

**mukha-sahita-nāsikayoccāryamāṇo varṇo 'nunāsika-saṁjñaḥ syāt**—a letter articulated in the mouth with the association of nose is to be termed as nasal (*ananunāsika*) \| **tadittham – a i u ṛ eṣāṁ varṇānāṁ pratyekam aṣṭādaśa bhedāḥ**—it is in this manner the *a, i, u, ṛ* are of eighteen varieties as these letters possess 3 types of prosodial lengths X 3 types of accentuations X 2 types of nasalities; **ḷ-varṇasya dvādaśa, tasya dīrghābhāvāt**—the *ḷ* is of twelve modifications due to lack of its long (*dīrgha*) form; **ecām api dvādaśa, teṣāṁ hrasvābhāvāt**— the diphthongs viz. – *e, o, ai, o* are of twelve alterations as these do not possess their short (*hrasva*) forms \|\|

## 10 – tulyāsya-prayatnaṁ savarṇam \| 1.1.9

(tulya-āsya-prayatnaṁ savarṇam)

*tālvādi sthānam ābhyantara-prayatnaś cety etad-dvayaṁ yasya yena tulyaṁ tanmithaḥ savarṇa-saṁjñaṁ syāt \| (ṛ-ḷ-varṇayor mithaḥ sāvarṇyaṁ vācyam (vārtikam)) \| a-ku-ha-visarjanīyānāṁ kaṇṭhaḥ \| i-cu-ya-śānāṁ tālu \| ṛ-ṭu-ra-ṣāṇāṁ mūrdhā \| ḷ-tu-la-sānāṁ dantāḥ \| u-pū-padhmānīyānām oṣṭhau \| ña-ma-ṅa-ṇa-nanāṁ nāsikā ca \| edaitoḥ kaṇṭha tālu \| odautoḥ kaṇṭhoṣṭam \| va-kārasya dantoṣṭham \| jihvāmūlīyasya jihvāmūlam \| nāsikā-anusvārasya \|*

yatno dvidhā – ābhyantaro, bāhyaśca \| ādyaḥ pañcadhā \| spṛṣṭeṣatspṛṣṭeṣadvivṛtavivṛta-saṁvṛta-bhedāt \| tatra spṛṣṭa-prayatanaṁ sparśānām \| īṣat-spṛṣṭam antaḥ sthānām \| īṣadvivṛtam uṣmaṇām \| vivṛtaṁ svarāṇām \| hrasvasyā 'varṇasya prayoge saṁvṛtam \| prakriyādaśāyāṁ tu vivṛtam eva \| bāhya-prayatnas tvekādaśadhā \| vivāraḥ – saṁvāraḥ – śvāso – nādo – ghoṣo – 'ghoṣo – 'lpa-prāṇo – mahāprāṇo – udātto – 'nudāttaḥ – svaritaś ceti \|\|

āsya (asyanti – uccārayanti varṇānaneneti – āsyam)= **tālvādi sthānam**— the place of origin for the articulation of a letter such as palate, throat etc.; **ābhyantara-prayatnaḥ ca-iti**—and also the internal effort; **etad-dvayam**—these two (the place of origin and the internal effort); **yasya yena tulyam**—which one is alike with another (the same place of origin); **tanmithaḥ**—that mutually or reciprocally; **savarṇa-saṁjñaṁ syāt**—to be termed as Homogeneous ( same class or same family) \|

Those letters are called reciprocally Homogeneous when their place of origin and internal effort are almost the same \|

another translation (RNS):

A sound which has the same articulation effort at the same plase of articulation (*sthāna*) in the oral cavity as another sound is called *savarṇa* "homogeneous" with taht sound \|

(vārtika ṛ-ḷ-varṇayor mithaḥ sāvarṇyaṁ vācyam)

According to *Kātyāyana*, the letters – *ṛ* & *ḷ* are termed mutually homogeneous although they are of separate origins \|

**Examples of the place of origin of letters**

\[maybe in future I can type these examples\]

**RNS**: Theword *tulyāsya-prayatnam* is *bahuvrīhi* compound meaning "that which shares the manner of articulation at the same place in the mouth". In the interpretation, *āsya* modifies *prayatna*, thereby giving the meaning "similar effort at a place" \|\|

**KR**: The word *āsya* formed with the taddhita affix *yat* means occurring in the mouth (oral cavity). The word *tulyāsya-prayatnam* is a *bahuvrīhi* compound which qualifies the word *savarṇam* \|\|

**LSKV1**: The effort (that we make in order to articulate phonemes) is of two kinds: 1. Internal and 2. External. The first has five kinds:

- 1\. *spṛṣṭa*
- 2\. *īṣat-spṛṣṭa*
- 3\. *īṣad-vivṛta*
- 4\. *vivṛta*
- 5\. *saṁvṛta.*

The effort called *spṛṣṭa* (contact) belongs to the stops (*sparśa*). The effort called *īṣat-spṛṣṭa* (in which the contact is partial) belongs to semi-vowels (antaḥstha). The effort called *īṣad-vivṛta* belongs to *ś, ṣ, s* and *h* (*ūṣma*). The internal effort known as *vivṛta* belongs to the vowels. The last effort, called *saṁvṛta*, takes place when *a* is used in spoken language, but during grammatical operation it is treated as *vivṛta.*

**laghu**: kharo vivārāḥ śvāsā aghoṣāś ca \| haśaḥ saṁvārā nādā ghoṣāś ca \| vargāṇāṁ prathama-tṛtīya-pañcamā yaṇaś cālpaprāṇāḥ \| vargāṇāṁ dvitīya-caturthau śalaś ca mahāprāṇāḥ \|

The external effort is of eleven kinds:

- 1\. *vivāra*
- 2\. *saṁvāra*
- 3\. *śvāsa*
- 4\. *nāda*
- 5\. *aghoṣa*
- 6\. *ghoṣa*
- 7\. *alpaprāṇa*
- 8\. *mahāprāṇa*
- 9\. *udātta*
- 10\. *anudātta*
- 11\. *svarita.*

The three external efforts *vivāra*, *śvāsa* and *aghoṣa* belong to *khar*, that is, *kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ* *s* (during the process of their being pronounced). The efforts known as *saṁvāra*, *nāda* and *ghoṣa* belong to the elements denoted by *has*, that is, *h, y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ* and *d*. The first, third and fifth stops of the class groups, and yaṇ (y v r l) have *alpaprāṇa* effort whereas the second and fourth of the class groups and *śal* (*ś ṣ s* and *h*) have *mahaprāṇa* effort.

The external effort known as *vivāra* consists in keeping the glottis open and is witnessed when the process of articulating the elements denoted by *khar* is on. The *saṁvara* effort consists in contracting the glottis while pronouncing the *haś* phonemes. In *śvāsa* effort comparatively more breath is exhaled as is seen while pronouncing the *khar* elements. The external effort *nāda* consists in creating some resonance as while pronouncing the *haś* phonemes. The efforts *ghoṣa* and *aghoṣa* belong to voiced (*haś*) and voiceless (*khar*) consonants respectively. The former have the depth of the tone, whereas the latter don't have it. The first, third and fifth of the five group classes and *yaṇ* require comparatively less breath from the mouth for their utterance. Hence they belong to *alpaprāṇa* (non-aspirate) category. Under *mahāprāṇa* (aspirate) category fall the second and fourth stops of the group classes and *śal* fricatives since they have aspirate characteristic and require more *prāṇa* while being pronounced. The *udātta*, *anudāta* and *svarita* vowels have already been explained under (6), (7) and (8).

**laghu**: kādayo māvasānāḥ sparśāḥ \| yaṇo 'ntaḥsthāḥ \| śala ūṣmāṇaḥ \| acaḥ svarāḥ \| \[...\] ka \[...\] kha iti ka-khābhyāṁ prag-ardha-visarga-sadṛśo jihvāmūlīyaḥ \| \[...\] pa \[...\] pha iti pa-phābhyāṁ prāg-ardha-visarga-sadṛśa upadhmānīyaḥ \| aṁ aḥ ityacaḥ parāvanusvāra-visargau \|\|

**Trans.**: The class sounds beginning with *k* and ending with *m* are called stops (*sparśa*). The phonemes denoted by *yaṇ* are semivowels (*antaḥstha*). The *śal* fricatives are known as spirants (*ūṣma*). The phonemes denoted by *ac* pratyahara are designated as *svaras* (vowels). The symbol preceding *k* and *kh* which somewhat resembles *half-visarga* is called *jihvāmūlīya*. The same symbol used before either *p* or *ph* is called *upadhmānīya*. @M @:– here the dot above @and two dots immediately following it respectively represent *anusvāra* (*ṁ*) and *visarga* (*ḥ*).

**Eluc**.: There is no obstruction in the way of the pulmonary air (*prāṇa*) coming out of the mouth when vowels are uttered. When we attempt to pronounce the vowels, we keep the throat and mouth open and they remain so when the vowels are being produced. This is the reason that they are called *vivṛta*. On the other hand there is obstruction when consonants are being articulated as there is contact between some part of the tongue and that of the organ of pronunciation. As an illustration, take ca-varga stops c, ch, etc. When we attempt to articulate these stops, the front portion of the tongue touches the hard palate and thus the air coming out is obstructed. When we attempt to pronounce the vowels, there is no such obstruction. The five group classes of the consonants beginning with *k* and ending with *m* are called stops or *sparśa* since, when we articulate them, there is contact of some portion of the tongue with some other part of the mouth. When we articulate the labial stops, both lips come in contact with each other to obstruct the pulmonary air.

This contact is slightly less in case of the semi-vowels, that is, *yaṇ* (*antaḥstha*). As such the internal effort used in producing these semivowels is called *īṣat-sprṣṭa*. *Uvaṭa* calls them *antaḥsthā* in the feminine and explains that they are so called because they are placed between the stops (*sparśa*) and the spirants (ūṣma). The *śal* are called *ūṣma* as there is an exhalation of breath while producing these sounds.

The *visarga*, when followed either by *k* or *kh*, is replaced by the symbol \[...\] which represents j*ihvāmūlīya*. The *visarga* is produced in the throat. When it is immediately followed by *k* or *kh*, it is produced at the root of the tongue. As such it is called (j*ihvāmūlīya*) which means articulated at the root of the tongue. When the *visarga* is immediately followed by either *p* or *ph*, it is replaced by this \[...\] symbol which represents *upadhmānīya*. The place of articulation of the former is the root of the tongue, whereas the articulation of upadhmanlya takes place at the lips. @M @:– here the dot above @represents the segment *anusvāra* and is pronounced with the help of the nose. The two dots immediately following @represent *visarga* and are pronounced in the throat. It deserves mention here that *visarga*, j*ihvāmūlīya*, *upadhmānīya* and *anusvāra* are also called *ayogavāhas*. They share the characteristic of articulation with their attendant phonetic element.

## 11 – aṇ-udit-savarṇasya cā'pratyayaḥ \| 1.1.69

**PP: aṇ-udit-savarṇasya ca apratyayaḥ svasya \|**

*pratīyate vidhīyate iti pratyayaḥ \| avidhīyamāno 'ṇ-udic ca savarṇasya saṁjñā syāt \| atraivāṇ pareṇa ṇa-kāreṇa \| ku cu ṭu tu pu ete uditāḥ \| tad evam – a ityaṣṭādaśānāṁ saṁjñā \| tathekārokārau \| ṛ-kāras triṁśataḥ \| evaṁ – ḷ-kāro 'pi \| eco dvādaśānām \| anunāsikānanunāsika-bhedena ya va lā dvidhā \| tenā'nanunāsikās te dvayor dvayos saṁjñā \|\|*

Let a letter, denoted by the *pratyāhāra* *aṇ*, not propounded as an affix or operative agent, but as something to be operated upon, and let in like manner a letter followed by an indicatory *u*, be the name of (and so imply) its homogeneous letters also \|

**pratīyate vidhīyate iti pratyayaḥ**—a letter when demonstrated for application by a precept is called *pratyayaḥ* or *vidhīyamānaḥ* (specified or indicated); **avidhīyamāno** (*apratyayaḥ*)—unspecified or non-indicatory (antonym of the *pratyayaḥ* or *vidhīyamānaḥ*); **aṇ-udic ca** (aṇ-udit ca)—

(I) *aṇ* – letters contained in *aṇ* *pratyāhāra*; ***atraivāṇ pareṇa ṇa-kāreṇa*** (*atra + eva + aṇ + pareṇa + ṇa-kāreṇa*)—here *aṇ pratyāhāra* is extended upto *ṇ* of the aphorism *laṇ* which denotes all vowels + *h, y, v, r, l* \|

(II) – *udit* – letters contained in the series *ku cu ṭu tu pu* are called *udit* (the five class of consonants);

**ku cu ṭu tu pu ete uditāḥ** (*ku cu ṭu tu pu ete ut itāḥ*)—*ku* is called *ka-varga*... The final *u* in each of the series *ku cu ṭu tu pu* (class consonants) is to be termed as *it* as per precept – *upadeśe 'janunāsika it*. So these are defined as ***udit*** (***ut it yasya***) by denoting the letters of class consonants viz. starting from *k* and ending in *m.*

**savarṇasya saṁjñā syāt**— to be the name or definition of their homogeneous (implied varieties).

Unspecified letters of *aṇ pratyāhāra* (the vowels, the aspirate *h* and the semi-vowels) and the *udit* (the class consonants) are to be the homogeneous with their implied varieties;

**tad evam**—that is in this matter (for example); **a ityaṣṭādaśānāṁ saṁjñā** (*a iti aṣṭādaśānāṁ saṁjñā*)— the *a* is the definition of its eighteen varieties and hence reciprocally homogeneous as it possesses – 3 *hrasva-dīrgha-pluta* X3 *udāttānudātta-svarita* X2 *anunāsikānanunāsika*; **tathekārokārau** (tathā i-kāra u-kārau)— accordingly, the *i* and *u* are also implying their eighteen modifications as mutually homogeneous; ṛ-**kāras triṁśataḥ**— the *ṛ* denotes its thirty varieties as homogeneous, viz.- eighteen modifications of its own and twelve to that of the *ḷ* as *ṛ* and *ḷ* are reciprocally homogeneous (*ṛ-ḷ-varṇayor mithaḥ sāvarṇasya vācyam*); **evaṁ – ḷ-kāro 'pi**— accordingly, the *ḷ* is mutually homogeneous to its thirty modifications like *ṛ* viz.- twelve modifications of its own and eighteen to that of the *ṛ*; **eco dvādaśānām**— each of the diphthongs (letters containing in *ec pratyāhāra* viz. *e o ai au*) are the name of their twelve varieties (due to want of their short form) and thus mutually homogeneous; **anunāsikānanunāsika-bhedena yavalā dvidhā** (anunāsika ananunāsika bhedena ya va lā dvidhā)—the semi-vowels *y, v, l* each denotes their two distinctive varieties viz. nasality and non-nasality (anunāsika ananunāsika) as homogeneous; **tenā'nanunāsikās te dvayor dvayos saṁjñā** (tena ananunāsikāḥ te dvayoḥ dvayoḥ saṁjñā)— rest two letters viz. *h* & *r* (out of the *aṇ pratyāhāra*) do not possess any distinctive varieties and hence they are self defined (*rephoṣmaṇāṁ savarṇā na santi*) \|\|

**Eluc.**: The word *svam* is carried over from the sūtra *svaṁ rūpaṁ śabdasyāśabda-saṁjñā* Pā. 1.1. 68 and is changed into the genitive in the present sūtra. The word *udit* is a *bahuvrīhi* compound which is analysed as follows : *ut it yasya sa udit*. This denotes *ku cu ṭu tu pu*. The meaning of the word *apratyayaḥ* in the sūtra is 'that which is not being enjoined grammatically' and it qualifies the *pratyāhāra* *aṇ* only and not that which is signified by *udit*. If any of the denotata of *aṇ* *pratyāhāra* is not grammatically enjoined, then it will denote itself and all its homogeneous counterparts. This comes to mean that if *a* is not being enjoined, it will denote all its eighteen forms, but if it is being enjoined, it will denote only its six forms of the duration of a single mora and not those forms having the duration of two or three morae. In the sītra *iko yaṇaci, ik* is not enjoined; hence every element signified by it will denote itself and all its homogeneous forms. On the other hand *yaṇ* is being enjoined in place of *ik*. The denotata of *yaṇ* will not signify all their homogeneous forms. It means one of the denotata of *yaṇ* , that is, *y* will denote its own form and not its *anunāsika* form. This is the reason why *i*-substituend may be any of its eighteen forms but the *y*-substitute will confine to itself. It will not include *anunāsika* *ya-kāra*. Similarly in the sūtra *sanāśaṁsabhikṣa uḥ* (837), the affix *u* is being enjoined. Hence it will stand for itself only and not for its long and prolated forms. Pāṇini does not intend to qualify *udit* by *apratyayaḥ*. It is clear from the sūtra *coḥ kuḥ* (305). On the basis of this sūtra *c ch j jh ñ* which are denoted by *cu* will be substituted by *k kh g gh ṅ* respectively at the end of a pada or when *jhal* follows. Had Pāṇini intended to qualify *udit* by *apratyayaḥ*, the sūtra *coḥ kuḥ* would have meant: only *k* is substituted in place of any of *c ch j jh* and *ṅ*, which obviously is not the case.

The marker *ṇ* comes twice in the *pratyāhāra-sūtras*. The present sūtra is the only place in the *Aṣṭādhyayī* where the *pratyāhāra* *aṇ* is formed with the latter *ṇ*, i.e. the *ṇ* of *laṇ*. In all other occurrences of *aṇ*, it is formed with the *ṇ* of *a i uṇ*. This has been clarified by Patañjali in the

Mahābhāsya on the lan sūtra. The conclusion is given in the following verse:

*pareṇaiveṇgrahāḥ sarve pūrveṇaivāṅgrahā matāḥ,*  
*ṛte 'ṇ-udit-savarṇasyety etad-ekam pareṇa tu (Mahābhāṣya edited byVedavrata, lan sutra, p. 115, fn +)*

The *iṇ*-pratyāhāra, at all places, is accepted to be constituted by the *ṇ* of *laṇ*-pratyāhāra-sūtra. The pratyāhāra *aṇ* is understood to be formed with the *ṇ* of praty·hāra-sūtra *a i uṇ*. This excludes the sūtra, *aṇ-udit savarṇasya cāpratyayaḥ* in which *aṇ* is accepted as formed by the latter *ṇa-kāra* (of *laṇ*).

Thus *a i u* will signify all their eighteen forms in the manner already explained. The vowels *ṛ* and *ḷ* will each denote its thirty varieties (*ṛ's* 18 and *ḷ's* 12 = 30) since they have been declared to be homogeneous by Kātyāyana’s vārttika (1) under *tulyāsya-prayatnaṁ savarṇam* (10).

The vowels denoted by *ec* will denote their twelve forms because they do not have short counterparts. The *ananunāsika* *y, v* and *ḷ* will each signify its two forms.

## 12 – paraḥ sannikarṣaḥ saṁhitā \| 1.4.109

*varṇānām atiśayitaḥ sannidhiḥ saṁhitā saṁjñaḥ syāt \|*

**varṇānām**—of the letters; **paraḥ (atiśayitaḥ)**—the closest or subsequent or the next to; **sannikarṣaḥ (sannidhiḥ pratyāsattiḥ)**— proximity (gapless utterance); **saṁhitā saṁjñaḥ syāt**—to be termed as *saṁhitā* or *sandhi* (*aikyakaraṇam*) or euphony.

The closest proximity of letters is termed as *saṁhitā* or Euphony.

The coalition or euphonically alliance of two or more nearest letters, that conjoins separate words into one, is called as Euphony.

*saṁhitaikapade nityā nityā dhātūpasargayoḥ \| nityā samāse vākye tu sā vivakṣāmapekṣate \|*

the *saṁhitā* or *sandhi* or euphonically conjunction is mandatory in case of:

**(i)** an un-interrupted and inflected word (a crude word or *prātipadika* with a case-affix or *vibhakti-pratyaya*, such as – *rāma su = rāmaḥ*;

**(ii)** coalition of preposition (*upasarga*) with verbal root (*dhātu*),

**(iii)** a compound or samāsa (union of constituting words of a sentence in to one word).

Excluding these three grounds, the *sandhi* depends on the option of the speaker or *vaktā.*

The euphonically conjunctions are of three types viz. *svara-sandhi* or *ac-sandhi* (the euphony of vowels), *vyañjana-sandhi* or *hal-sandhi* (the conjunction of consonants) and *visarga-sandhi* (the conjunction of visarga).

**Eluc.**: When there is extreme proximity between two phonemes, there occurs natural phonetic coalescence of the preceding and the following sounds. This extreme juxtaposition between the preceding and the following phonemes is the condition for the euphonic combination (*sandhi*) to take place.

## 13 – halo 'nantarāḥ saṁyogaḥ \| 1.1.7

*ajbhir avyavahatā halaḥ saṁyoga-saṁjñāḥ syuḥ \|\|*

**halaḥ anantarāḥ (ajibhiḥ avyavahitā halaḥ)**— the consonants disassociated with the intervening vowels; **saṁyoga-saṁjñā syuḥ**—to be as Conjunction.

The coalition of consonants, not separated by the intervening vowels, is to be termed as *saṁyoga* or Conjunction.

(2) Let consonants unseparated by vowels be called a conjunction of consonants.

The coalition of two or more bare consonants (without association of any vowel) are to be termed as Conjunction or *saṁyoga.*

**Eluc.**: When two or more consonants (*hal*) are not separated by any vowel (*ac*), they are termed *saṁyoga* (conjunct). In the word *indra*, *n*, *d* and *r* are not separated by any vowel. Hence *n*, *d* and *r* will be designated as *saṁyoga*. Similarly in the word *uṣṭra*, *ṣ*, *ṭ* and *r* will be named *saṁyoga*. The application of this term will be seen in sutras like *saṁyogāntasya lopaḥ* (20), etc.

## 14 – sup-tiṅ-antaṁ padam \| 1.4.14

*sub-antaṁ tiṅ-antaṁ ca pada-saṁjñaṁ syāt \|\|*

Let a word that terminates in *sup* or *tiṅ* be designated as *pada* (inflected word).

**Eluc.**: *Yāska* divides the padas into four categories: *nāma, ākhyāta, upasarga* and *nipāta* but *Pāṇini* has reduced the classification to two, that is, *subanta* and *tiṅanta*. The terms *sup* and *tiṅ* are pratyāhāras which are respectively formed with the help of Pā. 4.1.2 and 3.4.78. In the former sūtra the inflectional affixes are given. These affixes are as follows: *su au jas, am auṭ śas, ṭā bhyām bhis, ṅe bhyām bhyas, ṅasi bhyām bhyas, ṅas os ām, ṅi os sup*. The pratyāhāra *sup* is formed by taking the initial *su* (underlined) and adding to it the final marker *p* (underlined) of the last inflectional affix *sup*. Similarly, *tiṅ* pratyahara is formed. This denotes the conjugational affixes which are given in the latter sūtra. The conjugational affixes given in the sūtra are as follows: *tip tas jhi, sip thus tha, mip vas mas, ta ātām jha, thās āthām dhvam, iṭ vahi mahiṅ*. The beginning *ti* (underlined) of *tip* with the marker *ṅ* (underlined) of the last conugational affix *mahiṅ* forms the *pratyāhāra* *tiṅ*. These are two examples where the technique of forming a *pratyāhāra* which is laid down by the sūtra *ādir antyena sahetā* (4) is also applied. The *sup*-affixes are added to the nominal stems (*prātipadikas*) to form *nama-padas* and the affixes denoted by *tiṅ pratyāhāra* are added to the verbal roots (dhātus) to form *kriyāpadas*. If these affixes are not added, a word is not a finished word and hence not fit to be used in a sentence according to the Pāṇinian sytem of grammar. Thus, according to Pāṇini, the whole corpus of Sanskrit language is exhausted by these two categories.

Thus ends the chapter on technical terms.

## sandhi-prakaraṇam

ac-sandhi (vowel sandhi)

## 15 – iko yaṇ aci \| 6.1.77

(ikaḥ yaṇ aci)

**PP. ikaḥ yaṇ aci saṁhitāyām \|**

*ikaḥ sthāne yaṇ syād aci saṁhitāyāṁ viṣaye \| sudhī upāsyaḥ iti sthite \|\|*

**ikaḥ sthane**—in the place of *ik* viz. *i, u, ṛ, ḷ* (*ikaḥ* is the termination of *ik* in the form of 6thcase); **yaṇ syāt**— the substitution is to be the *yaṅ* viz. *y, v, r* or *l* (*yaṅ* is the termination in nominative case); **aci**—if a letter of *ac* or a vowel (*a i u ṛ ḷ e ai o au*) follows; **saṁhitāyāṁ viṣaye (sandhi-viṣaye)**— in the event of euphonic coalition; **sudhī upāsyaḥ iti sthite**— in the occurrence of *sudhī* (the intelligent) + *upāsyaḥ* (to be worshipped)

In place of a letter of *ik* *pratyāhāra*, let there be a letter substituted by *yaṇ* *pratyāhāra*, if a letter of *ac pratyāhāra* follows. In the occurrence of *sudhī + upāsyaḥ*

The means of general application of the aphorisms in Pāṇinian Grammar are that: **(i)** any operation is to be executed in place of the genitive case, **(ii)** nominative case is to be substituted against the genitive case, and **(iii)** locative case is to be remain unchanged as it is regarded as the cause of the change. The force of these terminations is to be ascertained from other aphorisms.

As per the above agreement, the *ik pratyāhāra* is to be substituted by the

*yaṇ pratyāhāra*, if follows a letter of *ac pratyāhāra*. Here, the *ik pratyāhāra* is called *avidhīyamāna* owing to its removal by substituting the specified (*vidhīyamāna*) letter of

*yaṇ pratyahāra*. Hence the final *ī* of the word *sudhī* is to be denominated by its homogeneous (see sūtra 11).

The grounds, which occasion maximum proximity (*saṁhitā*) between two phonemes, are enumerated in the following verse:

*saṁhitaikapade nityā*  
*nityā dhātūpasargayoh*  
*nityā samāse vākye tu*  
*sa vivakṣāmapekṣate*

*Saṁhitā*is compulsory in a single finished word, between a preverb (*upasarga*) and a verbal root and in a compound, but in a sentence it depends upon the intention of the speaker.

Since the present illustration is a compound, *saṁhitā* (closest proximity) is necessarily available here. Thus in *sudhī + upāsya*, which is a compound, the present sutra *iko yaṇ aci* is applicable.

## 16 – tasminn iti nirdiṣṭe pūrvasya \| 1.1.66

saptamī nirdeśena vidhīyamānaṁ kāryaṁ varṇāntareṇa avyavahitasya pūrvasya bodhyam \|

**tasminn iti (saptamī nirdeśena)**—which is exhibited in the term of 7thcase; **vidhīyamānaṁ kāryaṁ**—which is directed or specified for execution (i.e. *yaṇ* is to be substitute of *ik*); **varṇāntareṇa**—in between the letters; **avyavahitasya pūrvasya**—of the unintervening or gapless preceding letter; **bodhyam**—to be understood or ascertained.

**(G)**It is to be ascertained that the operation (as directed in the precept 15 (*iko yaṇ aci*)) is to be executed against the unintervening letter prior to that of the letter exhibited in the term of locative case.

**(2)**When a term is exhibited in the seventh case the operation directed is to be understood as affecting the state of what immediately precedes that which the term denotes.

**(3)**When a term is stated in the locative case, let the grammatical operation directed by it be performed on that which precedes the term without being separated even by a single phoneme.

**Eluc.**:The use of the term *iti* in *śabdānuśāsana* shifts the emphasis from the word itself to its meaning. Consequently the term *iti* after *tasmin* shifts the emphasis from the word *tasmin* to the meaning conveyed by it, that is, *tasmin* means "the meaning conveyed by the term used in the locative nominal ending". The term *aci* which ends in the locative denotes a vowel... Jñanendra Sarasvatī interprets *nirdiṣṭe* as *avyavahitoccārite sati*). The present aphorism also ensures that the substitution takes place only in place of what immediately precedes and not in place of that which immediately follows.

(G) Now the question occurs which one of these letters of *yaṇ* (*y, v, r, l*) is to be substituted in place of the final *ī* of the word *sudhī*? In this context the following explanatory aphorism directs.

## 17 – sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ \| 1.1.50

prasaṅge sati sadṛśatama ādeśaḥ syāt \| su dh y upāsyaḥ iti jāte \|

**sthāne (prasaṅge sati)**—i place of (*ṣaṣṭhī sthāne yogā* – in place of the final *ī* of the word *sudhī*); **antaratamaḥ (sadṛśatama)**—a letter of the same class (homogeneous); **ādeśaḥ syāt**—to be substituted; **su dh y upāsyaḥ iti jāte**—in the occurrence of *su dh y + upāsyaḥ*.

(G) In place of (*sthāne*) a letter when more than one term arises as substitute, a homogeneous (*antaratamaḥ*) letter is to be substituted there.

(K) When (common) substitutes are available, let the most similar substitute replace the substituendum.

Similarity is of four types:

- 1\. similarity based on the place of articulation (*sthānakṛta*)
- 2\. similarity based on signification (*arthakṛta*)
- 3\. similarity based on sound quality (*guṇakṛta*)
- 4\. similarity based on duration (*pramāṇakṛta*).

## 18 – an-aci ca \| 8.4.47

**PP. acaḥ yaraḥ dve vā an-aci ca (saṁhitāyām)**

*acaḥ parasya yaro dve vā sto na tvaci \| iti dha-kārasya dvitvam \|*

**an-aci (na tu aci)**—but not if a vowel (*ac*) follows, in other words if a consonant (*hal*) follows; **acaḥ parasya yaraḥ**—a letter of *yar* (all the consonants exept the *h*) if comes after a vowel; **dve vā staḥ**—to be optionally reduplicated.

(G) A letter belongs to *yar* is to be reduplicated optionally, if that comes after a letter of *ac*, but not if a vowel follows\|

(G) That, in the euphony – *s u dh y + upāsyaḥ* – the *dh* of the word *sudhī*

(i) belongs to *yar*

(ii) follows to the vowel – *u*

(iii) precedes to the non-vowel (a consonant). Hence, the *dh* pertaining to *yar* is optionally reduplicated as *s u dh dh y + upāsyaḥ*. In this context the following rule comes in force.

**Eluc.**: In this sūtra *acaḥ* and *dve* are carried over from *aco rahābhyāṁ*

*dve*Pā. 8.4.46 and *yar* and *vā* from *yaro 'nunāsike 'nunāsiko vā* Pā. 8.4.45. When these four terms are read into this sūtra, it becomes a complete sentence as given by *Varadarāja* in his *vṛtti.*

The term *an-aci* in the sūtra is a *nañ-tatpuruṣa* compound (*na + ac*) and ends in the locative. The term *nañ* is a particle that conveys negation. The negation is of two kinds: **1.** paryudāsa (exclusion) and **2.** *prasajya* (prohibition connected with an action). The author of the Kāśikā interprets the negative particle here in the sense of exclusion, that is, the negative compound *an-ac* means *tad-bhinna-tat-sadṛśa* "that which is similar to and different from *ac*". In other words, the negative compound in the present sūtra conveys the sense of *anac-parasya.*

## 19 – jhalāṁ jaś jhaśi \| 8.4.53

spaṣṭam iti pūrva-dha-kārasya da-kāraḥ \|

**jhalaṁ**(6thcase)—in place of a letter pertaining to *jhal*; **jaś**(1stcase)—a letter of *jaś*; **jhaśi**(7thcase)—if a letter of *jhaś* follows. (So, the nominative case is to be the substitute of the genitive case, if follows the locative case).

(G) A letter of *jaś* is to be the substitute of a letter of *jhal*, if a letter of *jhaś* follows.

## 20 – saṁyogāntasya lopaḥ \| 8.2.23

**PP. saṁyoga-antasya padasya lopaḥ \|**

saṁyogāntaṁ yat padaṁ tadantasya lopaḥ syāt \|

**saṁyogāntaṁ yat padaṁ tadantasya**—of the *pada* that is annexed by any letters at the end (in *suddhy* the letters *ddhy* are annexed at the end); **lopaḥ syāt**—is to be elided.

(G) An inflected word or a *pada* is to be elided if that is annexed by any letters at the end \|

(K) Let *lopa* be substituted in place of the *pada* which ends in a conjunct (*saṁyoga*)\|

## 21 – alo 'ntyasya \| 1.1.52

(alaḥ antyasya)

**PP: alaḥ antasya saṣṭhī sthāne** \|

*ṣaṣṭhī nirdiṣṭasyāntasyāla ādeśaḥ syāt \| iti ya-lope prāpte \|*

**ṣaṣṭhī nirdiṣṭasya**—of the approach in term of genitive case (in the precept – 15, *iko yaṇ aci* the *iko* is directed in term of 6thcase *ikaḥ*); **antasya alaḥ ādeśaḥ syāt**—of the substitution that is executed against the final letter.

A substitution (operation) takes place against the final letter, if that is directed in term of the genitive case\|

**Eluc.**: The terms *saṣṭhī* and *sthāne* have been carried over here from *saṣṭhī sthāne yogā* Pā. 1.1.49. The word saṣṭhī will be changed into the instrumental, which will give the sense "indicated by the genitive" (*saṣṭhyā nirdiṣṭasya*). This coupled with the word *sthāne* will mean that the substitute, which has been enjoined to come in place of a substituendum indicated by the use of substitution-genitive, will replace only the final element of the term used in the genitive . By the preceding rule, the whole *pada* ending in a conjunct has to undergo deletion but the present rule restricts the deletion only to the last element of that which has been specified in the genitive. Thus in the illustration under discussion, the final *y* of both the forms has got to be elided. Here *Kātyāyana's vārttika* presents itself and prohibits the elision of *y* which is one of the elements denoted by *yaṇ* \|

*(vā.) yaṇaḥ pratiṣedho vācyaḥ \| suddhyupāsyaḥ sudhyupāsyaḥ \|*

**yaṇaḥ**—a letter of *yaṇ* (*y, v, r, l*); **pratiṣedhaḥ**—contradiction or prohibition; **vācyaḥ**—predicated or told \|

In the issue of elision of a final letter, it is prohibited, if that happens to be a letter of *yaṇ*, as predicted by *Kātyāyana.*

(K) If the last phoneme belongs to the elements denoted by *yaṇ*, then its deletion should be prohibited.

This supplementary rule *yaṇaḥ pratiṣedho vācyaḥ* contradicts to the preceding rule – *alo 'ntyasya* and *alo 'ntyasya* contradicts to the precept – *saṁyogāntasya lopaḥ*. As per the rule – *alo 'ntyasya* there must be the elision of the final letter of a *saṁyogānta pada*, if it is directed in the term of genitive case. Here, as per the supplementary rule – *yaṇaḥ pratiṣedho vācyaḥ*, it is forbidden for elision of the letters pertaining to *yaṇ*. So, the final letter *y* in the *saṁyogānta pada* i.e. *su d dh y* (and *su dh y* in absence of the optional precept *an aci ca*) is not to be elided and remains unchanged. Here, the euphony is to be concluded as – *su d dh y + upāsyaḥ* = (to be worshipped by the intelligent).

*maddhvariḥ madhvariḥ \| dhāttraṁśaḥ dhātraṁśaḥ \| lākṛtiḥ \|*

Note: The precept *iko yaṇ aci* is applicable if a nonhomogenic vowel comes after a letter of *ik*. When a homogenic vowel follows a letter of *ik*, the precept *ikaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ* is to be employed there.

## 22 – eco 'y av āy āvaḥ \| 6.1.78

**PP: ecaḥ ay av āy āvaḥ aci saṁhitāyām** \|

(ecaḥ ay av āy āvaḥ)

*ecaḥ kramād ay av āy āv ete syur aci \|\|*

**ecaḥ**—in the place of the letters belong to *ec* (*e, o, ai, au*); **kramād**—respectevly; **ay av āy āv ete syuḥ**—are to be the substitute;

(G) In place of the letters pertaining to *ec*, the *ay, av, ay & av* are to be substituted in due order, if a vowel follows\|

(K) When followed by *ac*, let the elements (*e o ai au*) denoted by *ec* be replaced by *ay av āy āv* respectively\|

As regards to the word *kramād* (respectively), the next explanatory rule simplifies.

## 23 – yathā saṅkhyam anudeśaḥ samānām \| 1.3.10

**PP: samānām anudeśaḥ yathā saṅkhyam** \|

*samasaṁ bandhī vidhir yathā saṁkhyaṁ syāt \| haraye \| viṣṇave \| nāyakaḥ \| pāvakaḥ \|*

**samānām (samasaṁ bandhī)**—when equal fitness involves in; **anudeśaḥ (vidhiḥ)**—a rule; **saṅkhyaṁ syāt**—to be in order of enumeration \|

(G) When a rule involves in an equal fitness of both the place and the substitution, that is to be executed in order of enumeration\|

## 24 – vānto yi pratyaye \| 6.1.79

**PP: vāntaḥ yi pratyaye (saṁhitāyām)**

*ya-kārādau pratyaye pare od autor av āv etau staḥ \| gavyam \| nāvyam \|*

**yi pratyaye (ya-kārādau pratyaye pare)**—in an affix anitiated by the *y* follows; **od autoḥ (ot aut)**—both in places of the *o* and *au*; **av āv (va-antaḥ vāntaḥ)**—which end with *va-kāra* (the *av* and *āv* in the precept (ecaḥ ay av āy āvaḥ); **etau staḥ**—these two (*av* and *āv*) are to be substituted \|

(G) *av* & *āv* are to be the substitute in place of *o* & *au* respectively, if an affix initiated by the *y* immediately follows\|

(vā.) adhva-parimāṇe ca \| gavyūtiḥ \|

**goḥ yūtau vāntaḥ adhva-parimāṇe ca** \|

**Eluc.**: The term *vāntaḥ* has been carried over from the sūtra *vānto yi pratyaye* and *yūtau* from the vārttika *goryūtau chandasi* (Mbh 6.1.79 vtk 2) which just precedes this *vārttika* in the *Mahābhāṣya*. Thus the elliptical *vārttika* becomes a complete sentence and means that the vowel *o* of *go* is substituted by *av* when the word *yūti* follows \|

**adhva**—distance of a way; **parimāṇe**ca—also, in the measurement of; **gavyūtiḥ (go yūtiḥ)**—*go* (cow/ox); yūtiḥ (though it is not an affix initiated by the *y*), **vyūtiḥ**—is an ambiguous (dual meaning) word that denotes the meaning of (i) the measure of four miles distance and (ii) to yoke of oxen for plough. This is a supplementary rule to *vānto yi pratyaye* \|

(B) And when the compound is employed in the sense of a measure of distance, the *o* of *go*, followed by *yūti* (though this is not an affix) becomes *av*. Thus *go + yūti = gavyūti* (to take the cow a distance of four miles), when it signifies “a distance of about four miles but the substitution does not take place when it signifies a “yoke of oxen” (goyūti) \|

(K) Although the substituenda are not mentioned in the rule, they are made available by the statement of Patañjali on *vāntādeśe sthāni nirdeśaḥ* Mbh 6.1.79 vtk 1. He says on this vārttika that the vowels *o* and *au* should be enunciated as the substituenda to be replaced by *av* and *av* (*o-kāra-u-kārayor iti vaktavyam*) \|

## 25 – ad eṅ guṇaḥ \| 1.1.2

(at eṅ guṇaḥ)

at eṅ ca guṇa-saṁjñaḥ syāt \|

**at eṅ ca**—*at* (*hrasva-a*) and also *eṅ* (*eṅ* pratyāhāra *e* and *o*); **guṇa-saṁjñaḥ syāt**—to be termed as *guṇa* \|

(G) The *a, e* and *o* are termed as *guṇa*\|

(K) Let *at* (*a*) and *eṅ* (*e* and *o*) be designated as *guṇa*\|

(G )The *hrasva a-kāra* is termed as *guṇa*, but not that of its long & prolated forms and the *e* & *o* are termed as *guṇa*, but not that of the *ai* & *au* \|

(B) But why is the short a alone understood here in seeming contradiction to what was said before? The next rule will account for this \|

## 26 – ta-paras tat-kālasya \| 1.1.70

**PP: ta-paraḥ tat-kālasya svasya rūpasya savarṇasya** \|

(taparaḥ tat kālasya)

*taḥ paro yasmāt sa ca tāt-paraś coccāryamāṇaḥ samakālasyaiva saṁjñā syāt \|*

**taḥ paro yasmāt sa ca**—a letter to whom the *t* follows (a letter after *t*); **accāryamāṇaḥ**—that articulater letter; **samakālasyaiva saṁjñā syāt**—to be the definition of its same prosodical lenth, (as per this rule, a letter of the avidhīyamāna *aṇ* pratāhara (*a i u ṛ ḷ e o ai au h y v r l*)) does not represent its other modifications if stated in term of the *tapara*) \|

(B) Let a vowel followed by the letter *t*, and a vowel following the letter *t*, be the name only of the letter which has the same prosodial length\|

The letter *a* is the representation of eighteen varieties; but when it is followed by *t*, as in the preceding rule, it represents neither the long nor the prolated modifications \|

**Eluc.**: The terms *svaṁ rūpam* are carried over from *svaṁ rūpaṁ śabdasyāśabda-saṁjñā* Pā. 1.1.68 into the present sūtra. The carried over terms *svaṁ rūpam* are changed into the genitive by the process of *vibhakti-vipariṇāma*. The term *savarṇasya* comes from *aṇudit savarṇasya cāpratyayaḥ* Pā. 1.1.69. The term *taparaḥ* in the sūtra is interpreted both as *tatpuruṣa* and *bahuvrīhi* compound. When it is understood as *bahuvrīhi*, it means a phoneme that has *t* after it. As *tatpuruṣa* compound it means a phoneme which follows *t*. Here taparakaraṇa is meant for both *a* and the elements denoted by *eṅ*. In the case of *a*, *t* follows it and in the latter case *eṅ* follows *t*. Both *a* and *eṅ* are *tapara*. The sūtra thus means: The phoneme (vowel), which is followed by *t* and which follows *t*, denotes itself and the homogeneous phonemes of the same duration (*sama-kālasya*).

The sūtra (11) lays down that the vowel which is not enjoined denotes itself and all other seventeen homogeneous counterparts irrespective of their duration. It means that the short vowel *a* which is not enjoined denotes itself, the long and the prolated counterparts. Each has six varieties. The six varieties of short *a* have one mora, the six varieties of long il have two morae and the other six varieties of the prolated one, three morae. The first six varieties which have one-mora-duration are to be denoted by the short *a* followed by *t* or preceded by it by virtue of the present sūtra and not its eighteen varieties which would have been the case in the absence of this sūtra. Similarly *e* and *o* which have no homogeneous short counterparts denote only the homogeneous long counterparts of two-mora-duration when either they are preceded by *t* or followed by it. They do not denote the prolated counterparts which have more than two morae. The same holds true in respect of the other vowel sounds, *i, u, r, l*, etc.

According to this sūtra, the vowels *a, e* and *o* have been assigned the *guṇa* designation which has its application in the following sūtra.

## 27 – ād guṇaḥ \| 6.1.87

**PP: āt aci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ guṇaḥ saṁhitayam** \|

*avarṇāt aci pare pūrva-parayor eko guṇa ādeśaḥ syāt \| upendraḥ \| gaṅgodakam \|*

**āt (avarṇāt)**—from the *a* or *ā* (here *āt* do not mean *tapara* and it is terminated in the 5thcase); **aci pare**—if a vowel follows; **pūrva-parayoḥ**—in both places of the prior and the subsequent; **eko guṇa ādeśaḥ syāt**—a single *guṇa* (*a, e* or *o*) is to be substituted for both \|

(G) After the *a* or *ā* if a vowel follows, a *guṇa* (*a, e* or *o*) is to be the single substitute for both the prior and the subsequent\|

(K) When *a* is followed by *ac*, let a single *guṇa* substitute replace both the preceding and the following (phonemes)\|

(K) The compound term *pūrva-parayoḥ*, occurring in the vṛtti of Varadarāja, is given because this sūtra falls in the domain of *ekaḥ pūrva-parayoḥ* Pā. 6.1.84. The domain extends upto Pā. 6.1.112. The term *aci* has been carried over from *iko yaṇ aci* Pā. 6.1.77. The sūtra *saṁhitāyām* Pā. 6.1.72 is also a governing rule. So the word *saṁhitāyām* recurs here also. This word is to recur in all the sūtras ranging from Pā. 6.1.72 to Pā. 6.1.158 in a similar way. In this way the completed sūtra means that when extreme proximity is intended (*saṁhitāyām*), one *guṇa* element substitutes both the preceding and the following phonemes if the vowel *a* is followed by *ac*. When *a* follows *a*, this sutra is blocked by *akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ* (42) and when *a* is followed by *ec*, it is debarred by *vṛddhir eci* (33). Effectively, the sūtra means that when *a* is followed by *i u ṛ ḷ*, both the preceding and following vowels are replaced by a *guṇa* vowel.

... The *ā* of *gaṅgā* and *u* of *udaka* together have three morae, whereas the substitute has only two morae. Should the replacement also have three morae? Here the sūtra *taparas tat-kālasya* (26) solves the problem. Since *eṅ* is preceded by *t*, only *e* and *o* having two morae can have the designation *guṇa*. Thus the sūtra *ād guṇaḥ* enjoins the *guṇa* substitute of two morae in place of both the following and preceding vowels. This is the reason why the *guṇa* substitute of two morae will replace even the substituenda having three or more morae.

In the other two illustrations in which *a* is followed by *ṛ* and *ḷ*, the sūtra *uraṇ raparaḥ* (29) is applied. According to Varadarāja, the term *ra* in the *bahuvrīhi* compound *raparaḥ*, which occurs in the sūtra (29), is a pratyāhāra. With the help of the sūtra *ādir antyena sahetā* (4), this pratyāhāra is formed with the repha of the pratyāhāra-sūtra *ha ya va raṭ* as the initial member and *a-kāra* within the *laṇ* pratyāhāra-sūtra as the last member of the *ra*-pratyāhāra since the phoneme a being anunāsika has been designated as it in accordance with the following sutra:

## 28 – upadeśe 'janunāsika it \| 1.3.2

**PP: upadeśe ac anunāsikaḥ it** \|

*upadeśe 'nunāsiko 'jit-saṁjñaḥ syāt \| pratijñānunāsikyāḥ pāṇinīyāḥ \| laṇ sūtra-sthā'varṇena sahoccāryamāṇo repho ralayoḥ saṁjñā \|\|*

**upadeśe**—in case of an *upadeśa* or specification (*hal antyam*); **anunāsikaḥ ac**—a vowel uttered the the association of the nose; **it saṁjñaḥ syāt**—is to be termed as *it* or abolition (whatever is termed as *it* that is liable for *lopa*) \|

(G) In an *upadeśa* (specification), a vowel uttered with the association of the nose is to be termed as *it* (abolition)\|

**pratijñānunāsikyāḥ pāṇinīyāḥ**—the declaration of vowels as nasal might have been illustrated by the triad of sages – Pāṇini , Kātyāyana and Patanñali and such practice is continuing on the basis of regular succession, although no such nasal articulation of the vowels is found in the present time.

**laṇ sūtra-sthā'varṇena sahoccāryamāṇo repho ralayoḥ saṁjñā**—in the aphorism *laṇ* of the *caturdaśa śiva-sūtram* the *a-kāra* comes after the *la-kāra* is termed as *it*. This *a-kāra* is assumed as *anunāsika* by Pāṇini although no such nasal symbol is attached to it. By conjoining the *it* – *a* of the aphorism *laṇ* with the *r* of the aphorism *hayavaraṭ*, the *r-pratyāhāra* is framed so as to ascertain both the letters *r* & *ḷ* (see *laṇ madhyetvit-saṁjñakaḥ*). In the aphorism *laṇ* the final *ṇ* is termed as *it* as per the rule *eṣām antyā itaḥ* and remains as *l a*. Here *a* is not meant for easy articulation (*hakārādiṣvakāraḥ uccāraṇārthaḥ*) and again termed as *it*. So, *laṇ* remains as *l a* and with the coalition of the raṭ of *hayavaraṭ* frames as *raṭ-l a* and subsequently, by deleting the final *it* – *ṭ* & *a* both the *r* & *l* are ascertained.

By adhering this rule (*upadeśo'janunāsika it*), the final vowel of any *upadeśa* (specification), as described in *hal antyam*, is liable to be *it*. Whatever is designated as *it*, that is to be elided by the precept *tasya lopaḥ* \|

(B) When speaking of the Śiva-sūtras, it was mentioned that the vowel in the sūtra *laṇ* is called *it*. Therefore, this vowel may be employed as the final of a pratyāhāra, and the *ra* in the next rule is held to be this pratyāhāra, the name common to the two letters *ra* and *la.*

**Eluc.**: This sūtra enjoins that the nasalized vowel occurring in *upadeśa* is to be designated as *it*. It is known in Pāṇini's grammatical tradition by *pratijñā* (*guru-śiṣya-paramparā*) that a particular vowel in *upadeśa* is nasalized. Here in the pratyāhāra-sūtra *laṇ*, *a-kāra* is accepted as nasalized and hence it is termed *it*. Then the *repha* occurring in the pratyāhāra-sūtra *ha ya va raṭ* combined with *a* within *laṇ* becomes *ra* pratyāhāra which denotes both *repha* and *la-kāra* leaving the intervening *t* since it is *it* by *hal antyam*. The application of this pratyāhāra is observed in the following sūtra.

## 29 – ur aṇ ra-paraḥ \| 1.1.51

**PP: uḥ sthāne aṇ ra-paraḥ** \|

(uḥ \[*of the ṛ*\] aṇ ra-paraḥ \[*followed by ra*\])

*ṛ iti triṁśataḥ saṁjñety uktam \| tat-sthāne yo 'ṇ sa raparaḥ sanneva pravartate \| kṛṣṇarddhiḥ \| tavalkāraḥ \|*

**uḥ**—of the *ṛ* (*uḥ* is the termination of *ṛ* in the 6thcase like *mātṛ* as *mātuḥ*); **aṇ**—letters pertaining to *aṇ* (*a, i, u*); **paraḥ (paro yasmāt)**—which is followed by the *r* (and also the homogeneous *l*) \|

**ṛ iti triṁśataḥ saṁjñā iti uktam**—the *ṛ* is the representative of its thirty modifications i.e. self 18 + 12 to that of the ḷ (see *aṇ udit savarṇasya cā'pratyayaḥ* and *ṛ-ḷ-varṇayor mithaḥ savarṇasya vācyam*); **tat-sthāne yo'ṇ**—in place of that ṛ, the *aṇ* (*a i u* which is united with the *ṛ*); **sa raparaḥ sanneva pravartate**—that *aṇ* transmutes as *ra-para*, (induces to both the rapara as *ar, ir, ur* as well as the *la-para* as *al, il, ul*).

(G) In the alliance of the letters pertaining to *aṇ* with the *ṛ* (as well as the homogeneous *ḷ*), that induces the *aṇ* as *ra-para* (as well as *la-para*)\|

(K) It has been said that the vowel *ṛ* denotes its thirty (homogeneous) forms. The elements denoted by *aṇ* substituting *ṛ* are always followed by *ra* pratyāhāra\|

**kṛṣṇa + ṛddhiḥ = kṛṣṇarddhiḥ**(prosperity of kṛṣṇa); **tava + ḷ-kāraḥ = tavalkāraḥ**\|

## 30 – lopaḥ śākalyasya \| 8.3.19

**PP: a-pūrvayoḥ padāntayoḥ v-yoḥ lopaḥ śākalyasya aśi (saṁhitāyām)** \|

*a-varṇa-pūrvayoḥ padāntayor yavayor lopo vā aśi pare \|*

**lopaḥ**—the elision; **śākalyasya**—according to the sage *Śākalya* (4000 B.C. prior to Pāṇini, the grandson of the sage *Śākala*); **avarṇa-pūrvayoḥ padāntayoḥ yavayoḥ**—of the letters *y, v* ending in a *pada* and preceded by the *a* or *ā* (first *a* or *ā*, and then *y* or *v* ending in a *pada*); **aśipare**—if a letter of *aś* pratyaya follows (first *a* or *ā* then *y* or *v + aś*) \|

In difference to the opinion of *Śākalya* there be the elision of the letters *y* & *v* optionally, if – (i) ends in a *pada*; (ii) preceded by the letter *a* or *ā* and (iii) followed by a letter pertaining to to *aś* pratyāhāra\|

**hare + iha =\> (22) haray + iha (30) =\> hara iha**

In this situation the aphorism *ad guṇaḥ* (27) is applicable for substitution of a *guṇa* (*e*) in both of the places of (*hara + iha* and *hara + iha*). The next aphorism debars the substitution of the *e* (*guṇa*) \|

(B) The form of expression *hara iha* would then appear to furnish occasion for the operation of rule 27, but the rule here following debars this.

**31 – pūrvatrāsiddham**\| 8.2.1

*sapādasaptādhyāyīṁ prati tripādyasiddhā, tripādyām api pūrvaṁ prati paraṁ śāstram asiddham \| hara iha, harayiha \| viṣṇa iha, viṣṇaviha \|*

**pūrvatra**—what comes after (next one); **asiddham**—is refuted; **sapādasaptādhyāyīṁ prati (sapāda sapta adhyāya)**—towards the seven lectures and a chapter (towards the end of the first chapter of 8thlecture); **tripādyasiddhā**—remaining last three chapters are to be refuted; **tripādyām api pūrvaṁ prati paraṁ śāstram asiddham**—in the remaining three chapters each of the previous aphorism refutes the next one \|

The aphorisms described in the last three chapters are refutable to the preceding aphorisms mentioned up to the end of the first chapter in the eighth lecture. Also each of the following (forth coming) aphorisms are refutable to the preceding (surpassed) aphorisms described in the last three chapters of the Aṣṭādhyāyī\|

(K) The triad of the last three quarters of the *Aṣṭādhyayī* is considered inoperative (*asiddha*) with respect to the seven chapters and a quarter, i. e. that which precedes (this sūtra) and in the triad also the following aphorism is considered inoperative with respect to the ones that precede\|

(K) This is a governing (*adhikāra*) sūtra. Its scope extends up to the end of Aṣṭādhyāyī. Through this rule Pāṇini virtually divides his Aṣṭādhyāyī into two parts: Seven chapters and a quarter (sapāda-saptādhyāyī) on the one side and three quarter-chapters (*tripādī*) on the other. The rule says that if an operation is ordered by a rule in the last three quarter-chapters (*tripādī*), it will be deemed not to have taken place in the view of the rule that belongs to the *sapada-saptādhyāyī.*

In the illustrations *hara iha* and *viṣṇa iha*, *y* and *v* have been elided by the preceding sūtra *lopah śākalyasya* Pā. 8.3.19 which belongs to the *tripādī*. The single *guṇa* substitute is made available here by *ad guṇaḥ* Pā. 6.1.87 which belongs to *sapāda-saptādhyāyī*. The operation of the sūtra *lopaḥ śākalyasya* is deemed not to have taken place in view of *ad guṇaḥ* (27). As a consequence, the rule *ad guṇaḥ* does not apply, as it does not recognize the elision (*lopa*) of *y* and *v* in these illustrations. There is no *ac* following *a* because of the suspension of the operation of *lopaḥ śākalyasya* (30) resulting in the zero-replacement of *y* and *v*. The consonants *y* and *v* virtually exist and as a result there is an intervention of either *y* or *v* between *a* and *i*. So *hara iha* and *viṣṇa iha* remain as they are. This is according to the view of *Śākalya.*

According to Pāṇini *y* and *v* are not elided. Hence the forms of both the illustrations would be *harayiha* and *viṣṇaviha*. As such, where there is elision, the forms are *hara iha* and *viṣṇa iha* and where there is no elision of *y* or *v*, the forms are *harayiha* and *viṣṇaviha.*

## 32 – vṛddhir ād aic \| 1.1.1

ād aic ca vṛddhi-saṁjñaḥ syāt \|

**āt-aic ca**—ā t ai au (26, taparastatkālyasya)—*āt* and aic (*ai* and *au*) also—ā, ai, au; **vṛddhi-saṁjñaḥ syāt**—to be termed as vṛddhi \|

(G) The *ā, ai, au* are termed as *vṛddhi*\|

(K) The application of the technical term *vṛddhi* is seen in the following sūtra:

## 33 – vṛddhir eci \| 6.1.88

**PP: āt eci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ vṛddhiḥ saṁhitāyām**

ād eci pare vṛddhir ekādeśaḥ syāt \| guṇāpavādaḥ \| kṛṣṇaikatvam \| gaṅgaughaḥ \| devaiśvaryam \| kṛṣṇautkaṇṭhyam \|

**āt**(5thcase, not *tapara*)—after *a* or *ā*; **eci pare**—if a letter of *ec* follows; **vṛddhir ekādeśaḥ syāt**—a (single) *vṛddhi* (*ā, ai, au*) is to be the substitute for both \|

A *vṛddhi* is substituted for both, if a letters pertaining to *ec* comes after the *a* or *ā*\|

(B) This is a apavāda to the rule 27, *ād guṇaḥ***.**

**guṇāpavādaḥ**—This is a contravention to *guṇa* (a *guṇa* is substituted for both the prior and subsequent, if a letter pertaining to *ac* comes after either of tha *a* or *ā*). In the precept *vṛddhir eci*, a *vṛddhi* is to be the substitute, if a letter pertaining to ec comes after the *a* or *ā*. Here the letters pertaining to *ec* are also coming under the *ec*. For segregation of the limitations confined to *guṇa* and *vṛddhi*, it is to be ascertained that – **(i)**if, *a i u ṛ ḷ* (*ac*) comes after tha *a* or *ā* the substitution is to be a *guṇa*, **(ii)**if *e o ai au* comes after the *a* or *ā* the substitution is to be a *vṛddhi*. For example:

kṛṣṇa + ekatvam --\> kṛṣṇ a + e katvam (17, *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> kṛṣṇ ai katvam --\> kṛṣṇaikatvam (oneness with Kṛṣṇa).

gaṅgā + oghaḥ --\> gaṅg ā + o ghaḥ (17, *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) gaṅg au ghaḥ --\> gaṅgaughaḥ (the torrent of Gaṅgā).

deva + aiśvaryam --\> dev a + ai śvaryam (17, *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> dev ai śvaryam --\> devaiśvaryam (the divinity of God).

kṛṣṇa + autkaṇṭhyam --\> kṛṣṇ a + au tkaṇṭhyam (17, *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) kṛṣṇ au tkaṇṭhyam --\> kṛṣṇautkaṇṭhyam (anxiety for Lord Kṛṣṇa)

(K) *ād guṇaḥ* is a general rule which is blocked by *vṛddhir eci.*

Thus after leaving aside the area where the exception rules have performed their function, the scope of *ādguṇaḥ* is confined to the following situation: The *guṇa* vowel replaces both the preceding *a* and the following vowel denoted by *ik* (*i, u, ṛ* and *ḷ*). There is a paribhāṣa to this effect: *prakalpya cāpavādaviṣayam utsargo 'bhiniviśate* (Abhyankar, 63) "the general rule becomes applicable only after leaving aside the cases where the exception rule has already performed its function".

## 34 – ety edhaty ūṭhsu \| 6.1.89

(eti aidhati ūṭhsu)

**PP. āt eci eti-edhati-ūṭhsu pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ vṛddhiḥ saṁhitāyām** \|

*avarṇād ejādyoretyedhatyor ūṭhi ca pare vṛddhir ekādeśaḥ syāt \| para-rūpa guṇāpavādaḥ \| upaiti \| upaidhate \| praṣṭhauhaḥ \| ejādyoḥ kim? upetaḥ \| mā bhavān predidhat \|*

**a-varṇād**—from (after) the *a* or *ā*; **ejādyoretyedhatyor ūṭhi ca pare**(ec ādyoḥ aiti aidhatyoḥ ūṭhi ca pare)—if the words *aiti* and *aidhati* whose initial letters belong to *aic* and also the word *ūṭh* (whose initial letter is *ū*) follow; **vṛddhir ekādeśaḥ syāt**—a *vṛddhi* is to be the substitute for the concurring vowels \|

(K) When a is followed by the verbal forms of the roots *iṇ* and *edh* beginning with *ec* and also when it is followed by (the substitute) *ūṭh*, let a single *vṛddhi* vowel replace both *a* and the following *ec* or *u*. This is an exception to *eṅi para-rūpam* (38) and *ād guṇaḥ* (27)

(G) After the *a* or *ā*, if the words *eti* & *edhati* initiated by a letter of *ec* (*e*) and also the word *ūṭh* (initiated by the '*ū*') follow, let a *vṛddhi* be the single substitute for both \|

(B) When the verbs *eti* and *edhati*, in those forms which begin with *ec*, follow *a*, and when the substitute *ūṭh* follows it, let *vṛddhi* be the single substitute of the concurring vowels\|

(B) this rule limits 38: *eṅi para-rūpam*, which had previously limited by 27: *ād guṇaḥ* \|

for example: the dhātus *iṇ* and *edh* if terminated in the forms of *eti* and *edhi* respectively with the initial letter pertaining to *ec* (*e*) and come after the *a* or *ā*, a *vṛddhi* is to be substittuted for both \|

upaiti (upa + eti): up a + e ti (17: *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> up ai ti --\> upaiti(he comes near) \|

if the word *ūhaḥ*, beginning with the *ū*, comes after *a* or *ā*, the coalition is to be substituted by a *vṛddhi* in stead of a *guṇa* \| As for example *praṣṭhauhaḥ*(praṣṭha + ūhaḥ): praṣṭh a + ū haḥ (33: *vṛddhir eci*, 17: *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> praṣṭh au haḥ --\> praṣthauhaḥ (a young steer training for plough)

**para-rūpa guṇāpavādaḥ**—this rule is a contradiction to both 38: *eṅi para-rūpam* and 27: *ād guṇaḥ* \|

**ejādyoḥ kim**?—the verbal roots *iṇ* and *edh* if terminated in the forms of *itaḥ* and *ididhat* respectively (instead of *eti* and *edhati* initiated by the *e*) and come after the *a* or *ā*, the coalition is to be substituted by a *guṇa* (instead of a *vṛddhi*) \| such as upetaḥ (upa + itaḥ): up a + i taḥ (27, 17) --\> up e taḥ --\> upetaḥ(approached) \|

**mā bhavān predidhat**—pra + ididhat --\> pr a + i didhat (27, 17) pr e didhat --\> predidhat, mā bhavān predidhat (let not your honour promote)

some other examples:ava + eti --\> avaiti \| pra + eti --\> praiti \| pra + edhate --\> praidhate \| ava + edhate --\> avaidhate \| viśva \| ūhaḥ --\> viśvauhaḥ \|

**I** (vā.) - **(akṣād ūhinyām upasaṅkhyānam**) \| akṣauhiṇī senā \| (akṣād ūhinyām + upasaṅkhyānam)

**akṣāt**—from the word *akṣa* ending in *a*; **ūhinyām**—if the word *ūhinī* (*ūhiṇī*) beginning with *ū* follows; **upasaṅkhyānam**—is to be added (for substitution of a *vṛddhi*)

it is to be added that, if the word *ūhiṇī* comes after the word *akṣa* (ending in *a*), let there be the substitute of a *vṛddhi* for both, instead of a *guṇa*\|

example: akṣa + ūhinī senā --\> akṣ a + ū hinī senā (17) --\> akṣ au hinī senā --\> akṣauhinī senā (246: purva-padāt saṁjñāyāmagaḥ) --\> aksauhiṇī senā (a troop of army consisting of horses, elephants, chariots and cavalry – all together 2,18,700 in number) \|

**II** (vā.) - **(prād ūhoḍhoḍhyeṣaiṣy eṣu) \| prauhaḥ \| prauḍhaḥ \| prauḍhiḥ \| praiṣaḥ \| praiṣyaḥ \|**

(prā ūha ūḍha ūḍhi eṣa eṣya eṣu)

**prād**—from the preposition *pra* ending in *a*; **ūhoḍhoḍhyeṣaiṣy eṣu**—if the words *ūhaḥ*, *ūḍhaḥ* and *ūḍhi* beginning with *ū* and the words *eṣaḥ* and *eṣyaḥ* beginning with *e* follow (it is to be added for substitution of a *vṛddhi*) \|

If the words *ūhaḥ, ūḍhaḥ, ūḍhiḥ, eṣaḥ* & *eṣyaḥ* beginning with *ū* & *e* come after the preposition *pra* (ending in *a*), let there be the substitution of a *vṛddhi* instead of a *guṇa*\|

pra + ūhaḥ --\> pr a + ū haḥ (17: *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> pr au haḥ --\> prauhaḥ(a good argument)

**III** (vā.) - **(ṛte ca tṛtīyā samāse) \| sukhena ṛtaḥ sukhārtaḥ \| tṛtīyeti kim? paramartaḥ** \|

in the instrumental compound (*tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa samāsa),* if the word *ṛta* beginning with *ṛ* (at the beginning of the 2ndmember) follows the *a* (at the end of the 1stmember), a *vṛddhi* is to be the substitute for both, instead of a *guṇa*\|

for example sukhena ṛtaḥ sukhārtaḥ: sukha ṭā su (*supodhātu-pratipadikayoḥ: vibhakti lopaḥ*) sukha ṛta --\> sukh a ṛ ta (*a vṛddhi ār* is to substituted instead of a *guṇa ar*) sukh a ār ta --\> sukhārta, with case (*vibhakti*) termination --\> sukhārtaḥ(affected by joy)

**tṛtīyeti kim? paramartaḥ**—excepting the instrumental compound, if the word *ṛtaḥ* beginning with *ṛ* follows the *a*, a *guṇa* is to be the substitute for both. Such as parama + ṛta --\> param a + ṛ ta (26: *uraṇ ra-paraḥ*) param ar taḥ --\> paramartaḥ(most honest)

**IV** (vā.) - **(pra-vatsatara-kambala-vasanārṇa-daśānām ṛṇe) \| prārṇam, vatsatarārṇam \| kambalārnam \| vasanārṇam \| ṛṇārṇam \| daśārṇam** \|

The word *ṛṇam* (debt) beginning with *ṛ* if comes after the words *pra, vatsatara, kambala, vasana, ṛṇa* and *daśa* (ending in *a*) a *vṛddhi* instead of a *guṇa*, is to be substitute for both\| pra + ṛṇam --\> pr a + ṛ ṇam --\> prārṇam(principal debt)

## 35 – upasargāḥ kriyā-yoge \| 1.4.59

**PP: prādayaḥ upasargaḥ kriyā-yoge \|**

*prādayaḥ kriyā-yoge upasarga-saṁjñāḥ syuḥ \| pra parā apa sam anu ava nis (nir) dus (dur) vi āṅ ni adhi api ati su ud abhi prati pari upa – ete prādayaḥ \|*

**prādayaḥ**—the series of words mentioned in the *pra* and so on; **kriyā-yoge**—if prefixed to the verbs; **upasarga-saṁjñāḥ syuḥ**—are to be called as *upasarga* (prepositions);

The series mentioned in *pra* and so on (*prādigaṇa*), when prefixed to a *dhātu* are to be termed as *upasarga* (prepositions)\|

**pra**—before

**parā**—opposite

**apa**—off

**sam**—with

**anu**—after

**ava**—down

**nis** (**nir**)—out

**dus** (**dur**)—ill

**vi**—apart

**āṅ**—as far as

**ni**—within

**adhi**—over

**api**—also

**ati**—beyond

**su**—well

**ud**—up

**abhi**—opposite

**prati**—towards

**pari**—around

**upa**—next to/near to

*nipātāś cādayo jñeyā prādayastūpasargakāḥ \| dyotakatvātkriyā-yoge lokādavagatā ime \|*

The *ca* and so on (cādigaṇa) are called as *nipāta* (indeclinables) and the *pra* and so on (*prādigaṇa*) are termed as prepositions (*upasarga*). The verbal roots have various meanings in themselves (*anekārthāḥ hi dhātavaḥ*). These prepositions express their own and independent meaning either by intensifying, modifying or altering the sense of the words when they are prefixed with the verbs.

*upasargeṇa dhātv-artho balād anyatra nīyate*  
*prahārāhāra-saṁhāra-vihāra-parihāra-vat \|\|*  
*dhātv-artho vādhate kiścitkaścitamanuvartate*  
*tam eva viśinyaṣṭyanya upasargagatistridhā \|\|*

## 36 – bhū-vādayo dhātavaḥ \| 1.3.1

(bhū vā ādayaḥ dhātavaḥ)

*kriyāvācino bhvādayo dhātu-saṁjñāḥ syuḥ*

**kriyāvācinaḥ**—proclaiming or signifying to *kriyā* or action; **bhvādayaḥ** (bhūś ca vāś ca bhūvau – ādayaḥ)—the verbal roots mentioned in *bhvādigaṇa* (i) *bhū* (to be), (ii) *vā* (to blow); **ādayaḥ**—and so on; **dhātu-saṁjñāḥ syuḥ**—are to be termed as verbal roots;

The verbal roots – *bhvādayaḥ* (*bhū*, *vā* etc.) signifying the sense of an action (*kriyā*) are to be termed as *dhātu* (verbal roots)\|

## 37 – upasargād ṛti dhātau \| 6.1.91

**PP: āt upasargāt ṛti dhātau pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ vṛddhiḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

a-varṇāntād upasargād ṛ-kārādau dhātau pare vṛddhir ekādeśaḥ syāt \| prārcchati \|

**a-varṇāntād upasargād**—from an *upasarga* that ends with the letter *a* or *ā*; **ṛ-kārādau dhātau**—if dhātu initiated by the *ṛ* follows; **vṛddhir ekādeśaḥ syāt**—a *vṛddhi* (instead of *guṇa*) is to be the substitute for both (*guṇāpavādaḥ*);

When a *dhātu* initiated by the *ṛ* follows *upasarga* ending in *a* or *ā*, a *vṛddhi* instead of a *guṇa*, is to be the substitute for both\|

(K) When a *dhātu*, which begins with a short *ṛ*, follows an *upasarga* ending in a short *a*, a single *vṛddhi* replacement comes in place of both, *a* at the end of a preverb and *ṛ* at the beginning of a verbal root\|

pra + ṛcchati\| *vṛddhi ār* is to be the substitute fo both: prārcchati(he goes swiftly)

(K) With the help of *yena vidhistadantasya* Pā. 1.1.72, the expression *āt upasargāt*, given in the *vṛtti*, means "an *upasarga* that ends in the phoneme *a*" \|

Thus the whole sūtra will mean: When a dhātu, which begins with a short ṛ, follows an *upasarga* ending in a short *a*, a single *vṛddhi* replacement comes in place of both, *a* at the end of a preverb and ṛ at the beginning of a verbal root \|

(K) The *ta-para-karaṇa* in *ṛti* is used to block *vṛddhi* in examples such as *upa + ṛkārīyati – uparkārīyati*. In this example *ṛ* is long whereas ṛ qualified

with *ta-para-karaṇa* in the sūtra is short. Hence there is no vṛddhi substitution. Instead guṇa takes place here by *ād guṇaḥ* (27).

## 38 – eṅi para-rūpam \| 6.1.94

**PP: āt upasargāt eṅi dhātau pūrva-parayoḥ ekam para-rūpam (saṁhitāyām)**

*ād upasargād eṅādau dhātau pare para-rūpam ekādeśaḥ syāt \| prejate \| upoṣati \|*

**ād upasargād**—after an *upasarga* that ends with the letter *a* or *ā*; **eṅādau dhātau pare**—if a dhātu initiated by a letter of *eṅ* (*e, o*) follows; **para-rūpam ekādeśaḥ syāt**—the subsequent *e* or *o* (instead of a *vṛddhi*) is to be the substitute for both \|

If a *dhātu* initiated by a letter of *eṅ* comes after an *upasarga* ending in *a* or *ā*, the subsequent (*para-rūpa*) *e* or *o* is substituted for both\|

(K) Let the form of the second be a single substitute for both the preceding and the following phonemes when the root beginning with a vowel denoted by the abbreviatory term *eṅ* follows a preverb ending in a short vowel *a*\|

pra + ejate --\> prejate; upa + oṣate --\> upoṣate; pra + eṣayati --\> preṣayati \|

## 39 – aco 'ntyādi ṭi \| 1.1.64

*acāṁ madhye yo 'ntyaḥ sa ādir yasya taṭ ṭi-saṁjñaṁ syāt \|*

**acāṁ madhye yo 'ntyaḥ**—among the constituting vowels of a word, which one takes place at the end; **sa ādir yasya**—if that final vowel stands proir to a consonant; **tat ṭi-saṁjñaṁ syāt**—that (both of the ending vowel and the consonant) is to be termed as *ṭi* \|

(B) Let the final portion of a word, beginning with the last of the vowels in the word, be called *ṭi*\|

In a word, the vowel that stands as a final letter or prior to a final consonant, that final vowel or the final vowel alongwith the final consonant is to be termed as *ṭi* \|

(K) The use of singular instead of the plural genitive *acām* can be explained by taking the singular genitive *acaḥ* as signifying *jāti* (class) \| That is why *Varadarāja* interprets *acaḥ* as *acāṁ* *madhye* in the *vṛtti* \| When it is construed in the sense of plural, it makes sense to say that the genitive in *acaḥ* is in the sense of *nirdhāraṇa* which means "singling out one from among many" \| ...The sūtra *ādyantavad ekasmin* is purported to make a provision whereby a grammatical operation specific to an initial (*ādi*) or final (*anta*) may be performed on an item consisting of a single phoneme only. In other words, a single element can be treated as its own initial or final element \|

*(śakandhvādiṣu para-rūpaṁ vācyam) \| tacca ṭeḥ \| śakandhuḥ \| karkandhuḥ kulaṭā manīṣā \| ākṛti-gaṇo 'yam \| mārttaṇḍaḥ \|*
