**śakandhvādiṣu**—the words in the *śakandhvādi gaṇa*: *śakandhuḥ, karkandhuḥ, kulaṭā, manīṣā, patañjaliḥ* etc.; **para-rūpa**—substitution is to be the subsequent one (*para-rūpa*)

In the words pertaining to *śakandhvādi-gaṇa* viz. *śakandhuḥ, karkandhuḥ, manīṣā, patañjaliḥ* etc. the substitution is to be the *para-rūpa* (subsequent one) \|

**tacca ṭeḥ**—that substitution includes both the *ṭi* and the subsequent letter;

example: śaka + andhuḥ --\> śak a + a ndhuḥ --\> the ṭi (final *a* of *śaka*) and the subsequent *a* of *andhuḥ* are to be substituted the *para-rūpa* or subsequent *a* --\> śak a ndhuḥ --\> śakandhuḥ \|

**ākṛti-gaṇo 'yam** (ākṛtyā svarūpeṇa kārya-darśanena gaṇyate paricayate iti ākṛti-gaṇaḥ)—words like mārtaṇḍaḥ (mṛta + aṇḍaḥ), samarthaḥ (sama + arthaḥ) etc. other than the series of *śakandhvādi gaṇa* are coming under the same rule when applied to. Such as – *mṛta + aṇḍaḥ* (the Sun) are aggregated by adhering the same rule as started above. This is a suplementary rule to 38: *eṅi para-rūpa* \|

Some other examples *hala + īṣā*--\> (the handle of a plough). lāṅgala + īṣā --\> lāṅgalīṣā (the handle of a plough). kula + aṭā --\> kulaṭā (an unchaste woman). sāra + aṅgaḥ --\> sāraṅgaḥ(an antelope).

## 40 – omāṅoś ca \| 6.1.95

**PP: āt omāṅoḥ ca pūrva-parayoḥ ekaṁ para-rūpam (saṁhitāyām)**

*omi āṅi cāt pare para-rūpam ekādeśaḥ syāt \| śivāyoṁ namaḥ \| śiva ehi iti sthite \|*

**omi āṅi ca āt pare**—if the mystic syllable *om* and the preposition *āṅ* (*o*) come after the *a* or *ā*; **para-rūpam ekādeśaḥ syāt**—the subsequent letter is to be the substitute for both \|

If the mystic syllable *om* and the preposition – *āṅ* (*ā*) come after the *a* or *ā*, the subsequent letter or *para-rūpa* is to be the substitute for both \|

śivāya + om namaḥ --\> śivāyoṁ namaḥ

(K) It is because of the word *para-rūpam* that the word *ekaḥ* has been changed into the neuter gender \|

śiva + ā + ihi --\> śiva + ehi --\> to be continue

(K) grammatical operation in respect of *dhātu* and *upasarga* is considered to be *antaraṅga* (*dhātūpasargayor yat-kāryaṁ tad-antaraṅgam*) \|

## 41 – antādivacca \| 6.1.85

**PP: ekaḥ pūrva-parayoḥ antādivat ca**

*yo 'yam ekādeśaḥ sa pūrvasyāntavat parasyādivat syāt \| śivehi \|*

**yo 'yam ekādeśaḥ**—which of this substitution (the *e*of *ehi* is the coalition of *āṅ* (*ā*) + *i* of *ihi*); **sa pūrvasyāntavat parasyādivat syāt**—that (*e* of *ehi*) is as like as the final letter of the previous word *āṅ* (*ā*), as well as the beginning letter of the following word (*i* of *ihi*);

(B) And when the mystic syllable *om*, or the *upasarga* *āṅ* follows *a* or *ā*, let the single substitute be the form of the subsequent \|

Which of this substitution, that is like the final letter of the previous word as well as the beginning letter of the following word\|

śiva + ā + ihi --\> śiva + ehi --\> śivehi

(K) The word *antādivat* is an indeclinable derived by adding *vati* affix to the compound *antādī*. The analysis of the indeclinable *antādivat* is as follows: *antaś ca ādiś ca antādī antādibhyāṁ tulyam antādivat* \| The words *anta* and *ādi* denote *avayava* "part". Therefore it is natural that they are understood with respect to their *avayavin* "whole" with reference to which they are parts \| In this illustration, the single substitute *e* that has come in place of the *ā* of *āṅ* and the *i* of *ihi* is the whole \| As a result of this substitution, *āṅ* is not available. It is to bring about *āṅtva* in this single substitute that the present sūtra has been brought into service. By virtue of this sūtra, the single substitute here is to be treated as the final of that which precedes in this *ekādeśa* "single substitute". The preceding item in this *ekādeśa*, that is in *e*, is *āṅ*. Now the preceding element *āṅ* is nothing but *ā*, after *ṅ*, being *it* by *hal antyam* (1), has been elided by *tasya lopaḥ* (3). It is single. Hence it is to be treated as its own final. Consequent upon this, the single substitute *e* in this illustration is treated as *āṅ*, which is precisely the condition laid down by the present sūtra for *para-rūpa ekādeśa*. Thus *āṅ* being available, the single substitute in the form of the consequent element, that is *e* in the present case, takes place. The vowel sound *e* replaces both *a* at the end of *śiva* and *e* of *ehi*. This is the procedure by which the finished form *śivehi* illustrated in the *vṛtti* is obtained.

## 42 – akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ \| 6.1.101

**PP: akaḥ savarṇe aci pūrva-parayoh ekaḥ dīrghaḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*akaḥ savarṇe 'ci pare pūrva-parayor dīrgha ekādeśaḥ syāt \| daityāraḥ \| śrīśiḥ \| viṣṇūdayaḥ \| hotṝkāraḥ \|*

**akaḥ**—after *ak*; **savarṇe aci pare**—if a homogeneous vowel follows immediately; **pūrva-parayoḥ**—in place of the prior and the subsequent; **dīrgha ekādeśaḥ syāt**—a long is to be substitute for both \|

(K) If *ak* is followed by a homogeneous *ac*, let a single replacement consisting of a long vowel come in place of the preceding and the following vowels\|

Let a long be the substitute for both, if a homogeneous vowel comes after the *ak*\|

## 43 – eṅaḥ padāntād ati \| 6.1.109

**PP: padāntāt eṅaḥ ati pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrvaḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*padāntād eṅo 'ti pare pūrva-rūpam ekādeśaḥ syāt \| hare 'va \| viṣṇo 'va \|*

**padāntād eṅaḥ**—after a letter of *eṅ* ending in a *pada*; **ati pare**—if the *at* (short *a*) follows; **pūrva-rūpam ekādeśaḥ syāt**—the prior letter is to be substitute for both \|

If the short *a* comes after a letter of the *eṅ* ending in a *pada*, the prior letter (*pūrva-rūpa*) is to be the substitute for both\|

viṣṇo + atra --\> viṣṇo 'tra

## 44 – sarvatra vibhāṣāḥ goḥ \| 6.1.122

**PP: sarvatra padāntasya eṅaḥ goḥ ati vibhāṣā prakṛtya (saṁhitāyām)**

*loke vede caiṅ-antasya gor ati vā prakṛti-bhāvaḥ padānte \| go agram, go 'gram \| eṅ-antasya kim? citragvagram \| padānte kim? goḥ \|*

**sarvatra**(loke vede ca)—both in the Secular and Vedic Sanskrit usage; **eṅ-antasya goḥ**—of the *go* which ends in a letter of *eṅ*; **vibhāṣā**(*na vā iti vibhāṣā*)—(*vā* (*vikalya*)) optionally (sometimes imperative and sometimes forbidden); **prakṛti-bhāvaḥ**—is to be remained invariably unchanged;

Both in the Secular and Scriptural Sanskrit usage, if a short vowel *a* comes after the word *go* ending in *eṅ* (*o*), let the euphony be optionally restricted there\|

go + agram --\> go agram (multitude of cows)

As this aphorism *sarvatra vibhāṣā* *go* is an optional one, we can apply 43: *eṅ padāntād ati* and the result will be go 'gram

**eṅ-antasya kim? citragvagram**—(i) if the word *go* doesn't end in a letter of *eṅ* and intensifying the sense of a cow as *citragu* (a spotted cow or a brindled cow) and (ii) the short vowel *a* (*at*) follows immediately as – *citragu + agram*, the rule *sarvatra vibhāṣā go* is not liable to be executed and the euphony changes into *citragvagram* (rule 15)

## 45 – anekāl śit sarvasya \| 1.1.55

**PP: anekāl śit sarvasya sthāne \|** (aneka al śit sarvasya) iti prāpte \|

**aneka al**—(where the substitution is of) more than one letter; **śit** (*ś it yasya*)—(in a substitution) where *ś* is termed as *it*; **sarvasya**—(the substitution is to be replaced against) all of the constituting in a word;

A substitution containing more than one letter in general and the *śit* (ś + *it*) in particular, if directed against a word in genitive case, that is to be replaced against the whole word\| in case of *rāma + bhis*, the *ais* is directed for substitution against the *bhis*.

(K) the state of being *anekāl* is not determined by an *al* which is termed *it*. We have to first drop *its* and then see whether the substitute is *anekāl* or not \|

## 46 – ṅic ca \| 1.1.53

(ṅ it ca)

ṅid anekālapy antyasyayaiva syāt \|

**ṅ-it**—of which the final *ṅ* is termed as *it*; **aneka-al api**—if that is consisting of more than one letter; **antyasya eva syāt**—that is directed to be replaced against the final letter only;

If a substitution consisting of more than one letter with the final *ṅ* as *it*, that is directed to be replaced against the final letter\|

If the substitute consisting of more than one *al* is *ṅit*, let it replace only the last al of the substituendum (*sthānin*)\|

Example: *avaṅ*is a substitute consisting of more than one letter with the final *ṅ* as an *it* and if directed to be replaced against the word *go* (*g o*), that is to be substituted in place of the final *o*, but not against the entire *pada go* as directed in anekāl *śit sarvasya*\|

## 47 – avaṅ sphoṭāyanasya \| 6.1.133

**PP: eṅaḥ padāntasya goḥ avaṅ sphoṭāyanasya aci vibhāṣā (saṁhitāyām)**

*padānte eṅ antasya gor avaṅ vā syād āci \| gavāgram, go 'gram / padānte kim? gavi \|*

**sphoṭāyana**—a Grammarian of 2900 B.C. and prior to Pāṇini; **padānte**—at the end of a *pada*; eṅ **antasya goḥ**—of the word *go* ending in a letter of *eṅ* i.e. *o*; **avaṅ vā**—let there be optionally substituted the *avaṅ*; **aci**—if a vowel imediately follows;

In difference to the opinion of sage *Sphoṭāyana* – *avaṅ* is optionally substituted against the word *go* ending in *eṅ* (*o*), if a vowel follows\|

(K) Let the substitute *avaṅ* optionally replace the nominal stem *go* ending in *eṅ* (*o*) occurring at the end of a pada when *ac* follows\|

*avaṅ*is substituted against the word *go* that ends in a letter of *eṅ* (*o*), if a vowel immediately follows. As *avaṅ* is called *ṅ-it,* it is directed for substitution against the final letter of *go* by contravening the rule 45: *anekāl śit sarvasya*. Such

as *go + agram* --\> g o + agram (46: *ṅic ca,* 47) --\> g avaṅ + agram (ṅ-it) --\> g ava + agram --\> gava + agram --\> gav a + a gram (42) --\> gavāgram (a multitude of cows) \|

(K) The word *vibhāṣā* is carried over from *sarvatra vibhāṣā goḥ* Pa. 6.1.122. This word means "option". The optional substitution could be had by virtue of this word without mentioning the name of *Sphoṭāyana*. It is, therefore, held that the name of *Sphoṭāyana* has been used to express respect for him.

(K) In the alternative, where the substitute *avaṅ* is not available, a single

*purva-rūpa*replacement takes place by *eṅaḥ padāntādati* (43). In *go + agra* a single replacement in the form of the preceding *o*, which occurs at the end of the nominal stem *go*, comes in place of both the preceding *o* and the short *a* that follows. So we also have the form *go 'gram*. Thus we have three forms including the instance of *prakṛtibhāva*: 1. *goagram* (*prakṛtibhava*) 2. *gavāgram* and 3. *go 'gram* (2 and 3 in the absence of *prakṛtibhāva*).

The purpose of enunciating *padāntād* (pada-final *eṅ*) in the sūtra is to preclude the substitution of *avaṅ* in place of the vowel *o* at the end of the nominal stem *go* when it is not a *pada*. Take, for example, *gavi* which has the locative nominal ending: *go + ṅi* --\> *go + i*. At this stage, the present sūtra would have been applicable, had the expression pada-final not been included in the body of the sūtra.

There is one more point to be taken note of here. The term vibhāṣā which is carried over into this sūtra from *sarvatra vibhāṣā goḥ* Pa. 6.1.122 is interpreted as *vyavasthita-vibhāṣā* which means an option that does not apply universally in all the instances of the sūtra in which it occurs. It applies necessarily in some cases and does not apply at all in the other cases. It is this interpretation of *vyavasthita-vibhāṣā* that explains the compulsory substitution of *avaṅ* in *gavākṣa* (gavām aksi iva --\> go aksi a--\> g avaṅ akṣa --\> g ava akṣa —\> gavākṣa). The replacement of avaṅ is not optional here but obligatory. To achieve this end the carried over term *vibhāṣā* has been interpreted here as *vyavasthita-vibhāṣā.*

## 48 – indre ca \| 6.1.124

**PP: eṅaḥ goḥ indre ca avaṅ (saṁhitāyām)**

goravaṅ syād indre \| gavendraḥ \|

In place of the final *o* of the word *go* let there be the substitution of *avaṅ* if the word *indraḥ* follows\|

(K) When the nominal stem *indra* follows, let the pada-final (*eṅ*) of *go* be replaced by avaṅ \|

Substitution of *avaṅ* is mandatory in the euphony of *go + indraḥ* \|

(K) This sūtra is an exception to *avaṅ sphoṭāyanasya* (47). The substitute *avaṅ* was optionally available there. Here it is necessarily available \|

## 49 – dūrād dhūte ca \| 8.2.84

(dūrāt hūte ca)

**PP: durāt hūte ca vākyasya ṭeḥ plutaḥ vā**

dūrāt sambodhane vākyasya ṭeḥ pluto vā syāt \|

**dūrāt**—from a distant place; **sambodhane (hūte)**(āhvānaṁ hūtaṁ sambodhanam)—by calling or addressing a person; **vākyasya ṭeḥ pluto vā**—the final vowel (*ṭi*) of a sentence is substituted by its prolated modification optionally.

In calling or addressing a person from a distant place, in place of the final vowel (*ṭi*) of a sentence the substitution of prolated modification (*pluta*) is optional \|

(B) And in calling to person from a distance, the substitution of the prolated modification of the *ṭi* is optional\|

(K) In calling (a person) from a distance, let the *ṭi* of an utterance be optionally replaced by a prolated vowel\|

For example *āgaccha kṛṣṇa3*! (come Kṛṣṇa!). The substitution of prolated modification (*pluta*) in the final *a* (*ṭi*) of the expression – *āgaccha kṛṣṇa3* (*kṛṣṇ a3*) is to be executed on the basis there of – (i) *āgaccha kṛṣṇa3* is to be addressed from a distant place with an acute accent (*udātta*) and (ii) the vocative one (*kṛṣṇa3*) is to be uttered at the end of the sentence.

The substitution of prolated modification in place of the final vowel (*ṭi*) of a sentence is restricted, if the addressee is uttered first *kṛṣṇa āgaccha*, also with an grave accent (*anudātta*) like *āgaccha kṛṣṇa* as it does not come under the purview of the above rule.

(K) Although the word *udāttah* is also carried over into this sūtra, *Varadarāja* does not include it in the *vṛtti*, considering it to be of little use for an elementay book like the LKS.

(K) The indeclinable *vā* has been used by Varadarāja in the *vṛtti* since Patañjali has made optional the whole section on *pluta.*

(K) It is important to note here that if the name of the person who is being called is used at the end of an utterance, then only the *ṭi* of that utterance is optionally replaced by a prolated vowel. The *ṭi* of an utterance means the last syllable of the relevant word used at the end of that utterance. If this is not the case, then the replacement in place of *ṭi* does not take place even though the utterance is used to call somebody from a distance \|

## 50 – pluta-pragṛhyā aci nityam \| 6.1.125

**PP: pluta-pragṛhyā aci nityam prakṛtyā (saṁhitāyām)** \|

ete 'ci prakṛtyā syuḥ \| āgaccha kṛṣṇa3 atra gauś carati \|

**ete**—these two *pluta* and *pragṛhya* (excepted vowel); **aci**—if a vowel follows; **nityam**—are to remain invariably unaltered (are not liable to the rules of euphony);

(A vowel in certain occasion, is not liable to the rules of euphony and allowed to be written and pronounced separately, that is called as excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*) – see next rule),

If a vowel follows a prolated vowel (*pluta*) or an excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*), that remains invariably unaltered (not liable to be applied by the rules of euphony)\|

*āgaccha kṛṣṇa3 atra gauś carati*(come kṛṣṇa! the cow is grazing here). In this expression, the *ṭi* (final *a3*) of the word kṛṣṇa3 is in prolated modfication, and not liable to the rules of euphony even if a vowel follows (*kṛṣṇ a + a tra*) and is allowed to be written and pronounced separately. This is *apavāda* to 42 \|

(K) The term prakṛtyā is carried over from 6.1.115. The word means "staying in the original form" \|

The use of the word *nityam* in the present sūtra has a purpose. In *vayū + atra*, the long *ū* is *pragṛhya* by *īdūded dvi-vacanaṁ pragṛhyam* (51). In this illustration, because of the use of the word *nityam*, *prakṛti-bhava* is not optional with the shortening of *ik* as is prescribed by *iko ’savarṇe śakalyasya hravaś ca* (59). So the use of the word *nityam* ensures *prakṛti-bhava*. In addition, it blocks the short vowel substitution in place of a long vowel denoted by *ik* as enjoined by (59).

## 51 – īd-ūd-ed dvi-vacanaṁ pragṛhyam \| 1.1.11

*īdūded-antaṁ dvi-vacanaṁ pragṛhyaṁ syāt \| harī etau \| viṣṇū imau \| gaṅge amū \|*

**īd-ūd-ed-antaṁ dvi-vacanaṁ** (*īt ūt* or *et* *antam dvi-vacanam*)—a dual case termination (*vibhakti*) ending in the long form viz. *īt, ūt* or *et* (*dīrgha ī, dīrgha ū* or *e*) – (taparas tat-kālasya); **pragṛhyam**—is to be termed as excepted vowel;

A dual case-termination ending in the long form viz. *ī, ū* or *e* is termed as *pragṛhya* or excepted vowel\|

*kavī āgacchataḥ, ṛtu atitau (two seasons surpassed), bālike adhiyāte (two girls are reading)\|*

(K) The word *īd-ūdet* is a *samāhāra-dvandva* compound which is analysed as *īc ca ūc ca ec ca* \| The *ta-para-karaṇa* is purported to remove doubt, if there is any, about their being long. The compound īd-ūd-et qualifies the word *dvivacanam* \|

(K) Remaining in the original state is also called *prakṛti-bhāva* \|

## 52 – adaso māt \|1.1.12

**PP: adasaḥ māt īd-ūt pragṛhyam** \|

*asmāt parāv īdūtau pragṛhyau staḥ \| amī īśāḥ \| rāma-kṛṣṇāvamū āsāte \| māt kim? amuke 'tra \|*

**adasaḥ**(*asmāt*)—after the case termination of the pronoun *adas* (that); **(māt) parau īt ūto**—in the case inflection where the *ī* and *ū* come after *m*, such as *amī* and *amū*; **pragṛhyau staḥ**—these (*ī* and *ū*) are to be termed as *pragṛhya*;

In the case termination of the pronoun *adas*, the long *ī* and *ū* are termed as *pragṛhya* when they come after the *m*\|

(K) (The inflectional endings) *ī* and *ū* which follow the phoneme *m* of *adas* are designated as *pragṛhya*\|

As the final letters of the inflected words *amī* and *amū* possess the title *pragṛhya*, the euphony remains unaltered even if a vowel follows as per the rule 50: pluta-pragṛhyā aci nityam (If a vowel follows a prolated vowel (*pluta*) or an excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*), that remains invariably unaltered (not liable to be applied by the rules of euphony)).

amī + īśāḥ --\> amī īśāḥ, rāma-kṛṣṇau amū āsāte\|

**māt kim? amuke 'tra**—if the inflections of the pronoun *adas* is diversified in the same sense as *amuke*, the rule 43: *eṅaḥ padāntād ati* is applied as the e of *amuke* is not same sense as *pragṛhya*, because the *e* of *amuke* doesn't follow the *m* and the coalition takes place as *amuke + atra* --\> *amuke 'tra* (those two are here).

(K) The term *māt* ends in fifth case-ending which means "what follows". The use of *a* in *māt* is to facilitate its articulation.

(K) The present sūtra is purported only for *adas* in the masculine.

## 53 – cādayo 'sattve \| 1.4.57

*adravyārthāś cādayo nipātāḥ syuḥ \|*

**cādayaḥ**(ca ādayaḥ)—the indeclinables (*avyaya*) mentioned in *cādigaṇa* viz. *ca, vā, paśu* etc.; **asattve**(*adravyārthāḥ*)—not a *dravya*, which doesn't mean to an animal or number, (*sattvam iti dravyam ucyate, liṅga-saṅkhyānvitam dravyam*); **nipātāḥ syuḥ**—are termed as *nipāta* (*na pātayati ātmānam* – which is not liable for change in any inflections);

The indeclinables in the *cādi-gaṇa* viz. *ca, va, paśu* etc. are termed as *nipāta* (a particle), if they don't denote the meanings of a *dravya* (an animate)\|

(K) Let the members of the list headed by *ca* be designated as *nipāta* when they do not denote a substance \|

For example: *lodhaṁ nayanti paśu manyamānāḥ* in this sentence the word *paśu* when denotes the meaning of an inanimate – "goodness" (*samyak*) there, it is to be termed as *nipāta*. But in the occasion where the word *paśu* reveals to an "animal" (*paśu*), it is not to be termed as *nipāta.*

## 54 – prādayaḥ \| 1.4.58

**PP: asattve prādayaḥ nipātāḥ**

ete 'pi tathā syuḥ \|

The *upasarga* (prepositions) in the *prādigaṇa* viz. *pra, parā, āṅ* etc. are termed as *nipāta* (a particle) if they don't denote the meanings of an animate\|

vi paśya– in this sentence the *vi* (*upasarga*) when denotes the meaning of an inanimate – *vi* (apart), it is termed *nipāta*. But in the occasion when it reveals an animal – *vi* (bird), it is not to be termed as *nipāta* \|

## 55 – nipāta ekājanāṅ \| 1.1.14

**PP: anāṅ ekāc nipātaḥ pragṛhyaḥ**

(nipāta eka ac anāṅ)

*eko 'c nipāta āṅ-varjaḥ pragṛhyaḥ syāt \| i indraḥ \| u umeśaḥ \| vākya-smaraṇayor aṅit; ā evaṁ nu manyase \| ā evaṁ kila tat \| anyatra ṅit – īṣaduṣṇam oṣṇam \|*

**eko 'c nipāta**—a single lettered vowel (*a, ā, i, ī, u, ū, e, o, ai, au*), consisting in the series of *nipāta* (indeclinables in *cādigaṇa*); **āṅ-varjaḥ**—excluding the *āṅ* or *ā*; **pragṛhyaḥ syāt**—is to be termed as Excepted Vowel;

Excluding the *āṅ* (*ā*), a single lettered vowel pertaining to the series of *cādigaṇa* (indeclinables), is to be termed as *nipāta* and subsequently called as an Excepted Vowel or *pragṛhya*\|

i + indraḥ, u + umeśa– here both the indeclinables *i* and *u* are the single lettered vowels consisting in the series of *cādigaṇa* and termed as *pragṛhya*. According to the rule 50: *pluta-pragṛhyā 'ca nityam*, the euphony is not to be undertaken in these places by the application of the precept 42 and invariably remains unaltered as *i indraḥ* (ho *indra*) and *u umeśaḥ* (ho Lord of *Umā*) \|

In the *cādigaṇa* there are eleven numbers of single lettered vowels which

are called as *nipāta* and subsequently termed as *pragṛhya* if applied in the following sense – **(i)** the *a* in the sense of *ākṣepārtham* (criticism), **(ii)** the *ā* in the sense of *vākya-smaraṇārtham* (recollecting a sentence), **(iii)** the *i* in the sense of *vismayārtham* (astonishment), **(iv)** the *i-dvaya, u-dvaya, e, o, ai, au* in the sense of *sambodhanārtham* (vocation) and **(v)** the *āṅ* or *ā* in the sense of *alpārtham* (insufficiency).

**vākya-smaraṇayor aṅit**—the *āṅ* (*ā*) found in both *cādigaṇa* (*avyaya*) and *prādigaṇa* (*upasarga*) with dual meaning – (i) in the sense of *vākya-smaraṇārtham* (recollecting a sentense) and (ii) in the sense of alpārtham (insufficiency). When the *a* is applied in the sense of *vākya-smaraṇārtham*, then it is termed as *pragṛhya* and the euphony is restricted there even if a vowel follows. Such as – ā evaṁ nu manyase \| *ā evaṁ kila tat* \| In this situation, the application of the precept 33: *vṛddhir eci* is prohibited and the coalition remains unchanged \|

**anyatra ṅit**—when the *āṅ* (*ā*) is applied in the sense of *alpārtham* (insufficiency), then it is not termed as ragṛhya and the euphony is undertaken as *īṣad ūṣṇam* --\> here, the word *īṣad* is employed in the sense of *alpārtham* (*īṣad-arthe*) – ā + uṣṇam (*ādguṇaḥ*) --\> oṣṇam (*luke worm*).

## 56 – ot \| 1.1.15

**PP: ot nipātaḥ pragṛhyaḥ**

odanto nipātaḥ pragṛhyaḥ syāt \| aho īśāḥ \|

**od-anto**(ot antaḥ)—which ends in *o*; nipātaḥ—the indeclinables consisting in the series of *ca* etc.; **pragṛhyaḥ syāt**—is termed as excepted;

The indeclinable consisting in the series of the *cādi-gaṇa* if ending in *o*, that is termed as an excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*)\| *o, āho, ūtāho, ho, aho*etc. āho + īśāḥ --\> āho īśāḥ

## 57 – sambuddhau śākalyasyetāv anārṣe \| 1.1.16

**PP: sambuddhau ot anārṣe itau pragṛhyaḥ śākalyasya**

*sambuddhi-nimittaka okāro vā pragṛhyo 'vaidika itau pare \| viṣṇo iti, viṣṇa iti, viṣṇav iti \|*

**śākalyasya sambuddhi-nimittaka okāraḥ**—in difference to the opinion of sage *śakalya*, the o that stands as a final letter in a vocation; **pragṛhyaḥ vā**—is optionally termed as *pragṛhya*; **anārṣe (na ārṣe)=avaidike (na vaidike)**—not in the holy texts (non-scriptual), so to say in the secular or common Sanskrit usage;

**itau pare**—if the *iti* follows;

In difference to the opinion of the sage *śakalya*, in the secular usage, the final *o* in a vocation (*sambudhi*) shall optionally be termed a excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*), if the indeclinable *iti* follows \| viṣṇo iti

## 58 – maya uño vo vā \| 8.3.33

**PP: mayaḥ uñaḥ vaḥ vā aci saṁhitāyām**

*mayaḥ parasya uño vo vā syād aci \| kimvuktam \| kimu uktam*

**mayaḥ parasya uñaḥ**—in place of the *uñ* or *u* that comes after a letter of *may*; **vaḥ-vā syad aci**—substitution of the *v* is optional, if a vowel follows;

If the *uñ* (*u*) comes after a letter of *may* the substitution of *v* in place of the *uñ* (*u*) is to be the optional, if a letter of *ac* follows\|

kim + uñ + uktam --\> kim + uñ + uktam --\> kim + u + uktam --\> in this situation the *uñ* (*u*) comes after *m* and thereafter the vowel *u* of *uktam* comes after the *uñ* \| According to the present rule, the *v* is to be substitute in the place of *uñ* (*u*), as kim + u + uktam --\> kim + v + uktam --\> kimvuktam

As the substitution of v in place of the *uñ* (*u*) is optional, in the euphony of *kim + u + uktam*, the *u* coming after the *kim* is termed as *pragṛhya* according to the rule 55 and the euphony remains unchanged as *kim* *u uktam* or *kimu uktam* \|

## 59 – iko 'savarṇe 'ci śākalyasya hrasvaś ca \| 6.1.127

**PP: padāntasya ikaḥ asarvarṇe aci hrasvaḥ śākalyasya ca (saṁhitāyām)**

*padāntā iko hrasvā vā syur asavarṇe 'ci \| hrasva-vidhi-sāmarthyān na svarasandhiḥ \| cakri atra, cakraytra \| padāntā kim? gauryau \|*

**padāntā ikaḥ**—in place of a long vowel of the *ik* ending in a *pada*; **hrasvā vā syuḥ**—a short vowel is to be optionally substituted there; **asavarṇe aci ?pare**—in a non-homogenised vowel (other then the *ī, ū, ṝ*) follows

In place of a (long) vowel pertaining to the *ik* ends in a *pada*, a short vowel is to be optionally substituted if a non-homogenic vowel follows \|

cakrī + atra --\> cakri atra

As per norms of the present precept *hrasva-vidhi-sāmyarthānna svara-sandhi* futher coalition is restricted after shortening of a long vowel even if a vowel follows \| So we cannot apply rule 15: *iko yaṇ aci* and keep *cakri atra* as it is \| On the other hand, in absence of the present optional precept, the euphony – *cakī + atra* is to be changed as *cakryatra* \|

**padāntā kim?**—the *iko 'savarṇe śakalyasya hrasvaś ca* is only to be operated for shortening a long vowel situated at the end of an inflected word, but not in the middle of a *pada*. For example in the dual form of the nominative and accusative case termination of the word *gaurī*as – *gauri* + *auṭ* --\> gaur + ī (15) gauryau(two gauris)

gaurī + āha --\> gauri āha, gauryāha

na samāse (vā.) \| vāpyaśvaḥ \|

The option of shortening the a vowel is restricted in a *samasa.*

vāpyām + aśvaḥ --\> vipī + aśvaḥ --\> vāpyaśvaḥ

(K) It has already been pointed out at (50) that because of the use of the word *nitya* in that sūtra, this aphorism does not apply where *prakṛti-bhava* has scope on account of an incidence of *pragṛhya* designation \|

## 60 – aco ra-hābhyāṁ dve \| 8.4.46

**PP: acaḥ ra-hābhyāṁ dve yaraḥ vā (saṁhitāyām)**

*acaḥ parābhyāṁ repha-ha-kārābhyāṁ parasya yaro dve vā staḥ \| gauryyau \| (na samāse) \| vāpyaśvaḥ \|*

**acaḥ parābhyāṁ repha-ha-kārābhyāṁ**—(from) the consonant *r* or *h* comming after a vowel; **(repha-ha-kārābhyāṁ) parasya yaraḥ**—after that if comes a letter of the *yar*; **dve vā staḥ**—substitution of a reduplicate is to be the optional;

A letter of *yar* is reduplicated optionally, if that comes after the consonant – *r* or *h* followed by a vowel\|

A consonant denoted by the abbreviatory term *yar*, immediately occurring after *r* or *h* which again are preceded by a vowel, is optionally reduplicated \|

gaury + au, in this situation (i) the *r* comes after the the vowel *au*, (ii) the *y* a letter of the *yar* just follows the *r* \| Hence the y is to optionally reduplicated as g au r y y + au --\> gaur y y + au --\> gauryyau

## 61 – ṛty akaḥ \| 6.1.128

**PP: ṛti padāntasya akaḥ hrasvaḥ śakalyasya (saṁhitāyām)**

ṛti pare padāntā akaḥ prāgvad vā \| brahma ṛṣiḥ, brahmarṣiḥ \| padāntāḥ kim? ārcchat \|

**ṛti pare**—if the short *ṛ* follows; **padāntā akaḥ**—a (long) letter of *ak* ending in a *pada*; **prāgvad vā**—the option of shortening the vowel is employed as before;

The option of shortening a vowel is employed against a long form of *ak* if that is ending in a *pada* and a short *ṛ* follows immediately \|

brahmā + ṛṣi --\> brahma ṛṣi or brahmarṣi ity ac sandhiḥ

## hal-sandhi-prakaraṇam

## 62 – s-toḥ ś-cunā ś-cuḥ \| 8.4.40

PP: **s-toḥ ś-cunā ś-cuḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*sa-kāra-ta-vargayoḥ śa-kāra-ca-vargābhyāṁ yoge śa-kāra-ca-vargau staḥ \| rāmaś śete \| rāmaś cinoti \| sac cit \| śārṅgiñ jaya \|*

**stoḥ** (*s + tu* = *stu,* terminated in 6thcase as *stoḥ*) = **sa-kāra-ta-vargayoḥ**—in place of these dentals *s* and *tu* (*ta-varga t, th, d, dh, n*);

**ścunā**(ś + cu, terminated in the 3rdcase as *ścunā*) = **śa-kāra-ca-vargābhyāṁ yoge**—if, conjoined with the palatal *ś* and *cu* (*ca-varga*);

**ścu:**(*ś + cu = ścu*, terminated in the 1stcase as *ścu:*) = **śa-kāra-ca-vargau staḥ**—the substitution is to be the palatal *ś* and *cu*;

In the coalition of the dental group of letters (*s, t, th, d, dh, n*) with the palatal group of letters (*ś, c, ch, j, jh, ñ*), the substitution is to be the palatal in place of the dentals\|

**This sūtra is equivalent for HNV**:

102\. da-tau para-varṇau la-ca-ṭa-vargeṣu nityam

103\. taś ca śe

111\. ja-jha-ña-śa-rāmeṣu ña-rāmaḥ

134\. ca-chayoḥ śa-rāmaḥ

241\. ta-vargasya ca-vargaś ca-varga-yoge

248\. sasya śaś ca-varga-yoge

As per the precept 23: *yathā saṅkhyam anudeśaḥ samānām*, the substitutions of the palatal in place of the dentals are to be inflicted in order of enumeration as both these groups consist of six letters each. Such as – in place of the dentals the palatals are to be substitute respectively \|

rāmas + śete --\> rāmaś śete \|

rāmas + cinoti --\> rāmaś cinoti \|

sat + cit --\> sac cit \|

śārṅgin + jaya --\> śārṅgiñjaya

In this connection it is to note that:

(i) the dentals (*s, t, th, d, dh, n*) are irrespectively liable for conjunction with the palatal (*ś, c, ch, j, jh, ñ*)

(ii) the substitution is to be made only in order of enumeration

(iii) the conjunction is to be made of **either** sides as *s, t, th, d, dh, n + ś, c, ch, j, jh, ñ* **or** *ś, c, ch, j, jh, ñ + s, t, th, d, dh, n* except the next rule "*śāt*" \|

(K) We have put *saṁhitāyām* in the parenthesis only to show that it is carried over here and in the relevant rules of *sandhi* that follow. This word means "when extreme proximity is intended" and this meaning should be taken as understood wherever this word occurs in the parentheses \|

(K)...the usages like *acsandhi*, *actva*, *jaśtva* etc. need explanation and justification. To account for these usages, it may, however, be said that *Pāṇini* has dropped a hint in this regard in the sūtra *alpāctaram* (985). In this sutra *c* is in contact with *t* but *t* is not replaced by *c* in accordance with the present sūtra. The implication is that at some places this replacement is not effected in order that these consonants may stand clearly distinguished (*spaṣṭapratipattyartham*) \|

## 63 – śāt \| 8.4.44

**PP: śat toḥ na (saṁhitāyām)**

*śāt parasya ta-vargasya cutvaṁ na syāt \| viśnaḥ \| praśnaḥ \|*

**śāt parasya ta-vargasya**—after the *ś*, in place of a letter of *ta-varga*;

**cutvaṁ na syāt**—the *cu* (*c, ch, j, jh, ñ*) is not to be substitute;

After the palatal *ś* if a letter of *ta-varga* (t, th, d, dh or n) follows, the palatal – *ca-varga* (*c, ch, j, jh, n*) is not to be the substitute of the dental – *ta-varga* (*t, th, d, dh, n*)\| viś + na --\> viśna \|

(K) ... This very sūtra also additionally implies that, if dental stops follow the palatal ś, they will not be replaced by the palatal stops since *Pāṇini* himself has used it. This implication does away with the need of this sūtra. Jinendrabuddhi in his commentary on the *Kaśikā* on this sūtra (*śāt*) says that, although it is possible to do away with this sūtra, it has been enunciated so that the less intelligent may not find it difficul to understand the implication of the sūtra *praśne cāsannakāle* Pā. 3.2.117.

## 64 – ṣṭunā ṣṭuḥ \| 8.4.41

**PP: stoḥ ṣṭunā ṣṭuḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*stoḥ ṣṭunā yoge ṣṭuḥ syāt \| rāmaṣṣaṣṭhaḥ \| rāmaṣṭīkate \| peṣṭā \| taṭṭīkā \| cakriṇḍhaukase \|*

**stoḥ**(*sa-kāra-tavargayoḥ*)—in place of the dentals (*s* and *tu*); **ṣṭunā yoge** (*ṣa-kāra-ṭa-vargābhyāṁ*)—if, conjoined with the cerebral (*ṣ* and *ṭu*); **ṣṭuḥ syāt**(*ṣa-kāra-ṭa-vargau staḥ*)—the substitution is to be the cerebral (*ṣ* and *ṭu*)

Let the dental sibilant *s* and the dental stops (*tu*) when in contact with the retroflex sibilant *ṣ* and retroflex stops (*ṭu*) be replaced by the latter\|

As per rule 23: *yathā saṅkhyam*..., the substitution of the cerebral in place of the dentals are to be operated in order of enumeration as both these groups consist of six letters each \|

rāmas + ṣaṣṭhaḥ --\> rāmaṣṣaṣṭaḥ; rāmaḥ + ṭīkate --\> rāmaṣṭīkate \| garutmān + ḍīyate --\> garutmāṇḍīyate \|

## 65 – na padāntāṭ ṭor anām \| 8.4.42

**PP: na padāntāṭ ṭoḥ anām s-toḥ ṣ-ṭuḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*padāntāt ṭa-vargāt parasyānāmaḥ stoḥ ṣṭur na syāt \| ṣaṭ santaḥ \| ṣaṭ te \| padāntāt kim?*

**padāntāt ṭoḥ**(*padāntāt ṭa-vargāt*)—after a letter of *ṭa-varga* ending in an inflected word; **parasya stoḥ**—of the following *s* and *tu*; **anāmaḥ**—except the affix *nām* (that begings with *n* of *tu*); **ṣṭuḥ na syāt**—the undergoing change is preverted;

(B) After *ṭu* final in a *pada* the change of a dental to a cerebral, except in the case of the affix *nām*, shall not take place\|

(G) If a dental *s* or *tu* comes after a cerebral *ṭu* ending in an inflected word, the undergoing cerebral change in place of a dental is prevented. But not if the affix – *nām* follows\|

(K) With the exception of *nām* (the plural sixth inflectional ending), let not the dental *s* and stops following the pada-final cerebral stops be replaced by the cerebral *ṣ* and stops\|

(ṣaḍ) ṣaṭ + santaḥ --\> ṣaṭsantaḥ

(G) The substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is to be restricted in case of a *padānta* only \|

īṭṭe \| ṭoḥ kim? sarpiṣṭamam \|

**The substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is to be restricted if a letter of** ***s*** **or** ***tu*** **comes after a letter of** ***ṭu*** **(excluding the** ***ṣ*****).**In case of the *ṣ* followed by a letter of *s* or *tu*, the substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is not to be restricted. Such as *sarpiṣ + tamam --\> sarpiṣṭamam*\|

**vā. anām navati nagarīṇām iti vācyam**\|

It is expounded by sage *Kātyāyana* that the affix – *nām* and the words – *navati* and *nagarī* (those begin with *n* of *tu*) if come after the *ṭu* ending in an inflected word, the substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is to be imperative\|

(K) The exception of *nām, navati* and *nagari* should be stated in the sutra na padantattoranam (65)\|

ṣaṇṇavatiḥ \| ṣaṇṇagaryyaḥ \|

## 66 – toḥ ṣi \| 8.4.43

**PP: toḥ ṣi na ṣṭutvam (saṁhitāyām)**

(ta-vargasya ṣa-kāre pare) na ṣṭutvam \| sanṣaṣṭhaḥ \|

The substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is to be prevented, if the cerebral – *ṣ* comes after a *tu*\|

(K) Let not the dental stops followed by the cerebral sibilant *ṣ* be replaced by the cerebral stops\| san + ṣaṣṭhaḥ --\> sanṣaṣṭhaḥ\|

(K) If we take into account the carried over words, then the meaning of the *sūtra* will be a little different from the one given in the translation: When the cerebral sibilant *ṣ* follows, the pada-final dental stops are not replaced by the cerebral stops \|

## 67 – jhalāṁ jaśo 'nte \| 8.2.39

**PP: padantasya ante jhalām jaśaḥ**

padānte jhalāṁ jaśaḥ syuḥ \| vāgīśaḥ \|

A letter of *jaś* is to be the substitute of a letter of *jhal* ending in an inflected word\|

vāk + īśaḥ --\> vāgīśaḥ \| manāk + hasati --\> manāghasati \| cit + ananda --\> cidananda \| madhuliṭ + guñjatu --\> madhuliḍguñjatu \| kukubh + īśaḥ --\> kukubīśaḥ \|

## 68 – yaro 'nunāsike 'nunāsiko vā \| 8.4.45

HNV: 97. **hariveṇau hariveṇur vā**

**PP: padāntasya yaraḥ anunāsike anunāsikaḥ vā (saṁhitāyām)** \|

*yaraḥ padāntasyānunāsike pare 'nunāsiko vā (syāt) \| etanmurāriḥ, etad murāriḥ \|*

**padantasya yaraḥ**—in place of a letter pertaining to *yar* ending in an inflected word; **anunāsike pare**—if a nasal (*ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n*) folllows; **anunāsiko vā syāt**—a nasal may be substituted there;

In place of a letter consisting in *yar* and ending in an inflected word, a nasal is substituted optionally, if a nasal follows immediately \|

(K) Let a nasal stop optionally substitute a pada-final consonant denoted by the abbreviatory term *yar* before (an initial) nasal stop (of the following *pada*)\|

etad + murāriḥ --\> etan murāriḥ or etad murāriḥ\|

(G) r, ś, ṣ, s don't have their nasal modifications \| As result there will be no changes \|

ṣaḍ + māsāḥ --\> ṣaṇmāsāḥ, ṣaḍmāsāḥ \| dhig + mūrkham --\> dhiṅ mūrkham, dhig mūrkham \|

**vā. pratyaye bhāṣāyāṁ nityam**\| tanmātram \| cinmayam \|

HNV: 279. **nityaṁ hariveṇu-vidhiḥ pratyaya-hariveṇau**

**pratyaye**—if an affix (initiated by a nasal) comes after the *yar*; **bhāṣāyāṁ**—in the usage of the secular language; **nityam**—(the substitution of a nasal) is mandatory;

In the usage of the secular language, the substitution of a nasal in place of a letter of *yar* is obligatory, if an affix (initiated by a nasal) follows\|

(K) In the domain of spoken language, if an affix beginning with a nasal stop follows, then the pada-final *yar* is to be necessarily replaced by a nasal stop \| tat + mātram --\> tad + mātram --\> tanmātram\|

## 69 – tor li \| 8.4.60

HNV: 109. **le la-rāma eva** (*na-rāma* at the end of a *viṣṇupada* becomes *la-rāma* when *la-rāma* follows)

HNV: 102. **da-tau para-varṇau la-ca-ṭa-vargeṣu nityam** (*da-rāma* and *ta-rāma* always change to the following *varṇa* when *la-rāma, ca-varga*, or *ṭa-varga* follows)

**PP: toḥ li para-savarṇaḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*ta-vargasya la-kāre pare para-savarṇaḥ (syāt) \| tallayaḥ \| vidvalṁ(plus half-moon) likhati \| nasyānunāsiko laḥ \|*

**la-kāre pare**—if, an initial *l* (of an inflected word follows); **para-savarṇaḥ**(syāt)—the substitution is to be the next homogeneous one;

The substitution is to be the *para-sa-varṇa* (next homogeneous) in place of a letter of *tu*, if a word initiated by the *l* follows\| tat + layaḥ --\> tad + layaḥ --\> tallayḥ

vidvān + likhati --\> vidvāl (nasal) likhati (*na-kārasyā 'nunāsiko la-kāraḥ*)

## 70 – udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya \| 8.4.61

HNV: 552. **udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ sasya haraḥ** (after *ud*, the *s* of *sthā* and *stanbh\[u\]* is deleted)

**PP: udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya savarṇaḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*udaḥ parayoḥ sthāstambhoḥ pūrva-savarṇaḥ (syāt) \|*

**udaḥ parayoḥ**—from the *ud* (preposition *ud* is modified as *udaḥ* in the 5thcase); **sthā-stambhoḥ**—in the place of the verbal roots *sthā* (to raise, stay) and *stambh* (to hold, support); **pūrva-savarṇaḥ (syāt)**—the substitution is to be the preceding homogeneous (*pūrva-savarṇa,* same class to the prior);

After the preposition *ud*, in place of the *dhātus* – *sthā*, *stambh* the substitution is to be the *pūrva-savarṇa*\|

(K) Let the verbal roots *sthā* and *stambh* which follow the preverb *ud* be replaced by (a phoneme) homogeneous with the preceding one\|

Now the questions arise – **(i)**in which direction of the *ud* the verbal roots *sthā, sthambh* are to be placed (either prior to or next to the *ud*)? **(ii)**to whom the substitution of the preceding homogeneous is to be executed, either to the prior or to the next one? To apprehend the ambiguities, the next explanatory rule (71) provokes...

(K) The *vṛtti* of the sūtra contains the word *parayoḥ* in it by virtue of the *paribhāṣā sūtra tasmādityuttarasya* (71). The sūtra says: Whatever is enjoined, by using the ablative inflectional ending, is to be performed on the item which follows that which is expressed with an ablative inflectional ending. The preverb *ud* ends in the ablative. Therefore the grammatical operation has to be performed on an element which follows the preverb *ud* \|

(K) One point is worth noting with regard to the *anuvṛtti* of the word *savarṇa* into the present sūtra. This comes from the sūtra *anusvārasya yayi para-savarṇaḥ* Pā. 8.4.58. The word *para-savarṇaḥ*, that occurs in this sūtra, is a compound word. Only a part of the compound *para-savarṇa*, that is *savarṇa* is carried over into the sūtra although generally the whole compound word should have been carried over. *Jñānendra Sarasvatī* explains (TB, I, p. 127) why only a part of the compound word has been carried over. He says that the carrying over of a particular word into the following sūtra or sūtras is based on the marking of a particular word with a *svarita* accent {cf. *svaritenādhikāraḥ* (Pa. 1.3.11)}. Although there is no marking of accents available nowadays, it is to be determined or understood on the basis of the convention that is followed in the *Pāṇinian Śāstra* (*pratijñāsvaritāḥ pāṇiniyāḥ*, see *Kāśikā* on Pā. 1.3.11). This is known from the tradition that the word *savarṇa* which is a member of the compound word *para-savarṇaḥ* is understood to have been marked with a *svarita* accent. Hence only the word *savarṇa* from Pā. 8.4.58 is carried over into the present sūtra.

In order to arrive at a cogent and complete meaning of the present sūtra, it is necessary to take into account what the following two paribhāṣā sūtras have to say:

## 71 – tasmād ity uttarasya \| 1.1.67

HNV: 155. (vṛtti) kārya-pūrve pañcamī syāt

**PP: tasmād iti nirdiṣṭe uttarasya**

*pañcamīnirdeśena kriyamāṇaṁ kāryaṁ varṇāntareṇāvyavahitasya parasya jñeyam \|*

**pañcamīnirdeśena**(*tasmāt*, from that)—in the direction of 5thcase inflection; **kriyamāṇaṁ kāryaṁ**—the operation is to be executed; **varṇāntareṇa-avyavahitasya parasya**—in between the gapless following room; **jñeyam**—to be apprehended;

It is for apprehension that, in the indication of 5thcase inflection, the operation is executed in between the gapless following room \|

(B) An operation caused by the exhibition of a term in the 5thcase, shall be understood to enjoin the substitution of something in the room of that which immediately follows the word denoted by the term\|

(K) When a term is stated in the 5thcase (*tasmāt*), the grammatical operation directed by it should be performed on that which follows the term without the intervention of a single phoneme \|

According to this explanatory Rule (*paribhāṣā sūtra*), it is for apprehension that, in the rule *udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya,* the preposition *ud* is indicated as *udaḥ* (in the 5thcase inflection) and shall remain unchanged. Hence, any changes are to be executed in the gapless next room i.e. in place of the verbal roots *sthā* and *stambh* \|

In the occurrence of *ud + sthānam* and *ud + stambhanam* the changes are to be executed in the gapless letter of the next rooms viz. *sthānam* and *stambhanam*. According to the precept *alo 'ntyasya,* the *pūrva-savarṇa* is to be executed at the ending letters of the verbal roots – *sthā* & *stambh* as these two are inflected in the dual sixth case-affix *os* (*sthā-stambh + os*) in the precept *udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya*. But the next rule debars the application of the precept *alo 'ntasya* as....

(K) ... we are now in a position to interpret the sūtra in the following way : If some grammatical operation is directed by a term expressed in the ablative case, that grammatical operation is to be performed on that element which immediately follows the term stated in the ablative case. In the sūtra *udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya* (70), the preverb *ud* is stated in the ablative case. This means that the grammatical operation of substituting a homogeneous phoneme directed by the term *ud* should be performed on an item which immediately follows the term *ud*. In other words, the verbal roots *sthā* and *stambh* are the *loci* where this grammatical operation is to be performed. Now the last phoneme of either of these verbal roots is to be replaced by a homogeneous phoneme in accordance with the sūtra *alo 'ntyasya* (21) but the following sūtra, which is an exception to 21, makes provision for the substitution to come in place of the initial phoneme of these verbal roots \|

## 72 – ādeḥ parasya \| 1.1.54

No Equivalent in Harināmāmṛta-vyākaraṇa

**PP: parasya ādeḥ alaḥ sthāne**

*parasya yad vihitaṁ tat tasyāder bodhyam \| iti sasya thaḥ \|*

**parasya yad vihitam**—(after the word in the 5thcase inflection) the indication which is laid down for the operation in the next room; **tat tasyādeḥ bodhyam**—that is to be inflicted in the initial letter of the next word;

After a word in the 5thcase inflection, when the direction is laid down for the operation in the next room, that is to be inflicted against the initial letter of the next word\|

(B) That which is enjoined to come in the room of what follows is to be understood as coming in the room only of the first letter thereof\|

(K) A substitute that has been enjoined to replace that which follows, should replace the initial phoneme of the following\|

In the occurence of *ud + sthānam* and *ud + sthambham* the changes are to be executed in the gapless initial letter *s* (not that of the final letter) of the next rooms viz. *sthānam* and *stambhanam*. Hence, the *s* is to be replaced by the *pūrva-savarṇa* *d* of the *ud* \|

As the *s* and *d* are of the same dental class (*lṛtulasānāṁ dantāḥ*), now the question arises, which one of the letters of the *tu* is to be the befitting letter to the *s*? According to the *guṇa-kṛta sādṛśya* of the precept *sthāne 'antaratamaḥ* the *th* is the befitting letter to be inflicted in place of the *s* (*iti sasya thaḥ*) by applying the external effort – *vivāra, śvāsa, aghoṣa & mahā-prāṇa*. So the conjunctions *ud + sthānam* and *ud + stambhanam* are to be changed in to *ud + ththānam* and *ud + thtambhanam* \|

(K)... The term *ādeḥ* qualifies *alaḥ* \| The sūtra thus means that a grammatical operation, which is directed to be performed with respect to the following, will be performed on the initial phoneme of that which immediately follows the term stated in the ablative case \|

(K) With the help of these two *paribhāṣā sūtras*, the meaning of the injunctive rule (*vidhi-sūtra*) *udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya* (70) becomes clear. It helps in identifying the locus where the grammatical operation prescribed by this rule is to be performed. That locus is the initial phoneme of the term which follows the term expressed in the ablative case. In this *vidhi-sūtra*, the term which is stated in the ablative is *ud* and the following term is either *sthā* or *stambh*. The initial phoneme which is common to both these verbal roots is the dental sibilant *s*. This dental *s* has to be replaced by a phoneme which is homogeneous with the dental stop *d*, occurring at the end of the preverb *ud*. The sūtra *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ* (17) becomes applicable and ensures that the most similar substitute will replace the dental sibilant *s* of either of the verbal roots *sthā* and *stambh*. This *paribhāṣā* sutra is an essential determinin factor since there are five phonemes homogeneous with the preceding *d: t, th, d, dh, n*. In order to find out which is the most similar to *s*, we have to look at the table 3 on page 18. The consonantal sounds *th* and *s* are most similar since both of them have four external efforts: *vivāra, śvāsa, aghoṣa and mahāprāṇa*. In this way, taking into account the external efforts, we come to the conclusion that it is the dental *th*, which will replace the dental sibilant *s* of the verbal roots *sthā* or *stambh*. The grammatical procedure of the substitution of *th* for *s* may be demonstrated as follows: 1. *ud + sthānam* and 2. *ud + stambhanam*. Here with the help of the *paribhāṣā sūtras* *tasmād ity uttarasya* (71) and *ādeḥ parasya* (72), the initial *s* of both the words *sthānam* and *stambhanam* is replaced by *th*. Then we arrive at the following stage: 1. *ud + ththānam* 2. *ud + thtambhanam*. At this stage, the following sūtra ensues:

## 73 – jharo jhari savarṇe \| 8.4.65

HNV: 125. **viṣṇujanād viṣṇudāsasyādarśanaṁ sa-varge viṣṇudāse** (a *viṣṇudāsa* that comes after a *viṣṇujana* optionally disappears when a *viṣṇudāsa* of the same *varga* follows)

**PP: halaḥ jharaḥ lopaḥ anyatarasyām savarṇe jhari (saṁhitāyām)**

*halaḥ parasya jharo vā lopaḥ savarṇe jhari \|*

*alt. reading*: halaḥ parasya jharo lopo vā syāt savarṇe jhari \|

**halaḥ parasya**—after a consonant; **jharaḥ**—of the letter pertaining to *jhar*; **lopo vā syāt**—is optionally elided; **savarṇe jhari**—if a homogeneous letter of the *jhar* follows;

After a consonant, a letter of the *jhar* is optionally elided, if a homogeneous letter of the *jhar* follows \|

ud + ththānam --\> ud + thānam \| ud + thtambhanam --\> ud + tambhanam \|

In the absence of this optional precept *jharo jhari savarṇe*, the conjunction remains unchanged as *ud + ththānam* and *ud + thtambhanam*. In both the situations the following rule comes in force as.

## 74 – khari ca \| 8.4.55

HNV: 98. **yādava-mātre harikamalam** (a *viṣṇudāsa* becomes a *harikamala* when any *yādava* follows)

**PP: khari ca jhalāṁ caraḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

khari (pare) jhalāṁ caraḥ syuḥ \|

**khari pare**—if a letter pertaining to *khar* follows; **jhalam**—in place of the letters pertaining to *jhal*; **caraḥ syuḥ**—the substitution is to be a letter pertaining to *car*;

In place of a letter of *jhal*, the substitution is to be *car*, if *khar* follows\|

*ity udo dasya taḥ \| utthānam \| uttambhanam \|*

**ity udo dasya taḥ**(*iti-udaḥ dasya taḥ*)—in place of the *d* of *ud* (a letter of *jhal*) in both the instances *ud + thānam* and *ud + tambhanam*, the substitution is to be the *t* (a letter of *car*), as the *th* (a letter of *khar*) follows immediately. So the conjugation changes as *ut + thānam* and *ut + tambhanam* --\> *utthānam* and *uttambhanam \|*

In the absence of the optinal precept *jharo jhari savarṇe*, the conjunction remains unchanged as *udththānam* and *udthtambhanam*. In this situation the rule *khari ca* comes in force as *ut + ththānam --\> uttthānam* and *ut + thtambhanam --\> utthambhanam* \|

other examples: ud + sthāpayati --\> utthāpayati, uttthāpayati \|

ud + sthāpakaḥ --\> utthāpakaḥ, uttthāpakaḥ\|

(K) In the illustrations under discussion, the dental stop *d* at the end of *ud* is a consonant denote by *jhal* and it is followed by *th* in the first illustration and by *t* in the second. Both are denoted by *khar*. Therefore *d* is replaced by *t* which is denoted by *car*. The *paribhāṣā* sūtra *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ* (17) helps determine the most similar *car*-substitute on the basis of the place of articulation. This replaces a similar *jhal*-consonant and we get *t* in place of *d* \|

## 75 – jhayo ho 'nyatarasyām \| 8.4.62

HNV: 101. **ho harighoṣaḥ** (*ha-rāma* that comes after a *viṣṇudāsa* can optionally become a *harighoṣa*)

**PP: jhayaḥ haḥ anyatarasyām pūrvasya savarṇaḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*jhayaḥ parasya hasya vā pūrva-savarṇaḥ (syāt) \| nādasya ghoṣasya saṁvārasya mahāprāṇasya tādṛśo varga-caturthaḥ \| vāgghariḥ, vāg-hariḥ \|*

**jhayaḥ parasya hasya**—in place of the *h*, if that comes after a letter of *jhay*; **anyatarasyām**(*vā*)—optionally; **pūrva-savarṇaḥ**(syāt)—the substitute is to be the prior homogeneous;

In place of the *h*, if that comes after a letter of *jhay*, the *pūrva-savarṇa* \[harighoṣaḥ\]is substituted optionally\|

(K) Let a phoneme homogeneous with the preceding one optionally replace the phoneme *h* which follows the consonants denoted by *jhay* \|

vāk + hariḥ (67: *jhalaṁ jaśo 'nte*) --\> vāg + hariḥ \| As both the *h* and *g* are homogeneous (*akuhavisarjanīyānāṁ kaṇṭhaḥ*), in place of the *h*, a substitute of the equal class to the prior *g* is to be operated \|

**nādasya ghoṣasya saṁvārasya mahāprāṇasya tādṛśo varga-caturthaḥ**—according to the guṇa-kṛta sādṛśya of the rule 17: *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ,* each of the fourth letters of the class consonants eg. *jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh* are the befitting substitutes to be inflicted in place of the *h* by applying the appropriate external effort *nāda, ghoṣa, saṁvāra, mahāprāṇa*. Such as the *gha*, on behalf of the *ga* (a letter of *jhay*), is the befitting substitute of the *h* (out of *k, kh, g, gh*).

So in the conjunction of *vāg + hariḥ* the appropriate *gh* is to be inflicted in place of the *h* of the word *hariḥ* as *vāg + ghariḥ* (the lion of speech or eloquence).

In the absence of this optional rule *jhayo ho 'nyatarasyām*, the conjunction of *vāg + hariḥ* remains unchanged as *vāghariḥ*. Accordingly, other fouth letters (jh, ḍh, dh, bh), on behalf of a letter of *jhay*, out of each class consonants are the befitting substitute of the *h.*

ac + hīnam (67, *jhalaṁ jaśo 'nte*) --\> aj + hīnam (75, *jhayo ho 'nyatarasyām*) --\> aj + jhīnam --\> ajjhīnam or achīnam (devoid of a vowel)

madhuliṭ + hasati --\> (67, *jhalaṁ jaśo 'nte*) --\> madhuliḍ + hasati (75, *jhayo ho 'nyatarasyām*) --\> madhuliḍḍhasati or madhuliḍhasati

(K) The consonantal phoneme *h* has these external efforts: *nāda, ghoṣa, saṁvāra* and *mahāprāṇa*. The most similar substitute phonem among the consonants denoted by *jhay* is the fourth class-consonant since the fourth class-consonant also has the same properties (external efforts). This comes to mean that the homogeneous fourth class-consonant optionally replaces the phoneme *h* which follows the *jhay* consonant without the intervention of any other phoneme \|

## 76 – śaś cho 'ṭi \| 8.4.63

HNV: 99. **tataḥ śaś cho vā** (*śa-rāma* that comes after a *viṣṇudāsa* can optionally become *cha-rāma*); ex. ap + śāyī --\> (98, 99) ap-chāyī or ap-śāyī

**PP: jhayaḥ śaḥ chaḥ anyatarasyām aṭi (saṁhitāyām)**

*jhayaḥ parasya śasya cho vā 'ṭi \| "tad + śiva" ity atra dasya ścutvena jakāre kṛte khari ceti jakārasya cakāraḥ \| tacchivaḥ, tacśivaḥ \|*

**padāntād jhayaḥ**—after a letter pertaining to *jhay*, that ends in an inflicted word; **parasya śasya**—in place of the following *ś*; **cho vā**(*syād*)—the *ch* is to be the optional substitute; **aṭi**—if a letter of *aṭ* follows the *ś*;

(K) Let *ch* optionally replace *ś* which follows *jhay* \[ending in a *pada*\]and which is followed by *aṭ*\|

In the next room, the *ch* is to be the optional substitute of the *ś* if the *ś* precedes to a letter of *aṭ* and comes after *jhay* ending in a *pada*\| in place of the initial *ś* of the subsequent word, the substitution of *ch* is to be the optional on the grounds of **(i)**if the ś comes after a letter of *jhay* that is ending in the preceding *pada* and **(ii)**if any letter of aṭ follows th *ś.*

tad + śivaḥ (62: *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) --\> taj + śivaḥ (74: *khari ca*) --\> tac + śivaḥ, in this situation the *ś* of the word *śivaḥ* follows the *c* of the word *tac* (a letter of *jhay*) and the *i* (a letter of *aṭ*) follows the ś of the word *śivaḥ*,

thus fulfil the norms of the precept *śaś cho 'ṭi*. So, by the infliction of *ch* in place of *ś*, the conjunction concludes as *tacchivaḥ* \|

tad + śivaḥ (62: *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) --\> taj + śivaḥ (74: *khari ca*) --\> tac + śivaḥ (76: *śaś cho 'ṭi*) --\> tacchivaḥ\|

in the absence of this optional rule the conjunction remains as *tacśivaḥ* \|

vāk + śūraḥ --\> vāk chūraḥ or vāk śūraḥ\|

**vā. chatvam amīti vācyam \| tacchlokena** (*chatvam ami iti vācyam*)

**chatvam**—the substitution of *ch* in place of the *ś*; **ami**—if followed by a letter of *am pratyāhāra* (extended upto the *am* instead of *aṭ*); iti **vācyam**—(in place of the initial *ś* of a subsequent word, the *ch* is to be substitute) as expounded by the sage *Kātyāyana*;

After a letter of *jhay* ending in a *pada*, the *ch* is to be the \[optional\]substitute in place of the initial *ś* followed by a letter of *am pratyāhāra*\|

(K) It should be stated that the substitute *ch* should **optionally** replace the palatal sibilant *ś* (even when) followed by *am*\|

tad + ślokena (62: *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) --\> taj + ślokena (74: *khari ca*) --\> tac + ślokena (76 vā. chatvam amīti vācyam) --\> tacchlokena (otherwise *tacślokena*)

(K) The present *sūtra* is subsequent to the other two sutras. Hence it is *asiddha* with respect to them. In other words, if the present rule applies first, it is deemed not to have performed any grammatical function with respect to *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ* and *khari ca*. The sūtra *khari ca* is *asiddha* so far as *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ* is concerned. Since *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ* is not deemed to be suspended in respect of the other two sūtras, it applies first of all \|

## 77 – mo 'nusvāraḥ \| 8.3.23

**PP: maḥ padasya anusvāraḥ hali (saṁhitāyām)**

māntasya padasyānusvāro hali \| hariṁ vande \|

**māntasya padasya**—in place of the *m* ending in an inflicted word; **anusvāraḥ (syād) hali**—the substitution is to be the *anusvāraḥ*; **hali**—if a consonant follows;

In place of the *m* ending in an inflected word, the substitution is to be the *anusvāra* if a consonant follows\|

(K) The word maḥ has the sixth case-ending \|

HNV: 230. **aviṣṇupadāntasya nasya masya ca viṣṇucakraṁ vaiṣṇave**

(naḥ ca apadāntasya jhali)

*nasya masya cāpadāntasya jhaly anusvāraḥ (syāt) \| yaśāṁsi \| ākraṁsyate \| jhali kim? manyate \|*

**nasya masya cāpadāntasya**—also in place of both the *n* and *m* in an apadānta; **jhali**—if a letter of *jhal* follows; **anusvāraḥ**(syāt)—the *anusvāra* is inflicted there;

Also in place of the *n* & *m* of an *apadānta* the *anusvāra* is to be inflicted, if a letter of *jhal* follows \|

yaśāṁsi \| ākraṁsyate \|

## 79 – anusvārasya yayi para-savarṇaḥ \| 8.4.58

HNV: 114. viṣṇucakrasya hariveṇur viṣṇuvarge, viṣṇupadāntasya tu vā

HNV: 115. ya-va-leṣu sa-viṣṇucāpa-para-rūpaṁ ca manyante

spaṣṭam \| śāntaḥ \|

**anusvārasya**—in place of the *anusvāra*; **yayi**—if a letter of *yay* follows; **para-savarṇaḥ**—the substitution is to be the subsequent homogeneous;

In place of the *anusvāra*, the substitution is to be the *para-savarṇa* (subsequent homogeneous), if a letter of *yay* follows \|

This rule is applicable in case of an *apadānta* \| ... *anusvāra* has to be replaced by a nasal letter of *yay* \| it is note that, out of the semi vowels, the *r* does not possess its nasal modification and if comes after the *anusvāra*, the question of replacement of a nasal form in place of the *anusvāra* doesn't arise. Although the *y, v, l* posessess the nasal modifications, they are rarely found in an *apadānta* placed after *anusvāra.*

śām taḥ (76: śaś cho 'ṭi) --\> śāṁ taḥ (79, 17) śāntaḥ\|

(K) *Jñānendra Sarasvatī* and *Nāgeśa* say that the term *yay* in the present sūtra is only for the sake of clarity. Even if it had not been used in the sūtra, it would have not made any difference. There is no *anusvāra* before *ac*. When the phonemes denoted by *śal* follow the *anusvāra*, no replacement homogeneous with *śal*-consonants is possible (TB, I, p. 130) as these do not have any homogeneous counterparts... Thus even without the term *yay*, the present sūtra could have served the purpose very well \|

## 80 – vā padāntasya \| 8.4.59

HNV: 114. **viṣṇucakrasya hariveṇur viṣṇuvarge, viṣṇupadāntasya tu vā**

*padāntāsya 'nusvārasya yayi pare para-savarṇo vā syāt \| tvaṅ karoṣi, tvaṁ karoṣi \|*

**padāntāsya anusvārasya**—in place of the *anusvāra* ending in an inflicted word; **yayi pare**—if a letter of *yay* follows; **para-savarṇo vā syāt**—the option of substitution is to be the next homogeneous;

In place of the *anusvāra*, ending in an inflected word, the option of substitution is to be the *para-savarṇa* (subsequent homogeneous), if a letter of *yay* follows \|

This rule is for *padānta* \| tvaṅ karoṣi, tvaṁ karoṣi \|

No Equivalent in Harināmāmṛta-vyākaraṇa

**ma-pare ha-kāre pare**(*m pare yasya sa maparaḥ* – to whom the *m* follows)—if the *h* follows being followed by the *m* (*h m* i.e. *m paraka h*); **masya mo vā**(*syāt*)—the *m* ending in a *pada* remains as *m* optionally (the substitution of *anusvāra* in place of the *m* is restricted optionally);

kim hmalayati or kiṁ hmalayati

**vā. ya-va-la-pare ya-va-lā vā \|**

**ya-va-la-pare**—(if the *h* follows) being followed by the semi vowel *y, v* or *l* (*h + y* or *v* or *l*); **ya-va-lā vā**—(in place of the *m* ending in a *pada*) the option of substitution is to be the *y* or *v* or *l* (being modified as the nasal)

According to this supplementary Rule, if follows the *h* being followed by the semi vowels *y, v* or *l*, the final *m* ending in a *pada* is substituted by the nasal modification (the substitution of the *anusvāra* in place of the final *m* is restricted)\|

kiy(with half-moon)hyaḥ, kiṁ hyaḥ \| kiv(with half-moon)hvalayati, kiṁ hvalayati \| kil(with half-moon)hlādayati, kiṁ hlādayati \|

ik$$$ma,+ ÷:--\> ik$$$ya.\*M ÷:orik$$$ma,+ ÷:--\> ik$M ÷:

No Equivalent in Harināmāmṛta-vyākaraṇa

## 84 – ādy-antau ṭa-k-itau \| 1.1.46

HNV 167. (vṛtti) **ṭid-āgamaḥ para-sambandhī, kid-āgamaḥ pūrva-sambandhī**

ṭ-it-k-itau yasyoktau tasya kramād ādyantāvayavau staḥ \|

**ādyantau**(*ādiś ca antaś ca iti ādyantau*)—the beginning (initial) and the ending (final); **ṭ-it-k-itau** (*ṭaś ca kaś ca iti ṭakau \| ṭakau itau yayoḥ tau ṭakitau*)—of which the final *ṭ* and *k* are to be termed as *it* and *lopa*; **ṭ-it-k-itau yasya uktau** (ṭ + it = ṭit, k + it = kit)—of which the ṭit or kit (elision *ṭ* or *k*) is enunciated; **tasya kramād ādyantāvayavau staḥ**—that is to be the *ādi* (initial) and *anta* (final) *avayava* (augment) respectively;

Of whatsoever the *ṭit* and *kit* are enunciated, that is to be the initial and final *āgama* (augment) respectively. So to say, the enunciation of an *āgama* (augment) is to be distinguished by the indicator *ṭit* and *kit* \|

liḍ + su --\> liṭtsu or liṭsu\|

## 85 – ṅ-ṇoḥ kuk-ṭuk śari \| 8.3.28

HNV: 130. **śaurau ṇa-ṅābhyāṁ ṭa-kau veti vaktavyam** (*ṭ* and *k* can optionally be inserted after *ṇ* and *ṅ* respectively when a *śauri* follows) \| sugaṇ + śaṅkaraḥ --\> (130) sugaṇ śaṅkaraḥ or sugaṇṭ śaṅkaraḥ

**PP: ṅṇoḥ kuk-ṭuk śari vā (saṁhitāyām)**

(K) vā staḥ \|

(G) ṅ-kāra-ṇa-kārayoḥ kuk-ṭuk-āvāgamau vā staḥ śari \|

**ṅ-kāra-ṇa-kārayoḥ**—of the *ṅ* and *ṇ* ending in a *pada*; **kuk-ṭukau āgamau vā staḥ**—*kuk* and *ṭuk* are to be the optional augments;

Of the *ṅa* and *ṇa* ending in a *pada*, the option of infliction is to be the *k*\[*uk*\] and *ṭ*\[*uk*\] as an *āgama* respectively, if a letter of *śar* follows \|

**vā. cayo dvitīyāḥ śari pauṣkarasāder iti vācyam \| prāṅkh ṣaṣṭhaḥ, prāṅk ṣaṣṭhaḥ, prāṅ ṣaṣṭhaḥ \| sugaṇṭh ṣaṣṭhaḥ sugaṇṭ sugaṇ ṣaṣṭhaḥ** \|

**cayaḥ**—in place of a first letter of the class consonants (*cay*); **dvitīyāḥ**—the (homogeneous) 2ndletter of the class consonants (*kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th*) is to be substituted; **pauṣkarasāder iti vācyam**—as expounded by the succession of *Pauṣkaras* (3100 B.C. prior to *Pāṇini*);

According to the predication of the succession of *Pauṣkaras*, in place of a letter of *cay* ending in a *pada*, the 2ndletter belongs to that class is to be the substitute, if a letter of *śar* follows\|

prāṅk + ṣaṣṭhaḥ --\> praṅkh ṣaṣṭhaḥ\| in the absence of the *vārtika* the euphony remains as *prāṅkṣaṣṭhaḥ* \|

## 86 – ḍaḥ si dhuṭ \| 8.3.29

HNV: 129. **sa-rāme ṭa-nābhyāṁ tug veti vaktavyam** (*t*\[*uk*\] can optionally be inserted after *ṭ* or *n* when *s* follows) \| ṣaṭ + sādhavaḥ --\> (129) ṣaṭ sādhavaḥ or ṣaṭt sādhavaḥ(“six sādhus”)

me: there is some difference between sūtras of *Jīva* and *Pāṇini*. *Jīva G*. introduces *āgama* *t*\[*uk*\] which connects with preceding word and *Pāṇini* uses *āgama* *dh*\[*uṭ*\] which connects with following word \|

**PP: ḍaḥ si dhuṭ vā (saṁhitayām)**

ḍāt parasya sasya dhuḍ vā syāt \|

**ḍāt parasya sasya**—of the *s*, that comes after the *ḍ*; **dhuṭ-vā syāt**—*dh*\[*uṭ*\] is to be the optional *āgama*, (*adeśaḥ śatrvat, āgama mitravat*);

*dh*\[*uṭ*\] is to be the optional *āgama* (augment) of the *s* if that comes after the *ḍ* \|

(RNS) Augment *dh*\[*uṭ*\] is optionally introduced to a *pada* which begins with *s* and occurs after a *pada* ending in *ḍ*, provided the context is that of close proximity between sounds\| in this rule *ḍaḥ* is in the 5thcase, *si* in the 7thcase, *dh*\[*uṭ*\] is in the 1stcase \|

Now the question arises, where the *dh*\[*uṭ*\] is to be fixed? As the *ḍaḥ* is in the ablative case termination, according to the precept 71: *tasmād ity uttarasya*, the execution of *dh*\[*uṭ*\] is to be operated in the next room i.e. with the *s*. As regards to the *si*, it is in the locative case termination and according to the precept 16: *tasminn iti nirdiṣṭe pūrvasya* the execution of *dh*\[*uṭ*\] is to be operated in the preceding room i.e. with the *ḍ*. In this contravening situation, the ablative case indicator *ḍ*(*ḍaḥ*) remains in force rather than the locative case indicator *s*(*si*) and it is to be converted into genitive case (*saḥ*) with reference to the maxim *ubhaya-nirdeśe pañcamī-nirdeśo balīyān* (Abhyankar, 70) \|

(RNS) The locative specification of *si*, when transformed into its genitive *saḥ* will make the ablative specification comparatively more powerful \| If this is what was intended, why did *Pāṇini* make this specification with *si* in the locative? This he did for economy (*lāghavārthaḥ*) \|

(K) The term *si* (*s*=half and *i*=one mora =\> one and a half) contains one and a half mora, whereas the term *saḥ* (*s*=half, *a*=1 and *visarga*=half =\> two) has two morae (*śīti saptamī-nirdeśas tu* *lāghavārthaḥ*, TB, I, p. 134)

## 87 – naś ca \| 8.3.30

HNV: 129. **sa-rāme ṭa-nābhyāṁ tug veti vaktavyam** (*t*\[*uk*\] can optionally be inserted after *ṭ* or *n* when *s* follows) \| bhagavān sādhuḥ --\> (129) bhagavān sādhuḥ or bhagavānt sādhuḥ \|

me: there is some difference between sūtras of *Jīva* and *Pāṇini*. *Jīva G*. introduces *āgama* *t*\[*uk*\] which connects with preceding word and *Pāṇini* uses *āgama* *dh*\[*uṭ*\] which connects with following word \|

**PP: naḥ ca si dhuṭ vā (saṁhitāyām)**

*nāntāt parasya sasya dhuḍ vā \| san tsaḥ, sansaḥ \|*

**na-antāt**—(ablative case) from the *n* ending in an inflected word; parasya sasya—of the following *s*; **dhuṭ vā**(*syāt*)—*dhuṭ* is to be the optional *āgama*;

*dhuṭ*is to be the optional *āgama* of the *s*, when the *s* comes after the *n* ending in an inflected word \|

san + saḥ --\> san + dhuṭ + saḥ --\> san + dhsaḥ --\> (*khari ca*) san + tsaḥ --\> santsaḥ(he is a good man) \| in absence of the optional precept *naśca*, form will be sansaḥ\|

## 88 – śi t\[uk\] \| 8.3.31 \|

HNV: 112. **śe cānto vā** (When *śa-rāma* follows, *na-rāma* at the end of a *viṣṇupada* optionally becomes *ña-rāma* followed by *ca-rāma* (that is to say it optionally becomes *ñc*).)

**PP: śi naḥ padasya vā t\[uk\] (saṁhitāyām)**

*padāntasya nasya śe pare tug vā (syāt) \| sañchambhuḥ, sañcchambhuḥ, sañcśambhuḥ, sañśambhuḥ \|*

**padāntasya nasya**—of the *n* ending in an inflicted word; **śe pare**—if the ś follows; **tug vā**(*syāt*)—the option of *tuk* is to the augment there;

Let there be the *t*\[*uk*\] as an optional *āgama* of the *n* ending in an inflected word, if the *ś* follows immediately \|

san + śambhuḥ --\> sant\[uk\] + śambhuḥ (62: *s-toḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) sanc + śambhuḥ (62: *s-toḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) --\> sañc + śambhuḥ (76: *śaścho 'ṭi*) sañc + chambhuḥ (73: *jharo jhari savarṇe*) --\> sañchambhuḥ

in the absence of the optional rule 73: *jharo jhari savarṇe*, the conjunction remains as sañcchambhuḥ\|

in the absence of the optional rule 76: *śaścho 'ṭi*, the conjunction remains as sañcśambhuḥ\|

in the absence of the optional rule 88: *śi t*\[*uk*\], the conjunction remains as sañśambhuḥ\|

## 89 – ṅamo hrasvād aci ṅamuṇ nityam \| 8.3.32

HNV: 119. vāmanāt ṅa-ṇa-nā dviḥ sarveśvare

**PP: ṅamaḥ hrasvāt aci ṅamuṭ nityam (saṁhitāyām)**

*hrasvāt paro yo ṅam tad antaṁ yat padaṁ tasmāt parasyāco ṅamuṭ \| pratyaṅ ṅātmā \| sugaṇṇīśaḥ \| sannacyutaḥ \|*

**hrasvāt paro yo ṅam tad antaṁ yat padaṁ**—after a short vowel, a letter of *ṅam* (*ṅ, ṇ, n*) if ending in a *pada*; **tasmāt parasya acaḥ**—of the vowel in the next room that comes after a letter of *ṅam*; **nityam ṅamuṭ āgamaḥ syāt**—the *ṅuṭ, ṇuṭ* or *nuṭ* is to be the invariable augment \|

The *ṅut, ṇut* or *nut* are to be the invariable augment of the subsequent vowel, if a letter of *ṅam* preceded by a short vowel is ending in a *pada*\|

(K) If the *pada*-final nasal stops denoted by *ṅam* come after *hrasva ac* and are followed by *ac*, then the augment *ṅamuṭ* necessarily becomes the initial segment of the following *pada* beginning with *ac* \|

According to *paribhāṣā ubhaya-niṛdeśe pañcamī nirdeśo balīyān* the *aci* is to be converted into *acaḥ* (6thcase) \| So, *ṅamuṭ* (*ṅuṭ, ṇuṭ* or *nuṭ*) is to be the definite *āgama* of the following vowel, when such vowel after the final letter either of the *ṅ, ṇ* or *n* respactively being followed by a short vowel (*hrasva + ṅ, ṇ* or *n* ending in a *pada* + ac = hrasva + ṅ, ṇ or n + the *āgama ṅ, ṇ* or *n + aci*) \|

pratyaṅ + ātmā --\> pratyaṅ + ṅātmā --\> pratyaṅṅātmā \|

## 90 – samaḥ suṭi \| 8.3.5

HNV: 767. (vṛtti)

**PP: samaḥ suṭi ru (saṁhitāyām)**

samo ruḥ (syāt) suṭi \|

**samaḥ**—in place of the word *sam*; **ruḥ**(*syāt*)—the *r*\[*u*\] (*upadeśe 'janunāsika it*) or *r* is to be the substitute; **suṭi**—if the *s*\[*uṭ*\] follows;

In place of the preposition *sam* the *r*\[*u*\] is to be the substitute, if the *s*\[*uṭ*\] follows \| sam + skartā (21:*alo 'ntasya*) --\> sar + skartā\|

(K) the term *ru* is used without any nominal ending \|

## 91 – atrānunāsikaḥ pūrvasya tu vā \| 8.3.2 \|

No Equivalent in HNV atra ru prakaraṇe roḥ pūrvasyānunāsiko vā (syāt) \|

**atra ru prakaraṇe**—in this affair or section when a nasal (*m* or *n*) is converted into *r*\[*u*\]; (conversion or subsitution of *r* is found in two sections **(i)**the *m* and *n* are converted into *r* from the rule *samaḥ suṭi* to 100: *kanāmreḍite ca*, **(ii)** the *visarga* or *s* is converted into r in the rule 105: *sa sajuṣo ruḥ*); **roḥ pūrvasya**—in place of the preceding letter of the r; **anunāsikaḥ vā**(*syāt*)—substitution of the nasal form(*candra-bindu*) is to be optional;

In this context when the nasal *m* or *n* is converted into *r*\[*u*\], the optional augment of the nasal form (*candra-bindu*) is inflicted with the preceding letter to the *r*\[*u*\] \|

sar + skartā, in place of the preceding *a*, the nasal form is to be optional āgama as sA\*r.+ sk$$$taAR\| As this is an optional rule, in absence of the nasal form, the augment is to be as follows:

(K) The *anunāsika* vowel optionally replaces the vowel which precedes *r*\[*u*\] \| ... and to have the other form, where the nasalized vowel does not replace the phoneme preceding *r*\[*u*\], the following sūtra presents itself for application:

## 92 – anunāsikāt paro 'nusvāraḥ \| 8.3.4

HNV: 767. (vṛtti)

*anunāsikaṁ vihāya roḥ pūrvasmāt paro 'nusvārāgamaḥ (syāt) \|*

**anunāsikam vihāya**—repudiating to the nasal form; **roḥ pūrvasmāt paraḥ**—after the prior letter to *r*; **anusvārāgamaḥ**(*syāt*)—an *anusvāra* is to be the augment;

In stead of the *candrabindu*, the *anusvāra* is to be the final augment with the preceding letter of the *r*\[*u*\]\| as the rule 91: *atrānunāsikaḥ pūrvasya tu vā* is an optional one, in absence of the nasal form, the *āgama* is to the *anusvāra* after the letter (*a*) the precedes to the *r* \|

## 93 – khar avasānayor visarjanīyaḥ \| 8.3.15

HNV: **155. sa-ra-rāmayor viṣṇusargo viṣṇupadānte**

PP: **khar avasānayor padasya raḥ visarjanīyaḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

*khari avasāne ca padāntasāya rephasya visargaḥ syāt \|*

Let *visarjanīya* replace the *pada*-final *repha* when the unvoiced consonants denoted by *khar* follow or when there is a cessation (*avasana*) \|

saṁr + skarta --\> saṁḥ + skarta \|

(K) at this stage, *vā śari*, which is an exception to the sūtra *visarjanīyasya saḥ,* applies and the optional replacement by *visarga* becomes available. In order to introduce the obligatory substitute *s* in place of *visarga*, *Kātyāyana* enunciates the following *vārtika*:

**XV – sam-puṅkānāṁ so vaktavyaḥ** \|

*saṁsskartā, saṁsskartā (with candrabindu) \|*

It should be stated that the final *visarga* of *sam, pum* and *kān* is to be replaced by *s \|*

(K) … It means that the *visarga* which is understood here from the context is to be replaced by the phoneme *s* and not by *jihvāmūliya* before *kān* or by upadhmāniya before *pum.*

(K) Some grammarians desire the optional deletion of the final phoneme *m* of *sam* (*samo vā lopameka icchanti,* Mbh 8.3.12). Since this has been mentioned in the *Mahābhāṣya* in the section on *ru,* both the substitution of *anunāsika* and the insertion of *anusvāra* are available here (see for explanation SK, I, p. 139 and BM, I, p. 139). Thus after the elision of the final *m* of *sam* and as a consequence even in the absence of substitution of *ru* in place of the final *m* of the preverb s*am,* we have two forms with a single s: *saṁskartā, saṁskartā* (with candrabindu)*.*

## 94 – pumaḥ khayy ampare \| 8.3.6

HNV: **1994. pumaḥ sa-rāmo harikamala-harikhaḍgayor yādavetara-parayoḥ, sa ca viṣṇucakra-pūrvo viṣṇucāpa-pūrvo vā, tatra ka-kha-pa-pheṣu viṣṇusarga-niṣedhaś ca** \|

**PP: pumaḥ ru khayi ampare (saṁhitāyām)** \|

*ampare khayi pumo ruḥ (syāt) \| puṁskokilaḥ (with candrabindu), puṁskokilaḥ \|*

Let the *ru* replace the final phoneme of *pum* if it is followed by the *khay* followed by *am* \|

pum + kokila --\> pur + kokila \|
