HNV:

**104. no ’ntaś ca-chayoḥ śa-rāmo, viṣṇucakra-pūrvo viṣṇucāpa-pūrvo vā**

**105. ṭa-ṭhayoḥ ṣa-rāmaḥ**

**106. ta-thayoḥ sa-rāmaḥ**

**107. na tse**

**108. praśāno nasya cādau hariveṇuḥ**

*ampare chavi nāntasya padasya ruḥ \| na tu praśān śabdasya \|*

*ru*replaces pada-final *n* before *chav* followed by *am* but not in case of *praśān* \|

cakrin + trāyasva --\> cakrir + trāyasva --\> cakriṁr + trāyasva --\> cakriṁḥ + trāyasva --\> …

## 96 – visarjanīyasya saḥ \| 8.3.34

HNV:

**134. ca-chayoḥ śa-rāmaḥ**

**135. ṭa-ṭhayoḥ ṣa-rāmaḥ**

**136. ta-thayoḥ sa-rāmaḥ**

**PP: khari visarjanīyasya saḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \|

*khari visarjanīyasya saḥ syāt \| cakriṁstrāyasva (with candrabindu), cakriṁstrāyasva \| apraśān kim? praśān tanoti \| padntasyeti kim? hanti \|*

before *khar* *visarga* is replaced by *s* \|

… cakriṁḥ + trāyasva --\> cakriṁstrāyasva \|

(K) … The expression *praśān tanoti* is an exception \|

## 97 – nṝn pe \| 8.3.10

HNV: Vaidika —No Equivalent in HNV

**PP: nṝn naḥ ru pe ubhayathā (saṁhitāyām)**\|

nṝn ityasya rur vā pe \|

The final *n* of *nṝn* is optionally replaced by *ru* before *p* \|

(K) … the word *ubhayathā* conveys the sense of being “optional” \|

nṝn + pāhi --\> nṝr + pāhi or nṝn pāhi \| nṝr + pāhi --\> nṝṁr + pāhi --\> nṝṁḥ + pāhi \| at this stage, the rule *visarjanīyasya saḥ* becomes applicable but the following sūtra blocks it:

## 98 – ku-pvoḥ x ka x pau ca \| 8.3.37

HNV:

**131. viṣṇusargo jihvāmūlīyaḥ ka-khayor vā**

**132. pa-phayor upadhmānīyaḥ**

**PP: ku-pvoḥ visarjanīyasya** ***x*** **ka** ***x*** **pau ca (saṁhitāyām)**\|

ka-varge pa-varge ca pare visargasya *x* ka *x* pau staḥ \| cād visargaḥ \| nṝṁ*x* (with *candrabindu*) pāhi, nṝṁḥ (with *candrabindu*) pāhi, nṝṁḥ*x* pāhi, nṝṁḥ pāhi, nṝn pāhi \|

The *visarga* is replaced by *jihvāmūlīya* or *upadhmānīya* if it is respectively followed by the *ka-varga* or *pa-varga*. The use of *ca* in the sūtra means that the *visarga* is optionally retained as it is \|

## 99 – tasya param āmreḍitam \| 8.1.2

HNV: No Equivalent in HNV

**PP: tasya dviruktasya (dve) param āmreḍitam**\|

dviruktasya param āmreḍitaṁ syāt \|

Of that which is iterated, let the second element be designated as *āmreḍita* \|

(K) The application of this term is seen in the following sūtra \|

## 100 – kānāmreḍite \| 8.3.12

HNV: **107. (vṛtti) kān kān ity atra kāṁs kān iti vācyaṁ vā** \|

**PP: kān āmreḍite naḥ ru (saṁhitāyām)**\|

kānnakārasya ruḥ syād āmreḍite \| kāṁskān (with CB), kāṁskān \|

If *āmreḍita* follows, then *n* of *kān* is replaced by *ru* \|

(K) In the doublet *kān kān* the word has been stated twice to denote either repetition or pervasion \|

kān kān --\> kār kān … \|

## 101 – che ca \| 6.1.73

**HNV: 116. dviḥ sarveśvara-mātrāc chaḥ**

**PP: hrasvasya t\[uk\] che ca (saṁhitāyām)** \|

hrasvasya che t\[uk\] \| śivac chāyā \|

The *āgama t*\[*uk*\] is inserted at the end of *hrasva ac* before *ch* \|

śiva + chāyā --\> śivat + chāyā --\> (67) śivad + chāyā --\> (62) śivaj + chāyā --\> (74) śivacchāyā \|

## 102 – padāntād vā \| 6.1.76

HNV: **117. viṣṇupadāntāt trivikramād vā**

**PP: dīrghāt padāntāt che tuk vā (saṁhitāyām)**\|

*dīrghāt padāntāc che tug vā \| lakṣmīc chāyā, lakṣmī chāyā \|*

*āgama t*\[*uk*\] is optionally inserted after *dīrgha ac* occurring at end of a *pada* before *ch* \|

--- **iti hal-sandhiḥ** ---

## atha visarga-sandhi-prakaraṇam

## 103 – visarjanīyasya saḥ \| 8.3.34 and vā śari \| 8.3.36

**PP: khari visarjanīyasya saḥ (saṁhitāyām)**

let *visarga* followed by *khar* be replaced by *s* \|

**PP: śari visarjanīyasya visarjanīyaḥ vā (saṁhitāyām)**

*śari visargasya visargo vā syāt \| hariḥ śete, hariś śete \|*

*visarga*remains unchanged optionally, if *śar* follows\|

hariḥ śete or hariś śete \|

## 104 – sa-sajuṣo ruḥ \| 8.2.66

**PP: sa-sajuṣoḥ ruḥ padasya** \|

padāntasya sasya sajuṣaś ca ruḥ syāt \|

let the pada-final *s* and the word *sajuṣ* be replaced by *ru* \|

śivas + acaryaḥ --\> śivar acaryaḥ \|

this *sūtra* precludes the pada-final *ṣ* from being replaced by *ḍ* according to *jhalāṁ jaśo ‘nte* (67) \| in this respect it is an exception to rule 67 \|

## 105 – ato ror aplutād aplute \| 6.1.113

(ataḥ roḥ aplutād aplute)

**PP: aplutāt ataḥ roḥ ut aplute ati (saṁhitāyaṁ)** \|

aplutād ataḥ parasya ror uḥ syād aplute 'ti \| śivor 'cyaḥ \|

Let the substitute *hrasva u* replace *ru* when it is preceded and followed by a non-prolated *hrasva a* \|

śivas + acaryaḥ --\> śivar + acaryaḥ --\> śiva + u + acaryaḥ (27: *ād guṇaḥ*) śivo + acaryaḥ (43: *eṅaḥ padāntad ati*) śivo ‘caryaḥ \|

## 106 – haśi ca \| 6.1.114

**PP: aplutāt ataḥ roḥ ut haśi ca (saṁhitāyām)**

tathā \| śivo vandyaḥ \|

In place of the *ru* that comes after the *srasva a*, the substitution is to be the *u*, if *haś* follows \|

śivas + vandyaḥ --\> (105: *sa sajuṣo ruḥ*) śivar + vandyaḥ --\> (*haśi ca*) śiva + u + vandyaḥ --\> (27) śivo vandyaḥ \|

## 107 – bho-bhago-agho-a-pūrvasya yo 'śi \| 8.3.17

**PP: bho-bhago-agho-a-pūrvasya roḥ yaḥ aśi (saṁhitāyām)**

*etat pūrvasya ror yādeśo 'śi \| devā iha, devāyiha \| bhos bhagos aghos iti sāntā nipātāḥ \| teṣāṁ ror yatve kṛte \|*

The substitute *y* replaces *ru* when preceded by *bho, bhago, agho, a* (*ā*) and followed by *aś* \|

devās + iha --\> (*sa sajuṣo ruḥ*) devar + iha --\> (106) devāy + iha --\> (30: *lopaḥ śākalyasya*) devā iha \|

## 108 – hali sarveṣām \| 8.3.22

**PP: bho-bhago-agho-a-pūrvasya yasya lopaḥ hali sarveṣām (saṁhitāyām)**

*bho-bhago-agho-a-pūrvasya yasya lopaḥ syād dhali \| bho devāḥ \| bhago namaste \| agho yāhi \|*

The phoneme *y* preceded by *bho, bhago, agho, a* (*ā*) and followed by a *hal* should be replaced by *lopa* (according to all grammarians) \|

bhos + devāḥ --\> bhoru + devāḥ --\> bhor + devāḥ --\> bhoy + devāḥ --\> bho devāḥ \|

## 109 – ro ‘supi \| 8.2.69

**PP: ahan raḥ asupi**\|

*ro ‘supi ahno rephādeśo na tu supi \| aharahaḥ \| ahar-gaṇaḥ \|*

Let the substitute *repha* replace the word *ahan* but not when followed by *sup*-affixes \|

(K) The word *ahan* is understood as having a genitive nominal ending which has been elided *(tac ca luptaṣaṣṭhīkaṁ padam,* BM, I, pp. 164-5). This is the reason why *Varadaraja* has used the term *ahnaḥ* in the *vṛtti* \|

(K) … when the word *ahan* is followed by the *sup*-affixes such as *bhis, bhyām, bhyas* etc., the final phoneme *n* of *ahan* is not replaced by *repha*. Instead, it is replaced by *ru* in accordance with the *sūtra* *ahan* Pā. 8.2.68, which then changes to *u* by *haśi ca* (106) \| Then by *ād guṇaḥ* (27) we get the forms like *ahobhyām, ahobhiḥ,* etc. Had the phoneme *repha* replaced the last phoneme *n* of *ahan,* the sūtra *haśi ca* (106) would not have applied. The reason is that the sūtra *haśi ca* (106) applies where the substitute *ru* has come as a replacement and not the phoneme *repha* \|

(K) … The present sūtra is an exception to *ahan* Pā. 8.2.68 which prescribes *ru*-replacement in place of the pada-final phoneme *n* of *ahan.* The present sūtra blocks it and enjoins *repha*-replacement in place of the phoneme *n. Varadarāja* gives two usages to illustrate this sūtra.

1 – aharahaḥ, 2 – ahar-gaṇaḥ\|

## 110 – ro ri \| 8.3.14

**PP: raḥ ri lopaḥ** \|

rephasya rephe pare lopaḥ \|

Let the *repha* followed by (another) *repha* be replaced by *lopa*\|

## 111 – ḍh-ra-lope pūrvasya dīrgho ‘ṇaḥ \| 6.3.111

**PP: ḍh-ra-lope pūrvasya aṇaḥ dīrghaḥ**\|

*ḍha-rephayor lopa-nimittayoḥ pūrvasyāṇo dīrghaḥ syāt \| punā ramate \| harī ramyaḥ \| śambhū rājate \| aṇaḥ kim? tṛḍhaḥ \| vṛḍhaḥ \|*

When the *ḍh* and *r* are the cause of deletion, *aṇ*, if stands prior to the *ḍh* or *r*, is substituted by a long\|

(K) Let a substitute long vowel replace *aṇ* immediately preceding a *lopa*-replacement of the phoneme *ḍh* or *r* which has become the cause of the elision of the phoneme *ḍh* or *r \|*

haris + ramyaḥ --\> harir + ramyaḥ --\> hari + ramyaḥ --\> harī ramyaḥ \|

**laghu – manas ratha ityatra rutve kṛte haśi ca (106) ityutve ro ri (110) iti lope prāpte** \|

In the expression *manas + ratha,* the phoneme *s* having been replaced by *ru,* the replacement of *ru* by *u* according to *haśi ca* (106) and the *lopa*-replacement (in place of the *repha* of *ru)* by *ro ri* (110) become available (simultaneously). (At this stage the following sūtra ensues):

## 112 – vipratiṣedhe paraṁ kāryam \| 1.4.2

tulya-bala-virodhe paraṁ kāryaṁ syāt \| iti lope prāpte – pūrvatrāsiddham (31) iti ro ri (110) ityasyāsiddhatvād uttvam eva \| manorathaḥ \|

When there is a conflict caused by two rules of equal strength, let the one which is subsequent in order (in the *Aṣṭādhyāyī*) apply \|

(K) … Let us take the declined form *rāmebhyaḥ* to illustrate this point. When we introduce the *sup*-affix *bhyas* to the nominal stem *rāma,* both the sūtras *supi ca* Pā. 7.3.102 and *bahu-vacane jhalyet* Pā. 7.3.103 apply simultaneously but in accordance with the present sūtra, the latter rule applies and we have the form *rāmebhyaḥ \|*

## 113 – etat-tadoḥ su-lopo ‘kor anañ-samase hali \| 6.1.132

**PP: etat-tadoḥ su-lopaḥ akoḥ anañ-samāse hali (saṁhitāyām)**

*akakārayor etat-tador yaḥ sus tasya lopaḥ syād dhali, na tu nañ-samāse \| eṣa viṣṇuḥ, sa śambhuḥ \| akoḥ kim? eṣako rudraḥ \| anañsamāse kim? asaḥ śivaḥ \| hali kim? eṣo 'tra \|*

Of the pronouns *etad* and *tad,* excepting the augment of *k* at the end, the singular nominative case affix *su* is elided, if a *hal* follows. But not in a compound with the negative particle *nañ \|*

(K) Let *lopa* replace the affix *su* introduced after the pronominal stems *etad* and *tad* not having the consonant *k* and not being used as members of a *nañ-samasa*, provided a *hal* follows \|

eṣa viṣṇuḥ, sa śambhuḥ \| asaś śivaḥ \|

Vaidika —No Equivalent in Harināmāmṛta-vyākaraṇa

*sas ityasya sor lopaḥ syād aci pādaś cel lope satyeva pūryeta \| semāmaviḍḍhi prabhṛtim (ṛg-veda 2.24.1) \| saiṣa dāśarathī rāmaḥ \|*

Let the affix *su* of *sas* (*saḥ*) be replaced by *lopa* (even) when followed by *ac*, provided that by the *lopa*-replacement only the quarter-verse becomes complete \|

(K) … the meaning of the sūtra is as follows: If *ac* follows, the *lopa*-substitution in place of the affix *su* of the nominative singular form of the pronominal stem *tad* occurs provided a verse quarter becomes complete only by virtue of that *lopa*-substitution \|

(K) Some commentators hold that the word *pāda* not only refers to a quarter of a Vedic *mantra* but also to a quarter of a *ś1oka* \|

**iti visarga-sandhiḥ**

## sub-anta-prakaraṇam

**aj-anta-puṁ-liṅgāḥ**

## 115 – arthavad adhātur apratyayaḥ prātipadikam \| 1.2.45

HNV: **148. adhātu-viṣṇubhaktikam arthavan nāma**

**PP: arthavad adhātuḥ apratyayaḥ prātipadikam** \|

*dhātuṁ pratyayaṁ pratyayāntañ ca varjayitvā arthavac chabda-svarūpaṁ prātipadika-saṁjñaṁ syāt \|*

Let any meaningful form of a word which is neither a verbal root, nor an affix, nor that which ends with an affix get the designation *prātipadika* \|

(K) The grammatical term *prātipadika* can be explained as that form of a word which includes every (declined) *pada* as the following analysis shows: *pratipadaṁ gṛhṇāti tat pratipadikam* in accordance with the rule *padottarapadam grhnati* Pā. 4.4.39. Although this term originally included verbal roots also, *Pāṇini* has defined it as a meaningful form of word which is neither a verbal root nor an affix.

There are two views which the grammarians hold in this context. *Śākaṭāyana* and his followers believed that all nominal stems were derivable from verbal roots, while others held that this was not always true. *Pāṇini's* view appears to postulate two sets of nominal stems: (1) underived (*avyutpanna*) nominal stems and (2) derived (*vyutpanna)* nominal stems. The present *sūtra* assigns the designation to the former kind of nominal stems. The following *sūtra* confers the *prātipadika* designation on the second kind of nominal stems.

## 116 – kṛt-taddhita-samāsāś ca \| 1.2.46

**HNV: 148. adhātu-viṣṇubhaktikam arthavan nāma**

**PP: kṛt-taddhita-samasāḥ ca prātipadikāni** \|

kṛt-taddhitāntau samāsāś ca tathā syuḥ \|

Let (the derived forms of a word) ending in *kṛt* (primary affixes) and in *taddhita* (secondary affixes) and the compounds also be assigned the same (*prātipadika*) designation \|

## 117 – sv au \[j\]as am au\[ṭ\] \[ch\]aṣ \[ṭ\]ā bhyām bhis \[ṅ\]e bhyām bhyas \[ṅ\]as\[i\] bhyām bhyas \[ṅ\]as os ām \[ṅ\]y os su\[p\] \| 4.1.2

**HNV: 151. tatra nāmnaḥ su au jas, am au śas, ṭā bhyām bhis, ṅe bhyām bhyas, ṅasi bhyām bhyas, ṅas os ām, ṅi os sup**

su au jas iti prathamā \| am auṭ śas iti dvitīyā \| ṭā bhyām bhis iti tṛtīyā \| ṅe bhyām bhyas iti caturthī \| ṅasi bhyām bhyas iti pañcamī \| ṅas os ām iti ṣaṣṭhī \| ṅi os sup iti saptamī \|

*su au jas* is the first triplet (nominative), *am auṭ śas* is second (accusative), *ṭā bhyām bhis* is the third (instrumental), *ṅe bhyām bhyas* is the fourth (dative), *ṅasi bhyām bhyas* is the fifth (ablative), *ṅas os ām* is the sixth (genitive), *ṅi os sup* is the seventh (locative) \|

(K) … To arrive at a complete sense of the present sūtra, it is essential to know which are the governing rules and in which domain this sūtra occurs. The following three sḹtras provide the requisite details with the help of which we can interpret this sūtra in the right perspective.

(K) Taking into account all facts, the sūtra *svauja*... (117) means: The declensional affixes *su, au*, etc. are to be introduced after the word forms which end in *ṅīp, ṅīṣ, ṅīn, ṭāp, ḍāp* and *cāp* and also after nominal stems (*prātipadika*). The latter (nominal stems) are covered by the *saṁjñā* sūtras *arthavad adhātur apratyayaḥ prātipadikam* (115) and kṛt-taddhita-samāsāś ca (116).

## 118 – ṅy āp prātipadikāt \| 4.1.1

**HNV: 151. tatra nāmnaḥ su au jas, am au śas, ṭā bhyām bhis, ṅe bhyām bhyas, ṅasi bhyām bhyas, ṅas os ām, ṅi os sup**

(B) After what ends with the feminine terminations ṅī or āp or after a crude form \|

The sutra *ṅyāp prātipadikāt* (118) occurs in the domain of next two governing sutras \|

## 119 – pratyayaḥ \| 3.1.1

HNV: **150. pratyayaḥ paraḥ**

(K) \[from this moment I will not be showing (K), if notes belong to Kanshi Ram.\] … items which are introduced after a verbal root (*dhātu*) or a nominal stem (*prātipadika*) in this section are to be designated by this technical term (*pratyaya*) \|

## 120 – paraś ca \| 3.1.2

HNV: 150. pratyayaḥ paraḥ

*ity adhikṛtya \| ṅyantādābantāt prātipadikāc ca pare svādayaḥ pratyayāḥ syuḥ \|*

In view of these governing rules, (the present sūtra means): Let the affixes *su*, etc. be introduced after a form of word, which ends in either feminine affix *ṅī* or *āp* or after a nominal stem (*prātipadika*) \|

The second governing sḹtra is paraś ca (120). It also has the same range of operation as the preceding rule. **It means that the affix is to follow that form of word to which it has been prescribed**. As a consequence of this, the *paribhāṣā* sūtra *tasmād ity uttarasya* (71) is not relevant with reference to the sūtra *ṅyāpprātipadikāt* (118) in which ablative case-ending has been used \|

## 121 – supaḥ \| 1.4.103

No Equivalent in Harināmāmṛta-vyākaraṇa

**PP: supaḥ trīṇi trīṇi ekaśaḥ eka-vacana-dvi-vacana-bahu-vacanāni \|**

supas trīṇi trīṇi vacanāny ekaśa ekavacana-dvivacana-bahu-vacana-saṁjñāni syuḥ \|

Let the triplets of the affixes denoted by the abbreviatory term *sup* taken one by one get the designations *eka-vacana, dvi-vacana* and *bahu-vacana* respectively \|

The following sūtra prescribes where singular (*eka-vacana*) or dual (*dvi-vacana*) is to be used:

## 122 – dvy-ekayor dvi-vacanaika-vacane \| 1.4.22

HNV: **918. eka-dvi-bahutveṣv eka-dvi-bahu-vacanāni**

dvitvaikatvayor ete staḥ \|

Let a dual or singular affix be used to respectively denote duality or singularity \|

## 123 – virāmo 'vasānam \| 1.4.110

*varṇānām abhāvo 'vasāna-saṁjñaḥ syāt \| rutva-visargau \| rāmaḥ \|*

Let absence or termination of speech be designated as *avasāna* or cessation \|

The *Bālamanorama* derives the word *virāma* in two ways: 1. *virāma* is that in the vicinity of which the speech is being terminated. In this case the primary affix *ghañ* is introduced after *vi + ram* in the sense of locus of vicinity (*sāmīpika-adhikaraṇa).* In this sense a phoneme, which having been articulated is followed by the absence of the articulation of other succeeding phonemes, is assigned the technical term *avasāna (viramyate asminn iti virāmaḥ sāmīpike 'dhikaraṇe ghañ,* BM, I, p. 38). 2. In the second derivation, the primary affix *ghañ* is introduced after the verbal root *ram* in the sense of the verbal root itself (*bhāva*). The derived word *virāma,* in this sense, means the absence of the articulation of any following speech-units, a situation which can emerge when the articulation of some phoneme is followed by the absence or cessation of the articulation of other succeeding phonemes. In the Kāśikā on this very sūtra, the term *virāma* is also derived in the sense of instrumentality *(viramyate 'neneti vā virāmaḥ).* In the first and third senses, the last phoneme is assigned the technical term *avasāna* and in the second sense, the absence of the articulation of succeeding speech units or phonemes is assigned the designation *avasāna \|*

When the affix *ghañ* is introduced in the sense of bhāva, the absence of the articulation that follows the articulation of the last speech-unit is assigned the designation *avasāna.* In this sense the last phoneme *repha* is followed by *avasāna* consisting in the absence of the articulation of any succeeding speech-units. The *pada*-final *repha* of the word *ramar* is followed by the absence of the articulation of any succeeding speech sounds and hence is replaced by *visarjanīya*. So the *pada*-final *repha* is either itself *avasāna* or is followed by *avasāna*, in both the cases it is replaced by *visarjanīya* in accordance with the sūtra *khar-avasānayor visarjanīyaḥ* (93). In the latter case the locative case-ending used in the sutra *khar-avasanayor visarjanīyaḥ* (93) is interpreted as *para-saptamī*. It is easier to follow.

## 124 – sarūpāṇām ekaśeṣa eka-vibhaktau \| 1.2.64

HNV:

**1871. ekasya śeṣo rāmakṛṣṇe**

**1872. tulya-śabdānāṁ bhinnārthānām api**

*eka-vibhaktau yāni sarūpāṇy eva dṛṣṭāni teṣām eka eva śiṣyate \|*

Only one of the available similar forms of a nominal base remains before the same nominal ending \|

This sūtra prescribes the retention of only one from among several similar forms when they occur before a single inflectional ending *(samāna-vibhakti).* Thus given the string *rāma* + *su, rāma + su,* where two similar forms of *rāma* are occurring before *su,* the rule enjoins that only one form remains; the other is dropped. As a result, we have *rāma* + *au.* It should be borne in mind that the singular inflectional ending would be replaced by the nominative dual ending *au* since here the duality residing in the nominal stem *rāma* is intended to be denoted. The same process operates in the retention of one in deriving *rāmāḥ,* nominative plural, from *rāma* + *su, rāma + su, rāma* + *su = rāma + jas.* The same inflectional ending, to wit *su* in the above illustration, is intended. It is not actually available as will be made clear in the sequel \|

The idea behind the formation of this sūtra is that each unit of meaning requires a separate word for its denotation in deference to the principle that a single word can express only one single unit of meaning *(pratyarthaṁ śabdaniveśād naikenānekasyābhidhānaṁ tatrānekārtha- bhidhane 'nekaśabdatvaṁ prāptaṁ tasmād ekaśeṣaḥ,* Kāśikā, p. 43). In pursuance of this principle, more than one unit of meaning requires more than one word for its denotation. The purpose of the present sūtra is to facilitate the denotation of more than one unit of meaning by the use of a single word. For that the device of *ekaśeṣa* has been invoked and employed. The invocation of this device makes it possible to express two units of meaning of *rāma* to be expressed by *rāma* + *au* where the nominal stem is one. In a similar manner, more than two units of meaning can be expressed by *rāmāḥ* by introducing the sup-affix *jas* after a single nominal stem *rāma* which signifies the meanings of the forms of the word which have been dropped by this sūtra.

## 125 – prathamayoḥ pūrva-savarṇaḥ \| 6.1.102

HNV:

**157. daśāvatārasya trivikramaḥ śasi, tasmāt so naḥ puṁsi**

**178. harita au pūrva-savarṇaḥ**

**PP: akaḥ prathamayoḥ aci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrva-savarṇah dīrghaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \|

*akaḥ prathamā-dvitīyayor aci pūrva-savarṇa-dīrgha ekādeśaḥ syāt \| iti prāpte \|*

If *ak* is followed by *ac* belonging to the 1stand 2ndtriplets of nominal endings, both (the preceding and the following vowels) should be replaced by a single long vowel homogeneous with the preceding one. When this condition is available —

The word *prathamayoḥ* is construed as *ekaśeṣa* *dvandva* in the following way: *prathamā ca prathamā ca prathame* where only one *prathamā* is retained. The first word *prathamā* refers to the first triplet of inflectional endings *su au jas.* The 2nd*prathamā* is interpreted as referring to the first among the remaining triplets of inflectional endings. In this way, the word *prathamayoḥ* refers to both *prathamā* and *dvitīyā* inflectional endings, i.e., *su au jas* and *am auṭ śas.*

agnī (1.2, 2.2, 8.2), vayū (1.2, 2.2, 8.2) \|

Thus analysed, the sūtra gives the following meaning: If *ak* is followed by *ac* belonging to either *prathamā* or *dvitīyā*, then the preceding and the following vowels are replaced by one long vowel homogeneous with the preceding vowel. In the illustration *rāma + au,* the vowel *a* occurring at the end of the nominal stem *rāma* is a denotatum of *ak-pratyāhāra* and is followed by the vowel *au* which belongs to the first triplet being its second member. Thus the conditions for the substitution of a long vowel homogeneous with the vowel *a* of *rāma* are available but the replacement by a long vowel-substitution is negated by the following sūtra:

## 126 – nād ici \| 6.1.104

HNV: **141. a-dvaya-bho-bhago-aghobhyo lopyaḥ, sarveśvare tu yaś ca, na ca lope sandhiḥ**

**PP: āt ici pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrva-savarṇaḥ dīrghaḥ na saṁhitāyām**

ād ici na pūrva-savarṇa-dīrghaḥ \| vṛddhir eci \| rāmau \|

If *a* is followed by *ic*, then *dīrgha* *ac* replacement homogeneous with that which precedes does not come in place of the preceding and the following vowels \|

The expression *nād* is split as *na āt.* The word *āt* is in the ablative… With these carried over insertions, the sūtra means: If the vowel *a* is followed by *ic-pratyāhāra*, then a long vowel-replacement does not come in place of the preceding and the following elements… Consequently, a long vowel-replacement homogeneous with that which precedes does not take place. When the long replacement by *prathamayoḥ...* (125) is blocked by the present sūtra, the sūtra *vṛddhir eci* (33) again becomes available for application and a *vṛddhi*-vowel-replacement comes in place of both the preceding and following vowels… In this case the vowel *a* occurring at the end of the word *rāma* and the vowel *au* of the first triplet are both replaced by a *vṛddhi*-vowel *au* and we have the form *ramau \|*

## 127 – bahuṣu bahu-vacanam \| 1.4.21

HNV: **918. eka-dvi-bahutveṣv eka-dvi-bahu-vacanāni**

bahutva-vivakṣāyāṁ bahu-vacanaṁ syāt \|

Let a plural affix be used to denote plurality \|

## 128 – cu-ṭū \| 1.3.7

HNV: **152. (vṛtti) pratyayādyā ja-ṭa-ṇa-pāḥ**

**PP: pratyayasya ādī cu-ṭū itau**

pratyayādyau cu-ṭū itau staḥ \|

The palatal and cerebral stops occurring at the beginning of the affixes are designated as *it* \|

The affix remains as *as*. The dental sibilant coming at the end of *jas* has also got to be termed *it* by *hal antyam* (1) but this is debarred by the following two *sūtras*:

## 129 – vibhaktiś ca \| 1.4.104

No Equivalent in Harināmāmṛta-vyākaraṇa

**PP: sup tiṅ vibhaktiḥ ca**

sup-tiṅau vibhakti-saṁjñau staḥ \|

The elements denoted by the abbreviatory terms *sup* and *tiṅ* are to be designated as *vibhakti* \|

## 130 – na vibhaktau tu-s-māḥ \| 1.3.4

HNV: **152. (vṛtti) na viṣṇubhaktau ta-na-sa-mā**

**PP: na vibhaktau tu-s-māḥ itaḥ**

*vibhaktisthās ta-vargasamā netaḥ \| iti sasya nettvam \| rāmāḥ \|*

The dental stops, the phonemes *s* and *m* occurring in the *vibhakti*-affixes are not designated as it \|

(273) – **ato guṇe** (6.1.97) – If a *guṇa* (*a, e, o*) vowel follows a non-pada-final short *a*, then a single substitute in the form of the subsequent comes in place of both the preceding and the following vowels \|

The initial stage of the formation is: *rāma + as.* Here the sūtra *akaḥ savarṇe dirghaḥ* is available for application but is blocked by its exception *ato guṇe* (273) which prescribes a single vowel-replacement homogeneous with the following in place of a non-pada final short *a* and the following *guṇa* vowel *(a, e, o).* The vowel *a* coming at the end of the stem *rāma* is a non-pada final short vowel denoted by *at* and the following *a* occurring at the beginning of the sup-affix *as* constitutes a *guṇa* vowel. The conditions for a single substitution laid down by this sūtra are fulfilled. Hence the sūtra *ato guṇe* Pā. 6.1.97 blocks *akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ* and presents itself for application. At this stage, the sūtra *prathamayoḥ purva-savarṇaḥ* Pā. 6.1.102 being subsequent in order applies and a single long vowel homogeneous with the preceding one replaces both the short vowel coming at the end of *rāma* and the short vowel *a* occurring at the beginning of the sup-affix *as* since the preceding vowel *a* is *ak* and is followed by *ac* belonging to the first and second triplets of nominal endings. In this case both (the preceding and the following vowels) are replaced by a single long vowel homogeneous with the preceding one.

The aphorism *ato guṇe* Pā. 6.1.97 is an exception to *akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ* Pā. 6.1.101 and precedes it. The sūtra *prathamayoḥ pūrvasavarṇaḥ* Pa. 6.1.102 obviously follows the latter. Now by virtue of the metarule *purastād apavāda anantarān vidhīn bādhante nottarān* (*Abhyankar*, 59), the sūtra *ato guṇe* Pā. 6.1.97 can play the role of an exception only to *akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ* Pā. 6.1.101 and not to the sūtra *prathamayoḥ purva-savarṇaḥ* Pā. 6.1.102 which is subsequent to the former. That is why the sūtra *prathamayoḥ purva-savarṇah* Pā. 6.1.102 applies and we have the form *rāmās,* the *pada*-final *s* of which is replaced by *ru* in accordance with the rule *sa-sajuṣo ruḥ* (104). The remaining *repha* of *ru* is replaced by *visarjanīya* by the sūtra *khar-avasanayor visarjanīyaḥ* (93) and we have the form *rāmāḥ \|*

## 131 – eka-vacanaṁ sambuddhiḥ \| 2.3.49

HNV: **171. sambodhane sur buddha-saṁjñaḥ –** In *sambodhana*, *s*\[*u*\] is called *buddha* \|

**PP: sambodhane prathamāyāḥ eka-vacanaṁ sambuddhiḥ \|**

sambodhane prathamāyāḥ eka-vacanaṁ sambuddhiḥ \|

In the sense of vocative, let the singular affix of the first triplet be termed *sambuddhi* \|

This sūtra assigns *sambuddhi*-designation to the singular affix *su* when used in the sense of vocative \|

## 132 – yasmāt pratyaya-vidhis tadādi pratyaye 'ṅgam \| 1.4.13

HNV: **149. prakṛtiḥ pūrvā –** The first part (of a *viṣṇupada*) is called *prakṛti* **\|**

**PP: yasmāt pratyaya-vidhiḥ tadādi pratyaye aṅgam** \|

yaḥ pratyayo yasmāt kriyate tadādi śabda-svarūpaṁ tasminn aṅgaṁ syāt \|

Let the word-form beginning with that after which an affix has been introduced be designated as *aṅga* when the affix follows \|

It is to be borne in mind that the *aṅga*-designation is contingent upon the presence of the affix that follows. A word-form cannot be designated as *aṅga* simply because an affix has been prescribed after it. For the *aṅga*-designation to become operational the affix has to be present after the base after which it has been prescribed. The scope of *aṅga*-designation is observed in the following sūtra:

## 133 – eṅ-hrasvāt sambuddheḥ \| 6.1.69

**HNV: 172. e-o-vāmanebhyo buddhasyādarśanam** – *Buddha* disappears after *e, o*, or *vāmana.*

**PP: eṅ-hrasvāt sambuddheḥ hal lopaḥ** \|

eṅ-antād-dhrasvāntāc cāṅgād-dhal lupyate sambuddheś cet \| he rāma \| he rāmau \| he rāmāḥ \|

A consonantal phoneme of the vocative singular introduced after a nominal stem (*aṅga*) ending in a vowel denoted by *eṅ* or ending in *hrasva ac* is replaced by *lopa* \|

## 134 – ami pūrvaḥ \| 6.1.107

HNV: **156. daśāvatārād am-śasor a-rāma-haraḥ**

**PP: akaḥ ami aci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrvaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \|

ako 'my aci pūrva-rūpam ekādeśaḥ \| rāmam \| rāmau \|

When *ac* of *am* follows, the single replacement homogeneous with the preceding vowel (*ak*) comes in place of both the preceding and the following vowels \|

For the declension of the plural form of *rāma* of the second triplet we have *rāma* + *śas*. The dental *s* at the end of *śas* is not *it* by *hal antyam* since it has been negated by the *sūtra* *na vibhaktau tusmāḥ* (130). To designate the initial *ś* of *śas* as it we have the following sūtra:

## 135 – la-śa-kv ataddhite \| 1.3.8

HNV: **152. (vṛtti) śa-ka-vargāv ataddhite**

**PP: pratyayasya ādi la-śa-ku it ataddhite (upadeśe)** \|

taddhita-varja-pratyayādyā laśakavargā itaḥ syuḥ \|

Let the consonants *la-kāra*, *śa-kāra* and *ka-varga* occurring initially in the affixes except the *taddhita* ones be designated as *it* \|

In *rāma + śas*, the affix *śas* is not a *taddhita* affix, therefore the initial *ś* of *śas* is designated as *it*. So, this is replaced by *lopa* by the rule *tasya lopaḥ* (3). After the elision of *ś*, we have *rāma + as*. The vowel *a* at the end of the stem *rāma* and the vowel *a* at the beginning of the remaining affix as are both replaced by a long vowel homogeneous with the preceding vowel *a* in accordance with the sūtra *prathamayoḥ pūrva-savarṇaḥ* (125). We now have *rāmās*. At this stage, the following sūtra ensues:

## 136 – tasmāc chaso naḥ puṁsi \| 6.1.103

HNV: **157. daśāvatārasya trivikramaḥ śasi, tasmāt so naḥ puṁsi**

**PP: tasmāt śasaḥ naḥ puṁsi (saṁhitāyām)** \|

pūrva-savarṇa-dīrghāt paro yaḥ śasaḥ sas tasya naḥ syāt puṁsi \|

The phoneme *s* of *śas* which occurs after a long vowel replacement homogeneous with that which precedes is replaced by *n* in the masculine \|

At this stage, the following sūtra becomes available for application and the contingency of *na-kāra* coming at the end of *rāmān* to be replaced by *ṇa-kāra* arises. In order to show how this replacement is due, the author of the Laghu presents the following sūtra:

## 137 – aṭ-ku-pv-āṅ-num-vyavāye 'pi \| 8.4.2

HNV: **173. ra-ṣa-ṛ-dvayebhyo nasya ṇaḥ, sarveśvara-ha-ya-va-ka-varga-pa-varga-vyavadhāne ’pi, samāna-viṣṇupade, na tu viṣṇupadāntasya –** After r, ṣ, or ṛ-dvaya, a n situated in thesame viṣṇupada as them becomes ṇ. This is the case even when sarveśvara, h, y, v, ka-varga, or pa-varga intervenes. But n situated at the end of viṣṇupada doesn’t become ṇ \|

**PP: aṭ-ku-pv-āṅ-num-vyavāye api samāna-pade raṣābhyām naḥ ṇaḥ**

**(samhitāyām)** \|

*aṭ-ka-varga-pa-varga-āṅ-num-etair-vyastair-yathāsaṁbhavaṁ militaiś ca vyavadhāne 'pi raṣābhyāṁ parasya nasya ṇaḥ samāna-pade \| iti prāpte \|*

Even when separated by the intervention of the elements *aṭ*, velar stops, labial stops, (the particle) *āṅ* and (the infixed augment) *num* singly or combined in any possible way, let the cerebral *ṇa-kāra* replace the dental *na-kāra* following *repha* and *ṣa-kāra* in the same *pada* \|

## 138 – padāntasya \| 8.4.37

HNV: **173. ra-ṣa-ṛ-dvayebhyo nasya ṇaḥ, sarveśvara-ha-ya-va-ka-varga-pa-varga-vyavadhāne ’pi, samāna-viṣṇupade, na tu viṣṇupadāntasya**

**PP: aṭ-ku-pv-aṅ-num-vyavaye 'pi ra-ṣābhyam samāna-pade padāntasya naḥ naḥ na** \|

nasya ṇo na \| rāmān \|

Let the substitute cerebral nasal stop *ṇ* not replace a *pada*-final dental nasal stop *n*\|

Now we have rama + ta with the singular third case-ending. In this context the following sutra presents itself:

## 139 – ṭā-ṅasi-ṅasām ināt-syāḥ \| 7.1.12

HNV: **159. kṛṣṇāt ṭā inaḥ, 164. kṛṣṇāt ṅaser āt, 165. kṛṣṇāt ṅasaḥ sya**

**PP: ataḥ aṅgāt tā-ṇasi-ṅasām ināt-syāḥ** \|

adantāṭṭādīnāminādayaḥ syuḥ \| ṇatvam \| rāmeṇa \|

Let the substitute elements *ina*, *āt* and *sya* respectively replace \[*ṭ*\]*ā*, \[*ṅ*\]*asi* and \[*ṅ*\]*as* introduced after a nominal stem ending in a short vowel *a* \|

The cerebral *ṇ*-replacement takes place and we have*rāmeṇa*\|

## 140 – supi ca \| 7.3.102

HNV: **160. kṛṣṇasya trivikramo gopāle**

**PP: supi ca ahgasya atah dirghah yani** \|

yañādau supi ato 'ṅgasya dīrghaḥ \| rāmābhyām\|

Let a substitute long vowel replace the *aṅga*-final short vowel *a* of a nominal stem before *sup*-affixes beginning with *yañ* \|

## 141 – ato bhisa ais \| 7.1.9

HNV: **161. kṛṣṇād bhisa ais**

**PP: ataḥ aṅgāt bhisa ais** \|

Let the substitute *ais* replace the *sup*-affix *bhis* introduced after a pre-affixal base ending in the short vowel *a*. By the rule *anekāl śit sarvasya* (45), (the substitute *ais* replaces the whole of *bhis* and we have the form) *rāmaiḥ*\|

*adantād aṅgāt parasya bhisa ais syāt \| anekāl śit sarvasya \| rāmaiḥ\|*

*Varadarāja*has mentioned the sūtra *anekāl śit sarvasya* (45) in his commentary in order to drive home the following point: The *ais*-replacement is prescribed by using the sixth case-ending in the word *bhisa*. The replacement enjoined in this manner comes in place of the last phoneme of the substituendum ending in the genitive according to the sūtra *alo 'ntyasya* (21). The undesirable contingency of replacing the last phoneme *s* of *bhis* would be incurred. He reminds the reader by quoting the rule *anekāl śit sarvasya* (45), which is an exception to the rule *alo 'ntyasya* (21), that since the substitute contains more than one phoneme (*al*), it would replace the whole of *bhis* and not merely its last phoneme. Similarly, the rule *anekāl śit sarvasya* can also be considered to be an exception to the rule *ādeḥ parasya* (72) which prescribes the replacement by using the fifth case-ending to come in place of the initial phoneme of the substituendum which follows the term used in the ablative. After the substitute *ais* has replaced the whole of *bhis*, a *vṛddhi*-vowel substitute replaces both the vowel *a* at the end of *rāma* and the initial vowel *ai* of *ais* by the sūtra *vṛddhir eci* (33) and we have the form rāmaiḥ \|

## 142 – ṅer yaḥ \| 7.1.13

HNV: **162. kṛṣṇāt ṅer yaḥ**

**PP: ataḥ aṅgāt ṅeḥ yaḥ** \|

ato 'ṅgāt parasya ṅer yādiśaḥ \|

Let the substitute *ya* replace the *sup*-affix *ṅe* introduced after a pre-affixal base (*aṅga*) ending in a *hrasva* vowel *a* \|

## 143 – sthānivad ādeśo 'nalvidhau \| 1.1.56

HNV: **260. (vṛtti) ādeśaḥ sthāni-vat kvacit –** The replacement (*ādeśa*) is sometimes treated like the original (*sthāni*)

**PP: sthānivat ādeśaḥ analvidhau** \|

*ādeśaḥ sthānivat syāt na tu sthānyalāśrayavidhau \| iti sthānivattvāt supi ceti dīrghaḥ \| rāmāya \| rāmābhyām \|*

A substitute (*ādeśa*) is treated like a substituendum (*sthānin*) except with regard to an operation dependent on the phoneme (*al*) of the substituendum. By this, the substitute *ya* is treated as the substituendum *ṅe* which is a *sup*-affix. Consequently, the sūtra *supi ca* applies and with a long-vowel replacement we have *rāmāya*\|

Taking into account all circumstances, the sūtra means: A replacement is treated like the substituendum except with regard to any of the following operations, dependent on the phoneme (*al*) of the substituendum: 1. when an operation is to be performed by a phoneme or because of a phoneme 2. when an operation is to be performed on an element occurring after a phoneme 3. when an operation is to be performed in place of a phoneme and 4. when an operation is to be performed depending on the following phoneme \|

When the plural *sup*-affix is introduced after the nominal stem *rāma* we have *rāma + bhyas*. At this stage, the following sūtra applies:

## 144 – bahu-vacane jhalyet \| 7.3.103

HNV: **163. kṛṣṇasya e vaiṣṇave bahutve**

**PP: ataḥ aṅgasya bahu-vacane jhali supi et** \|

*jhalādau bahu-vacane supi ato 'ṅgasyaikāraḥ \| rāmebhyaḥ \| supi kim? pacadhvam \|*

Let the substitute phoneme *e* replace the *aṅga*-final vowel of a nominal stem ending in a short vowel *a* when followed by a plural *sup*-affix beginning with *jhal*. Why (is it said) when a *sup*-affix follows? (The counter example is) *pacadhvam* \|

## 145 – vāvasāne \| 8.4.56

HNV: **252. viṣṇudāsasya harikamalaṁ vā virāme**

**PP: avasāne jhalāṁ caro vā (saṁhitāyām)**

*avasāne jhalāṁ caro vā \| rāmāt, rāmād \| rāmābhyām \| rāmebhyaḥ \| rāmasya \|*  
*caroptionally comes in place of jhal, when jhal occurs in avasāna\|*

At this stage, the *pada*-final *t* is replaced by *t* \| In the alternative situation when the *car*-replacement does not take place, *jhalāṁ jaśo 'nte* (67) applies and we have the phoneme *d* substituted for the final phoneme *t*. Thus we have two forms of *rāma* with a singular nominal ending of the fifth triplet: *rāmāt* and *rāmād* \|

## 146 – osi ca \| 7.3.104

HNV: **166. kṛṣṇasya e osi**

**PP: osi ca ataḥ aṅgasya et** \|

ato 'ṅgasyaikāraḥ \| rāmayoḥ \|

Let the *aṅga*-final *hrasva ac* be replaced by the *e*, when the *sup*-affix *os* follows \|

rāma + os --\> (146) rāme + os --\> (22) rāmay + os … rāmayoḥ \|

To inflect *rāma* with the plural *sup*-affix of the sixth triplet, we introduce *ām* after the nominal stem *rāma* and we have *rāma + ām*. Now the rule *akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ* becomes available for application but the following sūtra blocks it and applies:

## 147 – hrasva-nadyāpo n\[uṭ\] \| 7.1.54

HNV: **167. vāmana-gopī-rādhābhyo nuḍ āmi** \|

**PP: hrasva-nadyāpaḥ aṅgāt āmaḥ nut** \|

hrasvāntād nady antād āb-antāc cāṅgāt parasya nuḍāgamaḥ \|

Let the *āgama n*\[*uṭ*\] be introduced to *sup*-affix *ām* when it occurs after an *aṅga* which ends in *hrasva ac* or which is denoted by *nadī* or which ends in the feminine affix *āp* (*āb-anta*) \|

The word *nadī* is a technical term which will be defined in the sūtra 193 \|

rāma + ām -\> rāma + n\[uṭ\] + ām -\> rāma + nām … \|

At this stage, the following sūtra applies:

## 148 – nāmi \| 6.4.3

HNV: **169. vāmanasya trivikramo nāmi, nṛ-śabdasya tu vā, na tisṛ-catasroḥ**

**PP: nāmi aṅgasya dīrghaḥ** \|

aj-antāṅgasya dīrghaḥ \| rāmāṇām \| rāme \| rāmayoḥ \| supi – ettve kṛte \|

Let the pre-affixal base ending in *ac* be replaced by *dīrgha ac* before *nām* \|

(The following sūtra applies) after replacing the *aṅga*-final short *a* by the substitute *e*(*t*) in the case where the plural nominal affix (*sup*) of the seventh triplet follows \| rām**ā**ṇām \|

One important point remains to be discussed here. The lengthening of the *aṅga*-final vowel could have been obtained by the rule *supi ca* Pā. 7.3.102 which is subsequent to the present sūtra but the rule *supi ca* (140) does not apply because of the meta-rule *sannipāta-lakṣano vidhir animittaṁ tadvighātasya* (*Abhyankar*, 85) \| The augment *nuṭ* is inserted at the head of the affix *ām*, when it is introduced after an *aṅga* which ends in a short vowel. This very *nuṭ* now occasions the lengthening of the *aṅga*-final vowel by *supi ca* (140) and the present sūtra. In this way it leads to the destruction of its own cause in that the short vowel occasions the insertion of the augment *nuṭ* which becomes the cause of the lengthening of that very short vowel. In the context of the present rule, the meta-rule (*Abhyankar*, 85) is blocked by the very fact that the present sūtra has been enunciated by *Pāṇini*. Otherwise the present rule would be rendered vacuous (cf. SK, I, pp. 203-04), which is not the case with the rule *supi ca* (140) \|

## 149 – ādeśa-pratyayayoḥ \| 8.3.59

HNV: **170. īśvara-harimitra-ka-ṅebhyaḥ pratyaya-viriñci-sasya ṣo, num-viṣṇusarga-vyavadhāne ’pi, na tu viṣṇupadādy-anta-sātīnām -** After an *īśvara, harimitra, k,* or *ṅ,* the *s* of a *pratyaya* or *viriñci* becomes *ṣ.* This is the case even when *n\[um\]* or *viṣṇusarga* intervenes. But *s* at the beginning or end of a *viṣṇupada* and the *s* of *sāt\[i\]* don’t become *ṣa-rāma \|*

**PP: num-visarjanīya-śar-vyavāye 'pi iṅ-koḥ ādeśa-pratyayayoḥ apadāntasya saḥ mūrdhanyaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \|

*iṇkubhyāṁ parasyāpadāntasyādeśasya pratyayāvayavaś ca yaḥ sas tasya mūrdhanyādeśaḥ \| īṣad-vivṛtasya sasya tādṛśa eva ṣaḥ \| rāmeṣu \| evaṁ kṛṣṇādayo 'pyadantāḥ \|*

Let the substitute *ṣ* replace a non-*pada*-final *s* of a replacement element or of an affix if it (*s*) follows the elements denoted by *iṅ-pratyāhāra* and *ku*. The *īṣad-vivṛta* *ṣ* resembles most the dental *s* and is therefore the proper replacement \|

After all points have been taken into consideration, the sūtra comes to mean: A non-*pada*-final dental *s* which is either a substitute (*ādeśa*) or a part of an affix is replaced by a cerebral *ṣ*. One more point is also to be taken note of in the context of this rule. The immediately preceding sūtra in the *Aṣṭādhyāyī* is *num-visarjanīya-śar-vyavāye 'pi* Pā. 8.3.58. It is also read into the present sūtra. Then the sūtra will mean that the replacement cerebral *ṣ* does come in place of the dental *s*, even if there is an intervention of *num*, *visarga* and śar between *iṅ-ku* and the dental *s* in the same *pada* \|

rāma + su -\> rāme + su -\> rāmeṣu \|

## 150 – sarvādīni sarvanāmāni \| 1.1.27

HNV: **311. sarvādīni kṛṣṇa-nāmāni**

sarva, viśva, ubha, ubhaya, ḍatara, ḍatama, anya, anyatara, itara, tvat, tva, nema, sama, sima \| pūrva-parā ‘varadakṣiṇottarā ‘parā ‘dharāṇi vyavasthāyāmasañjñāyām \| svamajñātidhanākhyāyām \| antaraṁ bahir yogopasaṁvyānayoḥ \| tyad, tad, yad, etad, idam, adas, eka, dvi, yuṣmad, asmad, bhavatu, kim \|

*sarvādīni = śabda-svarūpāṇi*

The word-forms given in the list of *sarvādi* group are to be designated as *sarvanāman* \|

The word *sarvādīni* is a *tad-guṇa-saṁvijñāna bahuvrīhi* compound which is analysed as follows: *sarvaḥ (śabdaḥ) ādir yeṣām śabda-svarūpānām tāni = sarvādīni*. Since the word *sarvādīni* being a *tad-guna-saṁvijñāna bahuvrīhi* is a qualifying term, it is expectant of *śabda-svarūpāṇi* in the form of a substantive to complete the sense in the present context. Thus the sūtra means as follows: The word-forms given in the list of sarvādi group are to be designated as sarvanāman \|

## 151 – jasaḥ śī \| 7.1.17

HNV: **312. kṛṣṇanāma-kṛṣṇato jasaḥ śīḥ**

**PP: ataḥ sarvanāmnaḥ aṅgāt jasaḥ \[ś\]ī**\|

*adantāt sarva-nāmno jasaḥ śī syāt \| anekāltvāt sarvādeśaḥ \| sarve \|*

After a pronominal stem ending in the short vowel *a*, let the substitute *śī* replace the *sup*-affix *jas* \|

The rule *jasaḥ śī* is not in the governing domain of *pratyayaḥ* Pā. 3.1.1. Therefore, the substitute *śī* does not get the *pratyaya*-designation and as a consequence the initial palatal *ś* of *śī* does not become *it* by *la-śa-kv ataddhite* (135). As such, being *anekāl*, the substitute *śī* replaces the whole of *jas*. After replacement, the substitute *śi* gets the *pratyaya*- designation by virtue of the rule *sthānivad ādeśo 'nalvidhau* (143). Now being an initial phoneme of a non-taddhita affix, it gets the *it*-designation and is consequently replaced by lopa in accordance with *tasya lopaḥ* (3). Only *ī* remains \|

The declined forms of the pronominal stem of *sarva* with the *sup*- affixes of the second and third triplets are: *sarvam, sarvau, sarvān*. *sarveṇa, sarvābhyām, sarvaiḥ*. The grammatical process of their declension does not differ from that which is followed in declining the corresponding forms of the nominal stem *rāma* with the affixes of the second and third triplets. When we introduce the singular *sup*-affix *ṅe* of the fourth triplet, we have *sarva + ṅe*. At this stage, the following rule becomes available for application, since the word *sarva* is sarvanāman by *sarvādīni sarvanāmani* (150):

## 152 – sarvanāmnaḥ smai \| 7.1.14

ataḥ sarvanāmno ṅeḥ smai \| sarvasmai \|

Let the substitute *smai* replace the substituendum *ṅe*, if it follows the pronominal stem ending in a short vowel *a* \|

## Appendix I – Terms

\[**ak**\]

a, i, u, ṛ, ḷ

JG: daśāvatārāḥ

\[**ac**\]

all vowels

JG: sarveśvaraḥ

anya: svaraḥ

\[**aṭ**\]

all vowels + h, y, v, r

\[**aṇ**\]

a, i, u

\[**aṇ**\]

all vowels + h, y, v, r, l

\[**am**\]

all vowels + h, y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n

\[**al**\]

whole alphabet

\[**aś**\]

all vowels + h, y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh,ḍh, dh

\[**ik**\]

i, u, ṛ, ḷ

\[**ic**\]

i, u, ṛ, ḷ, e, o, ai, au

\[**iṇ**\]

i, u, ṛ, ḷ, e, o, ai, au, h, y, v, r, l

\[**it**\]

indicatory letter

\[**uk**\]

u, ṛ, ḷ

\[**eṅ**\]

e, o

\[**ec**\]

e, o, ai, au

\[**aic**\]

ai, au

\[**khay**\]

kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p

\[**khar**\]

kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ, s

\[**ṅam**\]

ṅ, ṇ, n

\[**car**\]

c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ, s

\[**chay**\]

c, ṭ, t, k, p

\[**chav**\]

ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t

\[**jaś**\]

j, b, g, ḍ, d

\[**jhay**\]

jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p

\[**jhar**\]

jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ, s

\[**jhal**\]

jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ, s, h

\[**jhaś**\]

jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d

\[**jhaṣ**\]

jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh

\[**tiṅ**\]

endings of ten tenses

\[**pada**\]

1.1.14 \| let a word that terminates in *sup* or *tiṅ* be designated as *pada* (inflected word)

\[**prātipadika**\]

nominal stem

\[**pratyāhāra**\]

(*pratyāhriyante saṅkṣipyante varṇāḥ*) means a comprehension of several letters into one syllable. In other words, to frame pratyāhāra –

**(i)** there must be an *it* at the end,

**(ii)** beginning or initial letter may be any one of the prior letters excluding the letter that termed as *it*

**(iii)** intervening letters are to be present in between the initial and final *it* and

**(iv)** intervening *it* letters are not to be taken into account. For example – the intervening *it* letters – *ṇ*, *k*, *ṅ* in *ac pratyāhāra* are to be deleted and the vowels are taken into account (*pratyāhāreṣvitāṁ na grahaṇam*) \|

*Pratyāhāra* literally means “withdrawal.” In grammar, however, the term *pratyāhāra* refers to the abbreviation of a series of *varṇas* into one syllable by combining the first member with the indicatory letter of the last member.

\[**baś**\]

b, g, ḍ, d

\[**bhaṣ**\]

bh, gh, ḍh, dh

\[**may**\]

m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p

\[**yañ**\]

y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh

\[**yaṇ**\]

y, v, r, l

\[**yam**\]

y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n

\[**yay**\]

y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p

\[**yar**\]

y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ, s (all consonants except *h*)

\[**ra**\]

can be *r* and *l*

\[**ra-para**\]

ar, ir, ur

\[**ral**\]

r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ, s, h

\[**lapara**\]

al, il, ul

\[**val**\]

v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d, kh, ph, ch, ṭh, th, c, ṭ, t, k, p, ś, ṣ, s, h

\[**vaś**\]

v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh, ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, d

\[**śar**\]

ś, ṣ, s

\[**śal**\]

ś, ṣ, s, h

\[**saṁyoga**\]

1.1.7 \| the coalition of consonants, separated by the intervening vowels, is to be termed as *saṁyoga* or conjunction

\[**saṁhitā**\]

1.4.109 \| euphony or *sandhi*

\[**sup**\]

case endings

\[**haś**\]

h, y, v, r, l, ñ, m, ṅ, ṇ, n, jh, bh, gh,ḍh, dh, j, b, g, ḍ, b

\[**hal**\]

all consonants

## Appendix II – saṁjña-sūtrāṇi

**1 – hal antyam** \| 1.3.3 \| **upadeśe antyaṁ hal it (syāt)** \| In the event of an ***upadeśa*** or specification, the final consonant is to be termed as ***it*** \|

**2 – adarśanaṁ lopaḥ** \| 1.1.60 \| **sthānasya adarśanam lopaḥ** \| A letter which is apparently present in articulation and subsequently disappears, that is to be termed as ***lopa*** or elision with substitution of a blank \|

**4 – ādir antyena sahetā** \| 1.1.71 \| **ādiḥ antyena itā saha svasya**\|An initial letter conjoining with a final *it* of the *Māheśvara sūtra*, let be the name or definition of the initial itself and the intervening letters \|

**5 – ūkālo 'jjhrasva-dīrgha-plutaḥ** \| 1.2.27 \| **ū-kālaḥ ac hrasva-dīrgha-plutaḥ** \| Basing on the prosodial length of the *ū*, the vowels, whose time of articulation is equal to that of one unit, two units & three units, are to be designated as short, long & prolated respectively \|

**6 – uccair udāttaḥ** \| 1.2.29 \| That (a vowel) which is pronounced higher (with high pitch) at its place of articulation is termed *udātta*, high-pitched \|

**7 – nīcair anudāttaḥ** \| 1.2.30 \| That (a vowel) which is pronounced lower (with low pitch) at its place of articulation is termed *anuudātta*, low-pitched \|

**8 – samāhāraḥ svaritaḥ** \| 1.2.31 \| **udāttasya anudāttasya samāhāraḥ svaritaḥ**\| A vowel which is pronounced with a combination of features of *udātta* and *anudātta* is termed *svarita* \|

**9 – mukha-nāsikā-vacano 'nunāsikaḥ** \| 1.1.8 \| A sound pronounced at once in the nose and the mouth is termed *anunāsika* "nasal" \|

**10 – tulyāsya prayatnaṁ savarṇam** \| 1.1.9 \| Those letters are called reciprocally Homogeneous when their place of origin and internal effort are almost the same \|

**12 – paraḥ sannikarṣaḥ saṁhitā** \| 1.4.109 \| The closest proximity of letters is termed as *saṁhitā* or Euphony.

**13 – halo 'nantarāḥ saṁyogaḥ** \| 1.1.7 \| The coalition of consonants, separated by the intervening vowels, is to be termed as *saṁyoga* or Conjunction.

**14 – sup-tiṅ-antaṁ padam** \| 1.4.14 \| Let a word that terminates in *sup* or *tiṅ* be designated as *pada* (inflected word)\|

**25 – ad eṅ guṇaḥ**\| 1.1.2 \| Let *at* (*a*) and *eṅ* (*e* and *o*) be designated as guṇa\|

**32 – vṛddhir ād aic**\| 1.1.1 \| The *ā, ai, au* are termed as vṛddhi\|

## Appendix III – paribhāsā-sūtrāṇi

**16 – tasminn iti nirdiṣṭe pūrvasya**\| 1.1.66\|When a term is exhibited in the seventh case the operation directed is to be understood as affecting the state of what immediately precedes that which the term denotes.

**17 – sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ**\| 1.1.50 \| In place of (*sthāne*) a letter when more than one term arises as substitute, a homogeneous (*antaratamaḥ*) letter is to be substituted there.

**21 – alo 'ntyasya**\| 1.1.52 \| A substitution (operation) takes place against the final letter, if that is directed in term of the genitive case\|
