## Appendix V – list of sūtras

**(1) a i uṇ**

**(2) ṛ ḷk**

**(3) e oṅ**

**(4) ai auc**

**(5) ha ya va raṭ**

**(6) laṇ**

**(7) ña ma ṅa ṇa nam**

**(8) jha bhañ**

**(9) gha ḍha dhaṣ**

**(10) ja ba ga ḍa daś**

**(11) kha pha cha ṭha tha ca ṭa tav**

**(12) ka pay**

**(13) śa ṣa sar**

**(14) hal**

**1 – hal antyam** \| 1.3.3 \| **upadeśe antyaṁ hal it (syāt)** \| In the event of an ***upadeśa*** or specification, the final consonant is to be termed as ***it*** \|

**2 – adarśanaṁ lopaḥ** \| 1.1.60 \| **sthānasya adarśanam lopaḥ** \| A letter which is apparently present in articulation and subsequently disappears, that is to be termed as ***lopa*** or elision with substitution of a blank \|

**3 – tasya lopaḥ** \| 1.3.9 \| **tasya itaḥ lopaḥ**\| The letter which is termed as *it*, let there be an elision or *lopa* by deleting the it \|

**4 – ādir antyena sahetā** \| 1.1.71 \| **ādiḥ antyena itā saha svasya**\|An initial letter conjoining with a final *it* of the *Māheśvara sūtra*, let be the name or definition of the initial itself and the intervening letters \|

**5 – ū-kālo 'jjhrasva-dīrgha-plutaḥ** \| 1.2.27 \| **ū-kālaḥ ac hrasva-dīrgha-plutaḥ** \| Basing on the prosodial length of the *ū*, the vowels, whose time of articulation is equal to that of one unit, two units & three units, are to be designated as short, long & prolated respectively \|

**6 – uccair udāttaḥ** \| 1.2.29 \| That (a vowel) which is pronounced higher (with high pitch) at its place of articulation is termed *udātta*, high-pitched \|

**7 – nīcair anudāttaḥ** \| 1.2.30 \| That (a vowel) which is pronounced lower (with low pitch) at its place of articulation is termed *anuudātta*, low-pitched \|

**8 – samāhāraḥ svaritaḥ** \| 1.2.31 \| **udāttasya anudāttasya samāhāraḥ svaritaḥ**\| A vowel which is pronounced with a combination of features of *udātta* and *anudātta* is termed *svarita* \|

**9 – mukha-nāsikā-vacano 'nunāsikaḥ** \| 1.1.8 \| A sound pronounced at once in the nose and the mouth is termed *anunāsika* "nasal" \|

**10 – tulyāsya-prayatnaṁ savarṇam** \| 1.1.9 \| Those letters are called reciprocally Homogeneous when their place of origin and internal effort are almost the same \|

**11 – aṇ-udit-savarṇasya cā'pratyayaḥ** \| 1.1.69 \| **aṇ udit savarṇasya ca apratyayaḥ svasya** \| Let a letter, denoted by the *pratyāhāra* *aṇ*, not propounded as an affix or operative agent, but as something to be operated upon, and let in like manner a letter followed by an indicatory *u*, be the name of (and so imply) its homogeneous letters also \|

**12 – paraḥ sannikarṣaḥ saṁhitā** \| 1.4.109 \| The closest proximity of letters is termed as *saṁhitā* or Euphony.

**13 – halo 'nantarāḥ saṁyogaḥ** \| 1.1.7 \| The coalition of consonants, not separated by the intervening vowels, is to be termed as *saṁyoga* or Conjunction.

**14 – sup-tiṅ-antaṁ padam** \| 1.4.14 \| Let a word that terminates in *sup* or *tiṅ* be designated as *pada* (inflected word).

**sandhi-prakaraṇam**

ac-sandhi (vowel sandhi)

**15 – iko yaṇ aci** \| 6.1.77 \|In place of a letter of *ik* *pratyāhāra*, let there be a letter substituted by *yaṇ* *pratyāhāra*, if a letter of *ac pratyāhāra* follows \| **ik:** *i, u, ṛ, ḷ;* **yaṇ:***y, v, r*, *l;* **ac:** vowel \| ***sudhī*****(the intelligent) +** ***upāsyaḥ*** **(to be worshipped) --\>** ***sudhy*** ***upāsyaḥ***

**16 – tasminn iti nirdiṣṭe pūrvasya**\| 1.1.66\|When a term is stated in the locative case, let the grammatical operation directed by it be performed on that which precedes the term without being separated even by a single phoneme.

**17 – sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ**\| 1.1.50 \| In place of (*sthāne*) a letter when more than one term arises as substitute, a homogeneous (*antaratamaḥ*) letter is to be substituted there.

**18 – an-aci ca**\| 8.4.47 \| **acaḥ yaraḥ dve vā an-aci ca (saṁhitāyām) \|**A letter belongs to *yar* is to be reduplicated optionally, if that comes after a letter of *ac*, but not if a vowel follows.

**19 – jhalāṁ jaś jhaśi**\| 8.4.53 \| A letter of *jaś* is to be the substitute of a letter of *jhal*, if a letter of *jhaś* follows.

**20 – saṁyogāntasya lopaḥ**\| 8.2.23 \| Let *lopa* be substituted in place of the *pada* which ends in a conjunct (*saṁyoga*)\|

**21 – alo 'ntyasya**\| 1.1.52 \| **alaḥ antasya saṣṭhī sthāne** \| A substitution (operation) takes place against the final letter, if that is directed in term of the genitive case\|

**22 – eco 'y av āy āvaḥ**\| 6.1.78 \| **ecaḥ ay av āy āvaḥ aci saṁhitāyām** \| When followed by *ac*, let the elements (*e o ai au*) denoted by *ec* be replaced by *ay av āy āv* respectively\|

**23 – yathā saṅkhyam anudeśaḥ samānām**\| 1.3.10 \| **samānām anudeśaḥ yathā saṅkhyam** \|When a rule involves in an equal fitness of both the place and the substitution, that is to be executed in order of enumeration\|

**24 – vānto yi pratyaye**\| 6.1.79 \| *av* & *āv* are to be the substitute in place of *o* & *au* respectively, if an affix initiated by the *y* immediately follows\| **go + yam = gavyam**\|

**25 – ad eṅ guṇaḥ**\| 1.1.2 \|Let *at* (*a*) and *eṅ* (*e* and *o*) be designated as *guṇa*\|

**26 – ta-paras tat-kālasya**\| 1.1.70 \| Let a vowel followed by the letter t, and a vowel following the letter t, be the name only of the letter which has the same prosodial length\|

**27 – ād guṇaḥ**\| 6.1.87 \| **āt aci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ guṇaḥ saṁhitayam** \| After the *a* or *ā* if a vowel follows, a *guṇa* (*a, e* or *o*) is to be the single substitute for both the prior and the subsequent\| Effectively, the sūtra means that when *a* is followed by *i u ṛ ḷ*, both the preceding and following vowels are replaced by a *guṇa* vowel \| **upa + indra, gaṅgā + udaka**\|

**28 – upadeśe 'janunāsika it**\| 1.3.2 \| **upadeśe ac anunāsikaḥ it** \| In an *upadeśa* (specification), a vowel uttered with the association of the nose is to be termed as *it* (abolition)\|

**29 – ur aṇ ra-paraḥ**\| 1.1.51 \| **uḥ \[of the ṛ\] sthāne aṇ ra-paraḥ** \|In the alliance of the letters pertaining to *aṇ* with the *ṛ* (as well as the homogeneous *ḷ*), that induces the *aṇ* as *ra-para* (as well as *la-para*)\| **kṛṣṇa + ṛddhiḥ = kṛṣṇarddhiḥ**(prosperity of kṛṣṇa); **tava + ḷ-kāraḥ = tavalkāraḥ**\|

**30 – lopaḥ śākalyasya**\| 8.3.19 \| **a-pūrvayoḥ padāntayoḥ v-yoḥ lopaḥ śākalyasya aśi (saṁhitāyām)** \| In difference to the opinion of *Śākalya* there be the elision of the letters *y* & *v* optionally, if – (i) ends in a *pada*; (ii) preceded by the letter *a* or *ā* and (iii) followed by a letter pertaining to to *aś* pratyāhāra\| **hare + iha =\> (22) haray + iha (30) =\> hara iha**

**31 – pūrvatrāsiddham**\| 8.2.1 \|The triad of the last three quarters of the *Aṣṭādhyayī* is considered inoperative (*asiddha*) with respect to the seven chapters and a quarter, i. e. that which precedes (this sūtra) and in the triad also the following aphorism is considered inoperative with respect to the ones that precede\|

**32 – vṛddhir ād aic**\| 1.1.1 \| The *ā, ai, au* are termed as *vṛddhi*\|

**33 – vṛddhir eci**\| 6.1.88 \| **āt eci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ vṛddhiḥ saṁhitāyām \|** A *vṛddhi* is substituted for both, if a letters pertaining to *ec* comes after the *a* or *ā*\| kṛṣṇa + ekatvam --\> kṛṣṇ a + e katvam (17, *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> kṛṣṇ ai katvam --\> kṛṣṇaikatvam (oneness with Kṛṣṇa) \|

**34 – ety edhaty ūṭhsu**\| 6.1.89 \| **āt eci eti-edhati-ūṭhsu pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ vṛddhiḥ saṁhitāyām** \| After the *a* or *ā*, if the words *eti* & *edhati* initiated by a letter of *ec* (*e*) and also the word *ūṭh* (initiated by the '*ū*') follow, let a *vṛddhi* be the single substitute for both \| upaiti (upa + eti): up a + e ti (17: *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> up ai ti --\> upaiti(he comes near) \| if the word *ūhaḥ*, beginning with the *ū*, comes after *a* or *ā*, the coalition is to be substituted by a *vṛddhi* in stead of a *guṇa* \| As for example *praṣṭhauhaḥ*(praṣṭha + ūhaḥ): praṣṭh a + ū haḥ (33: *vṛddhir eci*, 17: *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> praṣṭh au haḥ --\> praṣthauhaḥ (a young steer training for plough) \| upetaḥ (upa + itaḥ): up a + i taḥ (27, 17) --\> up e taḥ --\> upetaḥ(approached) \| pra + ididhat --\> pr a + i didhat (27, 17) pr e didhat --\> predidhat, mā bhavān predidhat (let not your honour promote) \|

**I** (vā.) - **(akṣād ūhinyām upasaṅkhyānam**) \| akṣauhiṇī senā \| (akṣād ūhinyām + upasaṅkhyānam) \| it is to be added that, if the word *ūhiṇī* comes after the word *akṣa* (ending in *a*), let there be the substitute of a *vṛddhi* for both, instead of a *guṇa*\| akṣa + ūhinī senā --\> akṣ a + ū hinī senā (17) --\> akṣ au hinī senā --\> akṣauhinī senā (246: purva-padāt saṁjñāyāmagaḥ) --\> aksauhiṇī senā

**II** (vā.) - **(prād ūhoḍhoḍhyeṣaiṣy eṣu) \| prauhaḥ \| prauḍhaḥ \| prauḍhiḥ \| praiṣaḥ \| praiṣyaḥ \|** If the words *ūhaḥ, ūḍhaḥ, ūḍhiḥ, eṣaḥ* & *eṣyaḥ* beginning with *ū* & *e* come after the preposition *pra* (ending in *a*), let there be the substitution of a *vṛddhi* instead of a *guṇa*\| pra + ūhaḥ --\> pr a + ū haḥ (17: *sthāne 'ntaratamaḥ*) --\> pr au haḥ --\> prauhaḥ

**III** (vā.) - **(ṛte ca tṛtīyā samāse) \| sukhena ṛtaḥ sukhārtaḥ \| tṛtīyeti kim? paramartaḥ** \| in the instrumental compound (*tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa samāsa),* if the word *ṛta* beginning with *ṛ* (at the beginning of th2 2ndmember) follows the *a* (at the end of the 1stmember), a *vṛddhi* is to be the substitute for both, instead of a *guṇa*\| sukhena ṛtaḥ sukhārtaḥ: sukha ṭā su (*supodhātu-pratipadikayoḥ: vibhakti lopaḥ*) sukha ṛta --\> sukh a ṛ ta (*a vṛddhi ār* is to substituted instead of a *guṇa ar*) sukh a ār ta --\> sukhārta, with case (*vibhakti*) termination --\> sukhārtaḥ

**IV** (vā.) - **(pra-vatsatara-kambala-vasanārṇa-daśānām ṛṇe) \| prārṇam, vatsatarārṇam \| kambalārnam \| vasanārṇam \| ṛṇārṇam \| daśārṇam** \| The word *ṛṇam* beginning with *ṛ* if comes after the words *pra, vatsatara, kambala, vasana, ṛṇa* and *daśa* (ending in *a*) a *vṛddhi* instead of a *guṇa*, is to be substitute for both\|pra + ṛṇam --\> pr a + ṛ ṇam --\> prārṇam(principal debt)

**35 – upasargāḥ kriyā-yoge**\| 1.4.59 \| The series mentioned in *pra* and so on (*prādigaṇa*), when prefixed to a dhātu are to be termed as *upasarga* (prepositions)\|

**36 – bhū-vādayo dhātavaḥ**\| 1.3.1 \| The verbal roots – *bhvādayaḥ* (*bhū*, *vā* etc.) signifying the sense of an action (*kriyā*) are to be termed as *dhātu* (verbal roots)\|

**37 – upasargād ṛti dhātau**\| 6.1.91 \| **āt upasargāt ṛti dhātau pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ vṛddhiḥ (saṁhitāyām) \|** When a dhātu initiated by the *ṛ* follows *upasarga* ending in *a* or *ā*, a *vṛddhi* instead of a *guṇa*, is to be the substitute for both\|

pra + ṛcchati\| *vṛddhi ār* is to be the substitute fo both: prārcchati(he goes swiftly)

**38 – eṅi para-rūpam**\| 6.1.94 \| **āt upasargāt eṅi dhātau pūrva-parayoḥ ekam para-rūpam (saṁhitāyām) \|** If a *dhātu* initiated by a letter of *eṅ* comes after an *upasarga* ending in *a* or *ā*, the subsequent (*para-rūpa*) *e* or *o* is substituted for both\| pra + ejate --\> prejate; upa + oṣate --\> upoṣate; pra + eṣayati --\> preṣayati \|

**39 – aco 'ntyādi ṭi**\| 1.1.64\|Let the final portion of a word, beginning with the last of the vowels in the word, be called *ṭi*\|

**śakandhvādiṣu para-rūpa tacca ṭeḥ \|** In the words pertaining to *śakandhvādi-gaṇa* viz. *śakandhuḥ, karkandhuḥ, manīṣā, patañjaliḥ* etc. the substitution is to be the *para-rūpa* (subsequent one) \| śaka + andhuḥ --\> śak a + a ndhuḥ --\> the ṭi (final *a* of *śaka*) and the subsequent *a* of *andhuḥ* are to be substituted the *para-rūpa* or subsequent *a* --\> śak a ndhuḥ --\> śakandhuḥ \|

**40 – omāṅoś ca**\| 6.1.95 **\| āt omāṅoḥ ca pūrva-parayoḥ ekaṁ para-rūpam (saṁhitāyām) \|** If the mystic syllable *om* and the preposition – *āṅ* (*ā*) come after the *a* or *ā*, the subsequent letter or *para-rūpa* is to be the substitute for both \|

śivāya + om namaḥ --\> śivāyoṁ namaḥ; śiva + ā + ihi --\> śiva + ehi --\> *to be continue*

**41 – antādivacca**\| 6.1.85 \| **ekaḥ pūrva-parayoḥ antādivat ca** \| And when the mystic syllable *om*, or the *upasarga* *āṅ* follows *a* or *ā*, let the single substitute be the form of the subsequent \| śiva + ā + ihi --\> śiva + ehi --\> śivehi

**42 – akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ**\| 6.1.101\|**akaḥ savarṇe aci pūrva-parayoh ekaḥ dīrghaḥ (saṁhitāyām) \|** If *ak* is followed by a homogeneous *ac*, let a single replacement consisting of a long vowel come in place of the preceding and the following vowels\|

**43 – eṅaḥ padāntād ati**\| 6.1.109 \| **padāntāt eṅaḥ ati pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrvaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \| If the short *a* comes after a letter of the *eṅ* ending in a *pada*, the prior letter (*pūrva-rūpa*) is to be the substitute for both\|

**44 – sarvatra vibhāṣāḥ goḥ**\| 6.1.122 **\| sarvatra padāntasya eṅaḥ goḥ ati vibhāṣā prakṛtya (saṁhitāyām) \|** Both in the Secular and Scriptural Sanskrit usage, if a short vowel *a* comes after the word *go* ending in *eṅ* (*o*), let the euphony be optionally restricted there\| go + agram --\> go agram (multitude of cows)

**45 – anekāl śit sarvasya**\| 1.1.55 \| A substitution containing more than one letter in general and the *śit* (ś + *it*) in particular, if directed against a word in genitive case, that is to be replaced against the whole word\|

**46 – ṅic ca**\| 1.1.53 \| If a substitution consisting of more than one letter with the final *ṅ* as *it*, that is directed to be replaced against the final letter\| *avaṅ*is a substitute consisting of more than one letter with the final *ṅ* as an *it* and if directed to be replaced against the word *go* (*g o*), that is to be substituted in place of the final *o*, but not against the entire *pada go* as directed in anekāl *śit sarvasya*\|

**47 – avaṅ sphoṭāyanasya**\| 6.1.133 \| **eṅaḥ padāntasya goḥ avaṅ sphoṭāyanasya aci vibhāṣā (saṁhitāyām) \|**(in difference to the opinion of sage *Sphoṭāyana*) Let the substitute *avaṅ* optionally replace the nominal stem *go* ending in *eṅ* (*o*) occurring at the end of a pada when *ac* follows\| *go + agram*--\> g o + agram (46: *ṅic ca*) --\> g avaṅ + agram (ṅ-it) --\> g ava + agram --\> gava + agram --\> gav a + a gram (42) --\> gavāgram

**48 – indre ca**\| 6.1.124 \| **eṅaḥ goḥ indre ca avaṅ (saṁhitāyām) \|** In place of the final *o* of the word *go* let there be the substitution of *avaṅ* if the word *indraḥ* follows\| *gavendraḥ*

**49 – dūrād dhūte ca**\| 8.2.84 \| **durāt hūte ca vākyasya ṭeḥ plutaḥ vā \|** In calling or addressing a person from a distant place, in place of the final vowel (*ṭi*) of a sentence the substitution of prolated modification (*pluta*) is optional \|

**50 – pluta-pragṛhyā aci nityam**\| 6.1.125 \| **pluta-pragṛhyā aci nityam prakṛtyā (saṁhitāyām)** \| If a vowel follows a prolated vowel (*pluta*) or an excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*), that remains invariably unaltered (not liable to be applied by the rules of euphony)\| *āgaccha kṛṣṇa3 atra gauś carati*

**51 – īd-ūd-ed dvi-vacanaṁ pragṛhyam**\| 1.1.11 \| A dual case-termination ending in the long form viz. ī ū or e is termed as *pragṛhya* or excepted vowel\| kavī āgacchataḥ, ṛtu atitau (two seasons surpassed), bālike adhiyāte (two girls are reading)\|

**52 – adaso māt**\| 1.1.12 \| **adasaḥ māt īd-ūt pragṛhyam** \| In the case termination of the pronoun *adas*, the long *ī* and *ū* are termed as *pragṛhya* when they come after the *m*\| amī + īśāḥ --\> amī īśāḥ, rāma-kṛṣṇau amū āsāte\|

**53 – cādayo 'sattve**\| 1.4.57 \| The indeclinables in the *cādi-gaṇa* viz. *ca, va, paśu* etc. are termed as *nipāta* (a particle), if they denote the meanings of a *dravya* (an animate)\|

**54 – prādayaḥ**\| 1.4.58 \| **asattve prādayaḥ nipātāḥ** \| The *upasarga* (prepositions) in the *prādigaṇa* viz. *pra, parā, āṅ* etc. are termed as *nipāta* (a particle) if they don't denote the meanings of an animate

**55 – nipāta ekājanāṅ**\| 1.1.14 \| **anāṅ ekāc nipātaḥ pragṛhyaḥ** \| Excluding the *āṅ* (*ā*), a single lettered vowel pertaining to the series of *cādigaṇa* (indeclinables), is to be termed as *nipāta* and subsequently called as an Excepted Vowel or *pragṛhya*\| i + indraḥ, u + umeśa

**56 – ot**\| 1.1.15 \| **ot nipātaḥ pragṛhyaḥ** \| The indeclinable consisting in the series of the *cādi-gaṇa* if ending in *o*, that is termed as an excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*)\| *o, āho, ūtāho, ho, aho* etc. \| āho + īśāḥ --\> āho īśāḥ

**57 – sambuddhau śākalyasyetāv anārṣe**\| 1.1.16\|**sambuddhau ot anārṣe itau pragṛhyaḥ śākalyasya \|** In difference to the opinion of the sage *śakalya*, in the secular usage, the final *o* in a vocation (*sambudhi*) shall optionally be termed a excepted vowel (*pragṛhya*), if the indeclinable *iti* follows \| viṣṇo iti

**58 – maya uño vo vā**\| 8.3.33 \| **mayaḥ uñaḥ vaḥ vā aci saṁhitāyām** \|If the *uñ* (*u*) comes after a letter of *may* the substitution of *v* in place of the *uñ* (*u*) is to be the optional, if a letter of *ac* follows\| kim + uñ + uktam --\> kim + uñ + uktam --\> kim + u + uktam --\> in this situation the *uñ* (*u*) comes after *m* and thereafter the vowel *u* of *uktam* comes after the *uñ* \| According to the present rule, the *v* is to be substitute in the place of *uñ* (*u*), as kim + u + uktam --\> kim + v + uktam --\> kimvuktam

**59 – iko 'savarṇe 'ci śākalyasya hrasvaś ca**\| 6.1.127 \| **padāntasya ikaḥ asarvarṇe aci hrasvaḥ śākalyasya ca (saṁhitāyām)** \| In place of a (long) vowel pertaining to the *ik* ends in a *pada*, a short vowel is to be optionally substituted if a non-homogenic vowel follows \| cakrī + atra --\> cakri atra

**na samāse**(vā.) \| The option of shortening the a vowel is restricted in a *samasa \|* vāpyām + aśvaḥ --\> vipī + aśvaḥ --\> vāpyaśvaḥ

**60 – aco ra-hābhyāṁ dve**\| 8.4.46 **\| acaḥ ra-hābhyāṁ dve yaraḥ vā (saṁhitāyām) \|** A consonant denoted by the abbreviatory term *yar*, immediately occurring after *r* or *h* which again are preceded by a vowel, is optionally reduplicated \| gaury + au, in this situation (i) the *r* comes after the the vowel *au*, (ii) the *y* a letter of the *yar* just follows the *r* \| Hence the y is to optionally reduplicated as g au r y y + au --\> gaur y y + au --\> gauryyau

**61 – ṛty akaḥ**\| 6.1.128 \| The option of shortening a vowel is employed against a long form of *ak* if that is ending in a *pada* and a short *ṛ* follows immediately \| brahmā + ṛṣi --\> brahma ṛṣi or brahmarṣi

**hal-sandhi-prakaraṇam**

**62 – s-toḥ ś-cunā ś-cuḥ**\| 8.4.40\|**s-toḥ ś-cunā ś-cuḥ (saṁhitāyām)**\|In the coalition of the dental group of letters (*s, t, th, d, dh & n*) with the palatal group of letters (*ś, c, ch, j, jh & ñ*), the substitution is to be the palatal in place of the dentals\|

rāmas + śete --\> rāmaś śete \| rāmas + cinoti --\> rāmaś cinoti \| sat + cit --\> sac cit \| śārṅgin + jaya --\> śārṅgiñjaya

**63 – śāt**\| 8.4.44 **\| śat toḥ na (saṁhitāyām) \|** After the palatal *ś* if a letter of *ta-varga* follows, the palatal – *ca-varga* is not to be the substitute of the dental – *ta-varga*\| viś + na --\> viśna \|

**64 – ṣṭunā ṣṭuḥ**\| 8.4.41 \| **stoḥ ṣṭunā ṣṭuḥ (saṁhitāyām) \|** Let *s* and the dental stops (*tu*) when in contact with *ṣ* and retroflex stops (*ṭu*) be replaced by the latter\| rāmaḥ + ṭīkate --\> rāmaṣṭīkate \| garutmān + ḍīyate --\> garutmāṇḍīyate \|

**65 – na padāntāṭ ṭor anām**\| 8.4.42\|**na padāntāṭ ṭoḥ anām s-toḥ ṣ-ṭuḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \| After *ṭu* final in a *pada* the change of a dental to a cerebral, except in the case of the affix *nām*, shall not take place\| ṣaṭ + santaḥ --\> ṣaṭsantaḥ \|

**The substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is to be restricted if a letter of** ***s*** **or** ***tu*** **comes after a letter of** ***ṭu*** **(excluding the** ***ṣ*****).**In case of the *ṣ* followed by a letter of *s* or *tu*, the substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is not to be restricted. Such as *sarpiṣ + tamam --\> sarpiṣṭamam*\|

**X – anām navati nagarīṇām iti vācyam**\|

It is expounded by sage *Kātyāyana* that the affix – *nām* and the words – *navati* and *nagarī* (those begin with *n* of *tu*) if come after the *ṭu* ending in an inflected word, the substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is to be imperative\| ṣaṇṇavatiḥ \| ṣaṇṇagaryyaḥ \|

**66 – toḥ ṣi**\| 8.4.43 \| **toḥ ṣi na ṣṭutvam (saṁhitāyām)** \| The substitution of a cerebral in place of a dental is to be prevented, if the cerebral – *ṣ* comes after a *tu*\| san + ṣaṣṭhaḥ --\> sanṣaṣṭhaḥ\| Extended: When the cerebral sibilant *ṣ* follows, the *pada*-final dental stops are not replaced by the cerebral stops \|

**67 – jhalāṁ jaśo 'nte**\| 8.2.39 **\| padantasya ante jhalām jaśaḥ \|** A letter of *jaś* is to be the substitute of a letter of *jhal* ending in an inflected word\| vāk + īśaḥ --\> vāgīśaḥ \| manāk + hasati --\> manāghasati \| cit + ananda --\> cidananda \| madhuliṭ + guñjatu --\> madhuliḍguñjatu \| kukubh + īśaḥ --\> kukubīśaḥ \|

**68 – yaro 'nunāsike 'nunāsiko vā**\| 8.4.45 **\| padāntasya yaraḥ anunāsike anunāsikaḥ vā (saṁhitāyām)** \| In place of *pada*-final *yar*, a nasal is substituted optionally, if a nasal follows immediately \| etad + murāriḥ --\> etan murāriḥ or etad murāriḥ\|

(G) r, ś, ṣ, s don't have their nasal modifications \| As result there will be no changes \|

**vā. pratyaye bhāṣāyāṁ nityam**\| In the usage of the secular language, the substitution of a nasal in place of *yar* is obligatory, if an affix (initiated by a nasal) follows\| tat + mātram --\> tad + mātram --\> tanmātram\|

**69 – tor li**\| 8.4.60 \| **toḥ li para-savarṇaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \| The substitution is to be the *para-sa-varṇa* (next homogeneous) in place of *tu*, if a word initiated by the *l* follows\|tat + layaḥ --\> tad + layaḥ --\> tallayḥ

vidvān + likhati --\> vidvāl (nasal) likhati (*na-kārasyā 'nunāsiko la-kāraḥ*)

**70 – udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya**\| 8.4.61**\| udaḥ sthā-stambhoḥ pūrvasya savarṇaḥ (saṁhitāyām)**\|After the preposition *ud*, in place of the *dhātus* – *sthā*, *stambh* the substitution is to be the *pūrva-savarṇa*\|

**71 – tasmād ity uttarasya**\| 1.1.67 \| **tasmād iti nirdiṣṭe uttarasya \|** It is for apprehension that, in the indication of 5thcase inflection, the operation is executed in between the gapless following room \|

**72 – ādeḥ parasya**\| 1.1.54 \| **parasya ādeḥ alaḥ sthāne \|** A substitute that has been enjoined to replace that which follows, should replace the initial phoneme of the following\|

**73 – jharo jhari savarṇe**\| 8.4.65 \| **PP: halaḥ jharaḥ lopaḥ anyatarasyām savarṇe jhari (saṁhitāyām) \|** After a *hal*, a *jhar* is optionally elided, if a homogeneous *jhar* follows \| ud + ththānam --\> ud + thānam \| ud + thtambhanam --\> ud + tambhanam \|

**74 – khari ca**\| 8.4.55 \| **khari ca jhalāṁ caraḥ (saṁhitāyām) \|** In place of *jhal*, the substitution is to be *car*, if *khar* follows\| *ut + ththānam --\> uttthānam*\| *ut + thtambhanam --\> utthambhanam* \|

**75 – jhayo ho 'nyatarasyām**\| 8.4.62 \| **jhayaḥ haḥ anyatarasyām pūrvasya savarṇaḥ (saṁhitāyām) \|** In place of the *h*, if that comes after *jhay*, the *pūrva-savarṇa* \[harighoṣaḥ\]is substituted optionally\| ac + hīnam (67, *jhalaṁ jaśo 'nte*) --\> aj + hīnam (75, *jhayo ho 'nyatarasyām*) --\> aj + jhīnam --\> ajjhīnam or achīnam (devoid of a vowel) \|

**76 – śaś cho 'ṭi**\| 8.4.63 \| **jhayaḥ śaḥ chaḥ anyatarasyām aṭi (saṁhitāyām)** \| (K) Let *ch* optionally replace *ś* which follows *jhay* \[ending in a *pada*\]and which is followed by aṭ\| tad + śivaḥ (62: *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) --\> taj + śivaḥ (74: *khari ca*) --\> tac + śivaḥ (76: *śaś cho 'ṭi*) --\> tacchivaḥ\|

**vā. chatvam amīti vācyam \| tacchlokena** (*chatvam ami iti vācyam*) \| After *jhay* ending in a *pada*, the *ch* is to be the \[optional\]substitute in place of the initial *ś* followed by *am pratyāhāra*\| tad + ślokena (62: *stoḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) --\> taj + ślokena (74: *khari ca*) --\> tac + ślokena (76 vā. chatvam amīti vācyam) --\> tacchlokena (otherwise *tacṣlokena*)

**77 – mo 'nusvāraḥ**\| 8.3.23 \| **maḥ padasya anusvāraḥ hali (saṁhitāyām)** \| In place of the *m* ending in an inflected word, the substitution is to be the anusvara if *hal* follows\|

**78 – naś cāpadāntasya jhali**\| 8.3.24 \| **maḥ naḥ ca apadāntasya jhali anusvāraḥ (saṁhitāyām) \|** Also in place of the *n* & *m* of an *apadānta* the *anusvāra* is to be inflicted, if *jhal* follows \| yaśāṁsi \| ākraṁsyate \|

**79 – anusvārasya yayi para-savarṇaḥ**\| 8.4.58 \| In place of the *anusvāra*, the substitution is to be the *para-savarṇa* (subsequent homogeneous), if a letter of *yay* follows \| śām taḥ (76: śaś cho 'ṭi) --\> śāṁ taḥ (79, 17) śāntaḥ\|

**80 – vā padāntasya**\| 8.4.59 **\| vā padāntasya anusvārasya yayi para-savarṇaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \| In place of the *anusvāra*, ending in an inflected word, the option of substitution is to be the *para-savarṇa* (subsequent homogeneous), if a letter of *yay* follows \| tvaṅ karoṣi, tvaṁ karoṣi \|

**81 – mo rāji samaḥ kvau**\| 8.3.25 \| **samaḥ maḥ maḥ kvau rāji (saṁhitāyām)**

If the word *rāṭ* follows, the final *m* of the preposition *sam* remains as *m* (instead of the substitution of the *anusvāra* in place of the *m*) \| samrāṭ\|

**82 – he ma-pare vā**\| 8.3.26 \| **ma-pare he maḥ maḥ vā (saṁhitāyām)** \| The final *m* ending in a *pada* optionally remains as *m*, if the *h*, followed by the *m*, comes after it\| kim hmalayati or kiṁ hmalayati

**vā. ya-va-la-pare ya-va-lā vā \|** According to this supplementary Rule, if follows the *h* being followed by the semi vowels *y, v* or *l*, the final *m* ending in a *pada* is substituted by the nasal modification (the substitution of the *anusvāra* in place of the final *m* is restricted)\| ik$$$ma,+ ÷:--\> ik$$$ya.\*M ÷:orik$$$ma,+ ÷:--\> ik$M ÷:

**83 – na-pare naḥ**\| 8.3.27 **\|** (K) If the phoneme *m* is followed by *h* preceding *n*, then *m* is optionally replaced by the phoneme *n* \| kim + hnute --\> kin hnute or kiṁ hnute\|

**(84G) 86 – ḍaḥ si dhuṭ**\| 8.3.29 \| **ḍaḥ si dhuṭ vā (saṁhitayām) \|** *dh*\[*uṭ*\] is to be the optional *āgama* (augment) of the *s* if that comes after the *ḍ* \|

**84 – ādy-antau ṭa-k-itau**\| 1.1.46 *\|* Of whatsoever the *ṭit* and *kit* are enunciated, that is to be the initial and final *āgama* (augment) respectively. So to say, the enunciation of an *āgama* (augment) is to be distinguished by the indicator *ṭit* and *kit* \| liḍ + su --\> liṭtsu or liṭsu\|

**85 – ṅ-ṇoḥ kuk-ṭuk śari**\| 8.3.28 *\|* **ṅṇoḥ kuk-ṭuk śari vā (saṁhitāyām)** *\|* Of the *ṅa* and *ṇa* ending in a *pada*, the option of infliction is to be the *k*\[*uk*\] and *ṭ*\[*uk*\] as an *āgama* respectively, if a letter of *śar* follows \|

**vā. cayo dvitīyāḥ śari pauṣkaras āder iti vācyam \|** According to the predication of the succession of *Pauṣkaras*, in place of a letter of *cay* ending in a *pada*, the 2ndletter belongs to that class is to be the substitute, if a letter of *śar* follows\| prāṅk + ṣaṣṭhaḥ --\> praṅkh ṣaṣṭhaḥor prāṅkṣaṣṭhaḥ\|

**(84G) 86 – ḍaḥ si dhuṭ**\| 8.3.29

**87 – naś ca**\| 8.3.30 \| **naḥ ca si dhuṭ vā (saṁhitāyām)** \| *dhuṭ*is to be the optional *āgama* of the *s*, when the *s* comes after the *n* ending in an inflected word \|san + saḥ --\> san + dhuṭ + saḥ --\> san + dhsaḥ --\> (*khari ca*) san + tsaḥ --\> santsaḥor sansaḥ\|

**88 – śi t\[uk\]**\| 8.3.31 \| **śi naḥ padasya vā t\[uk\] (saṁhitāyām)** \| Let there be the *t*\[*uk*\] as an optional *āgama* of the *n* ending in an inflected word, if the *ś* follows immediately \| san + śambhuḥ --\> sant\[uk\] + śambhuḥ (62: *s-toḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) sanc + śambhuḥ (62: *s-toḥ ścunā ścuḥ*) --\> sañc + śambhuḥ (76: *śaścho 'ṭi*) sañc + chambhuḥ (73: *jharo jhari savarṇe*) --\> sañchambhuḥ

in the absence of the optional rule 73: *jharo jhari savarṇe*, the conjunction remains as sañcchambhuḥ\| in the absence of the optional rule 76: *śaścho 'ṭi*, the conjunction remains as sañcśambhuḥ\| in the absence of the optional rule 88: *śi t*\[*uk*\], the conjunction remains as sañśambhuḥ\|

**89 – ṅamo hrasvād aci ṅamuṇ nityam**\| 8.3.32 \| **ṅamaḥ hrasvāt aci ṅamuṭ nityam (saṁhitāyām) \|** The *ṅut, ṇut* or *nut* are to be the invariable augment of the subsequent vowel, if a letter of *ṅam* preceded by a short vowel is ending in a *pada*\| pratyaṅ + ātmā --\> pratyaṅ + ṅātmā --\> pratyaṅṅātmā \|

**90 – samaḥ suṭi**\| 8.3.5 \| **samaḥ suṭi ru (saṁhitāyām)** \| In place of the preposition *sam* the *r*\[*u*\] is to be the substitute, if the *s*\[*uṭ*\] follows \| sam + skartā (21: *alo 'ntasya*) --\> sar + skartā\|

**91 – atrānunāsikaḥ pūrvasya tu vā**\| 8.3.2 \| In this context when the nasal *m* or *n* is converted into *r*\[*u*\], the optional augment of the nasal form (*candra-bindu*) is inflicted with the preceding letter to the *r*\[*u*\] \|sar + skartā, in place of the preceding *a*, the nasal form is to be optional āgama as sA\*r.+ sk$$$taAR\|

**92 – anunāsikāt paro 'nusvāraḥ**\| 8.3.4 In stead of the *candrabindu*, the *anusvāra* is to be the final augment with the preceding letter of the *r*\[*u*\]\|

**93 – khar avasānayor visarjanīyaḥ** \| 8.3.15 \| **khar avasānayor padasya raḥ visarjanīyaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** Let *visarjanīya* replace the *pada*-final *repha* when the *khar* follow or when there is a cessation (*avasana*) \|saṁr + skarta --\> saṁḥ + skarta\|

**XV – sam-puṅkānāṁ so vaktavyaḥ** \| It should be stated that the final *visarga* of *sam, pum* and *kān* is to be replaced by *s \|*

**94 – pumaḥ khayy ampare** \| 8.3.6 \| **pumaḥ ru khayi ampare (saṁhitāyām)** \| Let the *ru* replace the final phoneme of *pum* if it is followed by the *khay* followed by *am*\| pum + kokila --\> pur + kokila \|

**95 – naś chavy apraśān**\| 8.3.7 \| *ru*replaces pada-final *n* before *chav* followed by *am* but not in case of *praśān*\| cakrin + trāyasva --\> cakrir + trāyasva --\> cakriṁr + trāyasva --\> cakriṁḥ + trāyasva --\>…

**96 – visarjanīyasya saḥ**\| 8.3.34 \| **khari visarjanīyasya saḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \| before *khar* *visarga* is replaced by *s* \| …cakriṁḥ + trāyasva --\> cakriṁstrāyasva \|

**97 – nṝn pe**\| 8.3.10 \| **nṝn naḥ ru pe ubhayathā (saṁhitāyām)** \| The final *n* of *nṝn* is optionally replaced by *ru* before *p* \|nṝn + pāhi --\> nṝr + pāhi or nṝn pāhi \| nṝr + pāhi --\> nṝṁr + pāhi --\> nṝṁḥ + pāhi …

**98 – ku-pvoḥ** ***x*** **ka** ***x*** **pau ca**\| 8.3.37 \| **ku-pvoḥ visarjanīyasya** ***x*** **ka** ***x*** **pau ca (saṁhitāyām)** \| The *visarga* is replaced by *jihvāmūlīya* or *upadhmānīya* if it is respectively followed by the *ka-varga* or *pa-varga*. The use of *ca* in the sūtra means that the *visarga* is optionally retained as it is \|

**99 – tasya param āmreḍitam**\| 8.1.2 \| **tasya dviruktasya (dve) param āmreḍitam** \| Of that which is iterated, let the second element be designated as *āmreḍita* \|

**100 – kānāmreḍite**\| 8.3.12 \| **kān āmreḍite naḥ ru (saṁhitāyām)** \| If *āmreḍita* follows, then *n* of *kān* is replaced by *ru* \| kān kān --\> kār kān… \|

**101 – che ca**\| 6.1.73 \| **hrasvasya t\[uk\] che ca (saṁhitāyām)** \| The *āgama t*\[*uk*\] is inserted at the end of *hrasva ac* before *ch*\| śiva + chāyā --\> śivat + chāyā --\> (67) śivad + chāyā --\> (62) śivaj + chāyā --\> (74) śivacchāyā \|

**102 – padāntād vā**\| 6.1.76 \| **dīrghāt padāntāt che tuk vā (saṁhitāyām)** \| *āgama t*\[*uk*\] is optionally inserted after *dīrgha ac* occurring at end of a *pada* before *ch* \|

--- **iti hal-sandhiḥ** ---

**atha visarga-sandhi-prakaraṇam**

**103G – visarjanīyasya saḥ**\| 8.3.34 \| **khari visarjanīyasya saḥ (saṁhitāyām) \|** let *visarga* followed by *khar* be replaced by *s* \|

**Pā. 8.3.35 – śar-pare visarjanīyaḥ**\| A *visarjanīya* is replaced with *visarjanīya* when *khar,* itself followed by *śar* follows in close proximity \|

**103 – vā śari**\| 8.3.36 \| **śari visarjanīyasya visarjanīyaḥ vā (saṁhitāyām)** \| *visarga*remains unchanged optionally, if *śar* follows\| hariḥ śete or hariś śete \|

**104 – sa-sajuṣo ruḥ**\| 8.2.66 \| **sasajuṣoḥ ruḥ padasya** \| let the pada-final *s* and the word *sajuṣ* be replaced by *ru*\|śivas + acaryaḥ --\> śivar acaryaḥ \|

**105 – ato ror aplutād aplute**\| 6.1.113 \| **aplutāt ataḥ roḥ ut aplute ati (saṁhitāyaṁ)** \| Let the substitute *hrasva u* replace *ru* when it is preceded and followed by a non-prolated *hrasva a*\|śivas + acaryaḥ --\> śivar + acaryaḥ --\> śiva + u + acaryaḥ (27: *ād guṇaḥ*) śivo + acaryaḥ (43: *eṅaḥ padāntad ati*) śivo ‘caryaḥ\|

**106 – haśi ca**\| 6.1.114 \| **aplutāt ataḥ roḥ ut haśi ca (saṁhitāyām)** \| In place of the *ru* that comes after the *hrasva a*, the substitution is to be the *u*, if *haś* follows \|śivas + vandyaḥ --\> (105: *sa sajuṣo ruḥ*) śivar + vandyaḥ --\> (*haśi ca*) śiva + u + vandyaḥ --\> (27) śivo vandyaḥ \|

**107 – bho-bhago-agho-a-pūrvasya yo 'śi**\| 8.3.17 \| **bho-bhago-agho-a-pūrvasya roḥ yaḥ aśi (saṁhitāyām) \|** The substitute *y* replaces *ru* when preceded by *bho, bhago, agho, a* (*ā*) and followed by *aś* \| devās + iha --\> (*sa sajuṣo ruḥ*) devâr + iha --\> (107) devāy + iha --\> (30: *lopaḥ śākalyasya*) devā iha \|

**108 – hali sarveṣām**\| 8.3.22 \| **bho-bhago-agho-a-pūrvasya yasya lopaḥ hali sarveṣām (saṁhītāyam) \|** The phoneme *y* preceded by *bho, bhago, agho, a* (*ā*) and followed by a *hal* should be replaced by *lopa* (according to all grammarians) \| bhos + devāḥ --\> bhoru + devāḥ --\> bhor + devāḥ --\> bhoy + devāḥ --\> bho devāḥ \|

**109 – ro ‘supi**\| 8.2.69 \| **ahan raḥ asupi** \| Let the substitute *repha* replace the word *ahan* but not when followed by *sup*-affixes \| aharahaḥ\|

**110 – ro ri**\| 8.3.14 \| **raḥ ri lopaḥ** \| Let the *repha* followed by (another) *repha* be replaced by *lopa* \|

**111 – ḍh-ra-lope pūrvasya dīrgho ‘ṇaḥ**\| 6.3.111 \| **ḍh-ra-lope pūrvasya aṇaḥ dīrghaḥ** \| When the *ḍh* and *r* are the cause of deletion, *aṇ*, if stands prior to the *ḍh* or *r*, is substituted by a long\| haris + ramyaḥ --\> harir + ramyaḥ --\> hari + ramyaḥ --\> harī ramyaḥ \|

**112 – vipratiṣedhe paraṁ kāryam**\| 1.4.2 \| When there is a conflict caused by two rules of equal strength, let the one which is subsequent in order (in the *Aṣṭādhyāyī*) apply \|

**113 – etat-tadoḥ su-lopo ‘kor anañ-samase hali**\| 6.1.132 \| **etat-tadoḥ su-lopaḥ akoḥ anañ-samāse hali (saṁhitāyām) \|** Of the pronouns *etad* and *tad,* excepting the augment of *k* at the end, the singular nominative case affix *su* is elided, if a *hal* follows. But not in a compound with the negative particle *nañ \|* eṣa viṣṇuḥ, sa śambhuḥ \| asaś śivaḥ \|

**114 – so ‘ci lope cet pāda-pūraṇam**\| 6.1.134 \| **saḥ su-lopaḥ aci lope cet pāda-pūraṇam (saṁhitāyām)** \| Let the affix *su* of *sas* (*saḥ*) be replaced by *lopa* (even) when followed by *ac*, provided that by the *lopa*-replacement only the quarter-verse becomes complete \|

**-- iti visarga-sandhiḥ ---**

**sub-anta-prakaraṇam**

**aj-anta-puṁ-liṅgāḥ**

**115 – arthavad adhātur apratyayaḥ prātipadikam** \| 1.2.45 \| Let any meaningful form of a word which is neither a verbal root, nor an affix, nor that which ends with an affix get the designation *prātipadika* \|

**116 – kṛt-taddhita-samāsāś ca** \| 1.2.46 \| **kṛt-taddhita-samasâḥ ca prātipadikâni** \| Let (the derived forms of a word) ending in *kṛt* (primary affixes) and in *taddhita* (secondary affixes) and the compounds also be assigned the same (*prātipadika*) designation \|

**117 – sv au\[j\]as am au\[ṭ\]\[ch\]aṣ \[ṭ\]ā bhyām bhis \[ṅ\]e bhyām bhyas\[ṅ\]as\[i\]bhyām bhyas \[ṅ\]as os ām \[ṅ\]y os su\[p\]**\| 4.1.2

**118 – ṅy āp prātipadikāt** \| 4.1.1 \| After what ends with the feminine terminations ṅī or āp or after a crude form \|

**119 – pratyayaḥ** \| 3.1.1 \| items which are introduced after (*dhātu*) or a nominal stem (*prātipadika*) in this section are to be designated by this technical term (*pratyaya*) \|

**120 – paraś ca**\| 3.1.2 \| the affix is to follow that form of word to which it has been prescribed\| In view of these governing rules, (the present sūtra means): Let the affixes *su*, etc. be introduced after a form of word, which ends in either feminine affix *ṅī* or *āp* or after a nominal stem (*prātipadika*) \|

**121 – supaḥ** \| 1.4.103 \| **supaḥ trīṇi trīṇi ekaśaḥ eka-vacana-dvi-vacana-bahu-vacanāni \|** Let the triplets of the affixes denoted by the abbreviatory term *sup* taken one by one get the designations *eka-vacana, dvi-vacana* and *bahu-vacana* respectively \|

**122 – dvy-ekayor dvi-vacanaika-vacane**\| 1.4.22 \| Let a dual or singular affix be used to respectively denote duality or singularity \|

**123 – virāmo 'vasānam** \| 1.4.110 \| Let absence or termination of speech be designated as *avasāna* or cessation \|

**124 – sarūpāṇām ekaśeṣa eka-vibhaktau**\| 1.2.64 \| Only one of the available similar forms of a nominal base remains before the same nominal ending \|

**125 – prathamayoḥ pūrva-savarṇaḥ** \| 6.1.102 \| **akaḥ prathamayoḥ aci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrva-savarṇah dīrghaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \|If *ak* is followed by *ac* belonging to the 1stand 2ndtriplets of nominal endings, both (the preceding and the following vowels) should be replaced by a single long vowel homogeneous with the preceding one \|agnī (1.2, 2.2, 8.2), vayū (1.2, 2.2, 8.2) \|

**126 – nād ici** \| 6.1.104 \| **āt ici pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrva-savarṇaḥ dīrghaḥ na saṁhitāyām** \| If *a* is followed by *ic*, then *dīrgha* *ac* replacement homogeneous with that which precedes does not come in place of the preceding and the following vowels \|

**127 – bahuṣu bahu-vacanam** \| 1.4.21 \| Let a plural affix be used to denote plurality\|

**128 – cu-ṭū** \| 1.3.7 \| **pratyayasya ādī cu-ṭū itau \|** The palatal and cerebral stops occurring at the beginning of the affixes are designated as *it* \|

**129 – vibhaktiś ca**\| 1.4.104 \| **sup tiṅ vibhaktiḥ ca** \| The elements denoted by the abbreviatory terms *sup* and *tiṅ* are to be designated as *vibhakti*\|

**130 – na vibhaktau tusmāḥ**\| 1.3.4 \| **na vibhaktau tusmāḥ itaḥ** \| The dental stops, the phonemes *s* and *m* occurring in the *vibhakti*-affixes are not designated as it\|

(273) – **ato guṇe** (6.1.97) – If a *guṇa* (*a, e, o*) vowel follows a non-pada-final short *a*, then a single substitute in the form of the subsequent comes in place of both the preceding and the following vowels\|

**131 – ekavacanaṁ sambuddhiḥ** \| 2.3.49 \| **sambodhane prathamāyāḥ eka-vacanaṁ sambuddhiḥ \|** In the sense of vocative, let the singular affix of the first triplet be termed *sambuddhi*\|

**132 – yasmāt pratyaya-vidhis tadādi pratyaye 'ṅgam**\| 1.4.13 \| **yasmāt pratyaya-vidhiḥ tadādi pratyaye aṅgam** \| Let the word-form beginning with that after which an affix has been introduced be designated as *aṅga* when the affix follows\|

**133 – eṅ-hrasvāt sambuddheḥ**\| 6.1.69 \| **eṅ-hrasvāt sambuddheḥ hal lopaḥ** \| A consonantal phoneme of the vocative singular introduced after a nominal stem (*aṅga*) ending in a vowel denoted by *eṅ* or ending in *hrasva ac* is replaced by *lopa*\|rāma + su --\> rāma + s --\> (133) rāma!\|

**134 – ami pūrvaḥ** \| 6.1.107\| **akaḥ ami aci pūrva-parayoḥ ekaḥ pūrvaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \| When *ac* of *am* follows, the single replacement homogeneous with the preceding vowel (*ak*) comes in place of both the preceding and the following vowels \|

**135 – la-śa-kv ataddhite** \| 1.3.8 \| **pratyayasya ādi la-śa-ku it ataddhite (upadeśe)** \|Let the *la-kāra*, *śa-kāra* and *ka-varga* occurring initially in the affixes except the *taddhita* ones be designated as *it* \|

**136 – tasmāc chaso naḥ puṁsi** \| 6.1.103 \| **tasmāt śasaḥ naḥ puṁsi (saṁhitāyām)** \| The phoneme *s* of *śas* which occurs after a long vowel replacement homogeneous with that which precedes is replaced by *n* in the masculine \|

**137 – aṭ-ku-pv-āṅ-num-vyavāye 'pi** \| 8.4.2 \| **aṭ-ku-pv-āṅ-num-vyavāye api samāna-pade raṣābhyām naḥ ṇaḥ(samhitāyām)** \| Even when separated by the intervention of the elements *aṭ*, velar stops, labial stops, (the particle) *āṅ* and (the infixed augment) *num* singly or combined in any possible way, let the cerebral *ṇa-kāra* replace the dental *na-kāra* following *repha* and *ṣa-kāra* in the same *pada* \|

**138 – padāntasya** \| 8.4.37 \| **aṭ-ku-pv-aṅ-num-vyavaye 'pi ra-ṣābhyam samāna-pade padāntasya naḥ naḥ na** \| Let the substitute cerebral nasal stop *ṇ* not replace a *pada*-final dental nasal stop *n*\|

**139 – ṭā-ṅasi-ṅasām ināt-syāḥ** \| 7.1.12 \| **ataḥ aṅgāt tā-ṇasi-ṅasām ināt-syāḥ** \| Let the substitute elements *ina*, *āt* and *sya* respectively replace \[*ṭ*\]*ā*, \[*ṅ*\]*asi* and \[*ṅ*\]*as* introduced after a nominal stem ending in a short vowel *a* \|rāmeṇa\|

**140 – supi ca** \| 7.3.102 \| **supi ca ahgasya atah dirghah yani** \|Let a substitute long vowel replace the *aṅga*-final short vowel *a* of a nominal stem before *sup*-affixes beginning with *yañ* \|rāmābhyām\|

**141 – ato bhisa ais** \| 7.1.9 \| **ataḥ aṅgāt bhisa ais** \|Let the substitute *ais* replace the *sup*-affix *bhis* introduced after a pre-affixal base ending in the short vowel *a*. By the rule *anekāl śit sarvasya* (45), (the substitute *ais* replaces the whole of *bhis* and wehave the form) *rāmaiḥ*\| rāmaiḥ\|

**142 – ṅer yaḥ**\| 7.1.13 \| **ataḥ aṅgāt ṅeḥ yaḥ** \| Let the substitute *ya* replace the *sup*-affix *ṅe* introduced after a pre-affixal base (*aṅga*) ending in a *hrasva* vowel *a* \|

**143 – sthānivad ādeśo 'nalvidhau** \| 1.1.56 \| **sthānivat ādeśaḥ analvidhau** \| A substitute (*ādeśa*) is treated like a substituendum (*sthānin*) except with regard to an operation dependent on the phoneme (*al*) of the substituendum. By this, the substitute *ya* is treated as the substituendum *ṅe* which is a *sup*-affix. Consequently, the sūtra *supi ca* applies and with a long-vowel replacement we have *rāmāya*\|

**144 – bahu-vacane jhalyet** \| 7.3.103 \| **ataḥ aṅgasya bahu-vacane jhali supi et** \| Let the substitute phoneme *e* replace the *aṅga*-final vowel of a nominal stem ending in a short vowel *a* when followed by a plural *sup*-affix beginning with *jhal*. Why (is it said) when a *sup*-affix follows? (The counter example is) *pacadhvam* \|

**145 – vāvasāne** \| 8.4.56 \| **avasāne jhalāṁ caro vā (saṁhitāyām)** \| *car*optionally comes in place of *jhal*, when *jhal* occurs in *avasāna*\|

**146 – osi ca** \| 7.3.104 \| **osi ca ataḥ aṅgasya et** \|Let the *aṅga*-final *hrasva ac* be replaced by the *e*, when the *sup*-affix *os* follows \| rāma + os --\> (146) rāme + os --\> (22) rāmay + os … rāmayoḥ \|

**147 – hrasva-nadyāpo n\[uṭ\]** \| 7.1.54 \| **hrasva-nadyāpaḥ aṅgāt āmaḥ nut** \|Let the *āgama n*\[*uṭ*\] be introduced to *sup*-affix *ām* when it occurs after an *aṅga* which ends in *hrasva ac* or which is denoted by *nadī* or which ends in the feminine affix *āp* (*āb-anta*) \| rāma + ām -\> rāma + n\[uṭ\] + ām -\> rāma + nām … \|

**148 – nāmi** \| 6.4.3 **nāmi aṅgasya dīrghaḥ** \|Let the pre-affixal base ending in *ac* be replaced by *dīrgha ac* before *nām* \|rām**ā**ṇām \|

**149 – ādeśa-pratyayayoḥ**\| 8.3.59 \| **num-visarjanīya-śar-vyavāye 'pi iṅ-koḥ ādeśa-pratyayayoḥ apadāntasya saḥ mūrdhanyaḥ (saṁhitāyām)** \| Let the substitute *ṣ* replace a non-*pada*-final *s* of a replacement element or of an affix if it (*s*) follows the elements denoted by *iṅ-pratyāhāra* and *ku*. The *īṣad-vivṛta* *ṣ* resembles most the dental *s* and is therefore the proper replacement \| rāma + su -\> rāme + su -\> rāmeṣu \|
